The worker now says whether it read the whole message, and only then may the fact be written (a backend test: a long mail with its question past the cut stays in Needs you; a cut reading in both stores' conformance).

**Round 2** (Codex, read-only, on the fixes): three findings, all fixed with tests that fail without them.

1. **[P1] Bare imperatives still read as only informing** ("FYI, fill out the form", "Para que sepas, completa el formulario"): the rest must state something about something ("the caterer is confirmed").
2. **[P1] The 14-day check turned an overdue request into "nothing asked"**: `checkDates` drops a request already over in a scan, which left no rows. `checkDates` now reports `asked` (marked before the scan's age filter and the items' bound), and the worker's verdict (`nothingAsked`) needs none.
3. **[P2] A question in a subject past the 200 characters sent** could still give "nothing asked": the subject must be sent whole too. (A backend test reads both through the reader: an overdue request by the 14-day check and a question in a long subject keep their conversations in Needs you.)

Two rounds, the cap.

**Tests run:** typecheck (store, db, idb, backend, ai), `oxlint --deny-warnings` (apps, packages, scripts/ai-real); unit: `backend/dated.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `ai/dated.test.ts` and its snapshot, all of `packages/ai`, `dated-checks.test.ts`, both stores' full conformance (`store.conformance.test.ts`, `idb.conformance.test.ts`), every `packages/db` and `packages/idb` unit file (the IndexedDB upgrade images gained the new store), `migrations.test.ts`, `migrations-shipped.test.ts`, the new `nothing-asked-upgrade.test.ts`, `needs-you-bodies.test.ts`; perf: `migrations.perf.test.ts`, `today.perf.test.ts`; the eval as above. Not run: `pnpm test:all`, e2e (no UI changed), `pnpm ui:rate`.

#### How iteration 2's rows were built

On `t3/what-matters-204`, from `a3536d768`, Codex's design taken as written (its risks are the numbered points below where a choice was made).

**A row per item (risks 2, 3, 10).**

- **Identity** (`packages/core/src/today-items.ts`): a row's id is `dated:<id>`, `promise:<id>`, `waiting:<thread>`, `reminder:<thread>` or `digest`; true duplicates share their canonical's. Never a day, a section, a revision or a position. `TodayItem.id` and `TodayHistoryItem.id` carry it; a history line also names its closed item (`item`).
- **One projection** (`packages/core/src/today-projection.ts`): `placeItems` (canonical items from the open dated items and promises, a related detail folded under its item while that item shows, each placed by `datedGroupAt` / `promiseGroupAt`), then `projectRows` (what's waiting and your reminders as rows, the order, the counts' buckets). `TodayService.project` (backend) reads the facts once each, batched: the cached open dated items, `followups.active()`, `today.waitingPage` (the stores' `needsYou` walk with `stillWaiting` as it is), and which placed conversations may show, from `today.liveAmong` for the count and from the threads' summaries the list reads anyway. The list, the sidebar's count and the morning notice all project; `countWith` and its thread arithmetic are gone from both stores. Where items sit changes only at `nextTodayChange`'s moments and midnight, so the placing is reused until then while the items are the same (the count runs on every mailbox change).
- **The substitutions are gone:** `owedAsk`, `owed`, `ahead`, `dedupe`, `withAlso`. An ask and your promise in one conversation are two rows; tomorrow's appointment doesn't hide a reply needed today. What stays, and why it's the same thing: a conversation waiting on you has no waiting row when an item of it *shown now* (not a Plan's kind, not behind Coming up) says what's asked (their ask, request or bill, or your promise to them); your reminder on a conversation is a row only when the conversation has no other.
- **`Also`** is a related detail only (`DatedRow.detailOf`): a trip's return leg under its outbound flight while that flight shows, its own row once it's over.
- **Earlier** is a line per closed item, even while its conversation has another item open; a merged item is one line at its newest closing, and none while any member is open (membership read in a batch a page, `today.members`); a held row has none. The per-conversation `seen`/`above` logic and `newestClosed` (both stores, and v59's two per-thread indexes, dropped in v61) went with it.
- **The renderer** (`state/today.ts`): the feed's rows (`FeedRow`, by id) are Today's state; the list keeps their conversations once each (what acts on conversations, pages the history and keeps a place). `useFeedRow` holds the open row and the one a conversation was put away from; the app's `selectedId` stays the open conversation and the open row follows it when another opens by other means. Opening a row opens its conversation, or, a sibling, only moves the reader's line; a second click puts it away. j/k walk rows (`feedMove`), Esc returns to the row, D/b/⇧D target the focused row, else the open one, else the one put away. Held rows are the open one and the checked conversations' rows, by id, kept in place (words only) and sent as `held` so they're never history lines; a sibling opening while a read is on its way asks again. Done/Remove/Later take out the row (never its sibling), the open one moving on; Undo restores the open row only when nothing was opened since (`moves`). The reader's line marks the open row's item (`useFeedTarget`: a merged row's every member, beyond the three it shows; a history line's closed item, through `dated.ofThread({ include })`).

**Duplicates and related details (risks 1, 5, 8).**

- **Matching** (`packages/backend/src/today-links.ts`), conservative: one event read twice (both timed at the very same moment, from two messages, the same words or the same place named, never after "with"/"con": "Dinner at Nopa" and "Dinner with Jess at Nopa"); one errand read twice in one conversation (a bill or request and your promise by the same amount, "$12 for the bus" and "send the slip and $12", or a request and your promise by `sameObject`); a request asked again in one conversation by the same sender (`sameThing`); one promise read as two (the same message, the same day, one sentence, an object in common: Margaret's "scan both papers" and "email both papers"). A shared person, day or noun alone never merges. A trip's legs (two events of one message, both travel, the later a detail).
- **Durable membership:** `same_as` (the canonical's key) and `detail_of`, written by `TodayLinks.reconcile` before any projection reads the items (only what's stored since its last look, bucketed by moment, conversation and message), through `today.link`, which writes only open rows, never over a membership already set, and never a chain. The newer joins the older's canonical, so the canonical keeps its id as evidence arrives. Membership moves no revision (it's no choice of the user's).
- **Acts:** `TodayService.act` resolves every item a row is (`today.members`) and applies Done, Remove or Later to all at once in one transaction across both tables (`today.act`), all or none; Undo (`today.restore`) puts every one back or none. `dated.act` and, for a promise, `followups.act` go through it, so the conversation's line and the promise card can't leave a duplicate behind either. *Decision:* the item named is checked at the revision it was seen at; its other members as they are now (they're the same thing), so Done on a dinner finishes the friend's note that arrived since. Later's window (`laterBefore`) is checked on every dated member.

**A broken promise, a promise's time (risks 4, 9).** `followups.broken_due_at` keeps the first day a promise had when its person's mail gave it a new one after that day was over (from that mail's date, at the move, in both stores' `finish`; a second move keeps the first). Its row stays under its new day with "Mon · promised Tue" in Missed's weight, never in Missed's bucket. Promises moved before this can't say it (the old day was overwritten). `followups.timed`: a dated promise whose words state a clock ("at 4:00 PM", "a las 16:30"; a zone only where its name reads one way: PT, MT, ET, UTC) has that moment, converted to the reader's zone (`promiseMoment`, follow-ups' checks and the dated reader's move alike); never a clock made from the day's default.

**Early visibility (risk 7).** Decided once, when the item is read (`checkDates`, one line there): a `respond` from a person to the reader (`askAllowed`) is `early` and shows from its arrival, in Today's people ("by Nov 30") until its day; a delivery whose words need someone there (a signature, an adult, an ID: `needsPresence`) shows from the day before, under its own day. `showsFrom`, `datedGroupAt` and `nextTodayChange` read the same flag; a move keeps it. Items stored before aren't early (no backfill: what made them a person's isn't stored).

**One time per row (risk 9).** The when column says the reader's time and, only when the mail's zone isn't theirs, its own in brackets ("Fri 4:00 PM (5:00 PM ET)"); the sentence never says a time: the dated reader's zone note (`ZONE_NOTE`, now core's, which `@terno/ai` reads too, written zone names like "Eastern" included) and a promise's stated clock come out of the words only when the column says the time.

**The header (risk 6).** "6 today · 8 this week" (es "6 hoy · 8 esta semana"), with Missed first when there's any ("2 missed · 6 today"; es "se te pasaron"), a part that's nothing unsaid. The exact intervals: *Missed*, the counted rows under Missed; *Today*, the counted rows under Today (due or happening today, what has no date, a person's request shown early); *this week*, the counted rows under a day ahead whose day is through the week's end, Monday to Sunday, local (`weekEndDay`; on a Sunday nothing). Counted is `todayCounted` as before: never a Plan's kind, nor a reminder, nor Coming up. The sidebar's badge and the morning notice: Missed + Today (`todayBadge`).

**Migration v61, shape only** (v60 is the waiting rule's `dated_nothing_asked`, rebased onto): `dated_items.early` (default 0), `same_as`, `detail_of`; `followups.same_as`, `broken_due_at`, `timed` (default 0); partial indexes on `same_as`; v59's two per-thread closing indexes dropped. 5 ms at 57,000 conversations (gate 1 s); v24 → v61, 141 ms (before the rebase; after it, below). IndexedDB: no version bump (its indexes are in memory; rows read without the fields read their defaults).

**Perf (the Today gate, 50k threads, both stores):** SQLite spread: list p95 17.9–18.7 ms (gate 20), the count's added 0.8–1.2 ms (gate 2); all showing: list 14.6–15.3 ms, added 1.0–2.2 ms (gate 4); Needs you at its bound: added 0.4–4.1 ms (gate 5). IndexedDB: spread list 7.9–9.8 ms, added 0.1–0.3 ms; all showing 7.8–8.0 / −0.0–0.8; at the bound 8.5–9.7 / 0.7–0.9. The first build, before the placing was reused and the count's words left unmade, was 2.1–2.6 ms added on SQLite's spread (over the gate): the count read an `Intl` formatter for the zone and placed 1,000 items each time.

**Tests.** Core: the projection (two items of one conversation, an ask and a promise, waiting rows represented or not, Spam, reminders, duplicates and their canonical, the trip's lifecycle, early, broken, one time, the counts and the week), `splitZoneNote`, `statedClock`. Backend: the matcher (`today-links.test.ts`), the history per item (`today-history.test.ts`), `promiseMoment` and follow-ups' timed promise, and through the RPC an errand merged (one row, Done on both, Undo both, a stale revision changing neither, the promise card acting on both), the same dinner from two conversations, a broken promise (its first day kept across two moves), early deliveries, one time, a history line's closed item; the earlier tests rewritten for rows. Conformance in both stores: the new reads and writes, all-or-none acts and restores, the v61 migration. Web: rows by id (two of one conversation, held by id, the open row following the app), the history's pages and the held rows through the list, Done/Remove/Later and Undo on rows (a sibling stays, Undo's open row only when nothing opened since), the keys' targeting, the header in en and es, the when column. E2E: two items of one conversation as two rows (the reader's line marking each, j/k, a second click, D, z), a merged dinner's Done and Undo, the header and the badge.

**Known limits.** Two promises from one sentence with no object in common (Diego's "enviar los ajustes" and "calibrar el verde") stay two: the follow-ups prompt's job, not a merge rule. A promise card marks its item for the feed (`data-target`) but draws nothing more than its card. Held rows keep their words, not their act.

**After the rebase and the review's round 1** (the machine at load 1–5): v61 5 ms at 57,000 conversations, v24 → v61 134 ms. The Today gate, SQLite: spread list 17.9–18.2 ms, the count's added 0.8 ms, the longest turn 53.5–55.0 ms (base `f7be635d2`: 43.3; the matcher's first look at all 1,000 items, once a start, is in it); all showing list 16.5–16.8, added 1.5–3.4; Needs you at its bound list 39.8–41.6, added 0.8–3.2, the longest turn 57.5, 58.7 and 65.9 ms in three runs (base 56.6, and the waiting rule's own runs saw 60.3 and 90.9: its gate of 60 is at the edge for both; the list now places afresh rather than also finding the next moment, which took it from 61–67). IndexedDB: spread 9.3 / 0.1 / 18.2 ms, all showing 8.3 / 0.4 / 11.3, at the bound 11.6 / 2.0 / 17.1 (list, added, longest turn).

**The bound's longest turn, taken apart** (timed inside `project`, then removed): a list at Needs you's bound is 37–40 ms, of which the waiting walk (`waitingPage`, plan 145's and the waiting rule's) is 33–35; placing the 1,000 items 1 ms, reading their conversations 3, the rows 0.4, the matcher's look 0.1. The longest turn is that list and the mailbox's `threads.counts` after it in one turn (SQLite answers synchronously): 56–61 ms on a quiet machine (one run of six at 78 in a load spike), against the base's 56.6 and the gate's 60. What this iteration adds there is the ~4 ms of rows, trimmed of a copy of every row; yielding a turn between the walk and the rows split it (the longest 36 ms) but made the list itself 11 ms slower to answer (18 → 29 ms p95 spread), so it isn't done: making the walk itself sliced is plan 145's, not this plan's.

**With the waiting rule (rebased onto `f7be635d2`).** The waiting rule lives in `stillWaiting` and reaches the projection through `waitingPage` as it is. Together: a conversation whose latest mail the reader read with nothing asked (a flight confirmation, a concert's tickets) has no waiting row at all, so "a Plan doesn't say what was asked" matters only for mail the reader hasn't read (plan 145's rule); the backend tests that had a Plan's conversation waiting now expect no waiting row there, and the core projection's tests keep the rule itself.

##### What the code review changed (iteration 2's rows)

**Round 1** (Codex, read-only, `review1.out`): twelve findings, all fixed, each with a test that fails without it.

1. **An amount alone merged two errands** ("$12 for the bus", "$12 for the museum"): the amount and what it's for, a word in common (three letters or more), or a request's object (`today-links.test.ts`).
2. **A recurring request merged across periods** ("the November payroll report", "the December one"): months, quarters and dates named apart keep two requests two (`sameRequest`, `sameErrand`).
3. **A dismissed right-click menu redirected later acts:** the menu's row is the menu's (`useTodayTask(…, "menu")`), cleared when it closes; the palette's is the open row (`today.test.tsx`).
4. **Membership was read outside the act's transaction:** an act reads a row's members and writes them alone with the matcher (`TodayLinks.exclusive`), so none joins between (`today-links.test.ts`).
5. **A promise's move converted its zone twice:** the move's own time (already the reader's) on its day, or, with none, its clock as it was; never its words' clock read again on a converted day (`dated.test.ts`, `checkDates`).
6. **The canonical's conversation in Trash hid a live duplicate:** a live member shows the same row (`today-projection.test.ts`).
7. **A newest closing in Spam hid a merged history line:** the line is the newest closing that may show (`today-history.test.ts`).
8. **Links found in one pass made chains the store refused, never retried:** the pass counts its own links (`findLinks`'s `joined`).
9. **A thread merge never re-matched:** the matcher remembers each item's conversation, and an item moved is looked at again (`today-links.test.ts`).
10. **Done read the next conversation:** the next row is opened by the app (`setAppOpened`), unread until chosen; Undo the same (`today-task.test.ts`).
11. **A merged promise's card left its row:** the card takes out every row the promise is, its own or a merged one, with its Undo (`today-task.test.ts`).
12. **Unsupported zones became local clocks:** zones in any case; one that doesn't read one way (CST, IST) leaves the promise untimed; a moved promise's old clock isn't said (`today-projection.test.ts`).

### Round 11 and iteration 3's design

Round 11 (iteration 2, medians of two reviewers; `scores.py` now reads Diego's Codex labels): Look 7.9, Acting 7.4, Plans 7.1, Needs you 7.0, Deadlines 6.9, Content 6.7, Missed 6.7, would use daily 6.4, Coming up 6.0. Cells at 8+: 12 of 63 (round 10: 11); under 6: 5 (17). The waiting rule is the round's win (Needs you +1.9, traps shown 13 → 4); the row per item gave Plans +0.7. Every claim the fourteen reviews make was checked against round 11's databases (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/panel/my-analysis-r11.md`) and by a Fable 5.2 analysis (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/panel/fable-analysis-r11.md`); they agree. What holds the scores down is mostly deterministic:

- **Paid is shown unpaid.** Diego's card (Missed 5/4): the reading took the minimum ($1,105.00) and the bank's receipt in the same conversation ("Recibimos tu pago por $8,432.17") was refused by `otherIds`, which splits "$8,432.17" into "432" and "17" and reads them as identifiers that differ from the item's "105".
- **Near things hidden, far things nowhere.** Kinds show on different leads (an event 3 days, a delivery on its day), so Theo's keynote, Jordan's QBR, Margaret's Tuesday and tomorrow's deliveries are behind Coming up while later days show; Coming up ends at 14 days, so stored trips and renewals (Jordan's and Priya's flights, Diego's domain, Theo's Appvault, Sam's registration) show nowhere; a renewal read as `renew` (7 days) rather than `expire` (30) flips between rounds.
- **A holding reply loses the deadline.** María's "I'll send it Wednesday" settled the school's request due Friday, so only her missed Wednesday shows; Margaret's "I'll get back to you" lost Dolores's Monday. A broken and re-dated promise under its new day as "Mon · promised Tue" reads as next Tuesday (Jordan, Priya, both reviewers).
- **Today holds what's due later**, in arrival order and without its time (Jordan's $1.18M RFP's 5:00 PM ET under a Nov 29 call), and "6 today · 8 this week" matches nothing on screen (13 of 14 reviews).
- **The reader's top line is another item** of the conversation, above a second card with its own Done (11 of 14).
- **Rows say a fact, not the thing to do** ("BLS certification expires", "domain expires"); the app's 120-character cut leaves broken sentences; the second line names the conversation's latest sender, not the item's (Margaret's premium "from Gary").
- **Reading misses** in two or more rounds: Theo's investor asking for a call, the dentist's "please call to choose a new time", "reply C to confirm", David's "I can swing by Saturday… let me know", a party's event beside its RSVP, a paid conference, Spendly's "books close on the 20th", "this weekend" in a promise; and two wrong asks (a courtesy "avísanos cuando vengas", a Cc "para que estés al tanto").

The owner's answers (2026-11-15): the feed shows **the next seven days whole** and Coming up has **no horizon** ("7 days whole"); a broken, re-dated promise **stays under its new day**, worded "You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed Mon", and a holding reply's request **carries its deadline onto the promise's row** ("Stay under the new day"); **"Waiting on you" inside Today** ("'Waiting on you' in Today"); a promise the person **replaced in plain words closes** ("Close replaced promises"). Not taken: an AutoPay bill that needs nothing stays a row; two asks from one message stay two rows; an undated promise still shows from its wake.

#### Iteration 3

**The panel first (fairness).** `scores.py` reads every label (fixed) and says when a review has fewer than nine scores; Margaret's key no longer calls a promise due Nov 8 "not yet due", and the walkthrough's Remove only ever acts on a key trap (none on screen: Remove and then its Undo); `score-items.py` counts a settled item shown open as a wrong row and takes a promise's row carrying the chase as the reply owed; the walkthrough adds the palette and the state after Undo; the harness writes each reading's raw answer (items, settlements, moves, nothing asked) beside the database, so a miss can be traced to the model or the checks.

**1. Paid is paid.** Amounts are one token in `idsOf` (a currency amount is never an identifier); a receipt (`SETTLES.pay`) from the item's own sender in the item's own conversation settles that conversation's one open `pay`, whatever amount it names; the prompt reads a card statement's `pay` as the amount that avoids interest (the minimum in its words at most).

**2. The next seven days whole, Coming up without a horizon.** An open item whose day is within the next seven days shows under that day (plans and deliveries quiet, as now), whatever its kind's lead; the leads still bring something further out forward (a bill a week ahead, an expiry 30 days); everything else not yet shown is Coming up's, at any distance, its days past the second week grouped by week. `COMING_UP_DAYS` goes; the open set's bound and `retire` keep it finite. A renewal that lapses unless the reader acts ("auto-renew is off", "renew before", a domain or membership that expires) takes `expire`'s lead whichever kind it was read as. `needsPresence` reads the mail's words, not the item's. `dayBefore` never applies to an expiry, a renewal or "end of the month"; a card's "11/26" is the 26th (the earlier day; v11's end-of-month rule withdrawn) with the mail's words in the row.

**3. Promises that keep their deadline.** A reply of yours that promises what a dated request asked (the follow-ups reader's promise in the message that settled it as answered) links the two (`followups.answers`, the request's id): the promise's row says the request's day beside its own. A broken promise (`broken_due_at`, or its own day past while a linked request's is ahead) stays under its live day (the new day, or the request's), its column "You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed Mon" (es "Dijiste el mar 10 (se pasó) · lo necesitan el lun"); with no live day ahead it's Missed as now. A promise to answer ("I'll get back to you", "let me check and tell you") never settles the request: the request stays the row, with its day. A request that replaces a promise in plain words (`saysReplaced`: "skip the piecemeal version… send me the full draft") closes the promise as replaced (Earlier: "Replaced {day}"), the new request its row.

**4. Today means today; "Waiting on you" inside it.** Today holds what's due or happening today and what was missed; people's asks, conversations waiting on you and requests shown early sit in a quiet "Waiting on you" part of Today, ordered by their own day (soonest first, undated last, by arrival). A request whose day is today is a deadline, not Waiting. An early row's column keeps its time and the mail's zone when it differs ("by Fri 5:00 PM (6:00 PM ET)"). The header is "1 missed · 3 today · 5 waiting" (es "1 se te pasó · 3 hoy · 5 te esperan"), each part unsaid at zero; "this week" goes. The sidebar badge and the morning notice: Missed + Today, as now.

**5. The reader shows the row you clicked.** One line on top, one design for a dated item and a promise: the open row's item, its Done/Later/Remove; the conversation's other items as one quiet line under it ("Also in this conversation: …"), each becoming the top line when chosen. The summary's "To do" drops what repeats the item. A promise card never names its person twice ("You told Jess you'd send the script").

**6. Rows that say what to do, whole.** The app never shows a sentence cut mid-clause (the prompt asks for at most about fourteen words and one bracket; a cut ends at a clause with an ellipsis and never gains ", the email says"). A failed payment is a `pay` worded as the fix ("Update the card for Stratus before Mon ($1,186.40)"). Expiries, trials and renewals say the decision ("Book a BLS renewal class (certification expires Dec 10)", "Cancel Notably Pro before Sun or pay $14.99/month", "Renew pingwire.dev (auto-renew is off)"). No file names, order or policy numbers in the sentence; a question worded as one ("Answer Elena about bringing tamales and arroz"). The row's second line names the item's own sender.

**7. Prompt v13 and follow-ups v11, eval cases first** (fictionalized, en and es, five readings on both models, none wrong shown): an investor asking for a call is an `ask`; "appointment cancelled… please call to choose a new time" settles the event and asks to call; "reply C to confirm" is the event and a `respond` to confirm now; a chase that also offers ("I can swing by Saturday… let me know") moves the promise and asks; a party invitation with an RSVP is the RSVP and the event; a paid registration for a dated conference is the event; "October books close on the 20th" in a November mail is the 20th of November (a local backstop: a bare day of the month read into the past rolls forward); a card statement's balance; a conditional courtesy ("cuando vengas, avísanos") and a Cc "for your information" ask nothing (a local check beside `onlyTells`); follow-ups: "this weekend", "tonight", "next week" are dated (their last day), "I'll be there for the tech check" beside the event is no second promise (the matcher: a promise to attend and the event of one conversation and day are one row, the event), two steps of one delivery in one sentence are one promise, "your" not "my".

**8. Small things on the screen.** "Remove from What matters" in the menu, apart from Delete (a separator), its hint "The email stays"; one treatment for "Today" in the column (no blue beside grey); "N coming up" as the catalog's disclosure, staying open once opened, its rows in the row ink; Settings → AI names What matters and its provider ("What matters: OpenAI"), never "digest" or "Claude or OpenAI"; a merged row keeps its members' details (Priya's "car pickup 7:05").

**Tests.** Unit and backend for every rule above (both readings of Diego's card; the seven-day split on a Friday with Tuesday's keynote and tomorrow's delivery; Coming up at 30 and 60 days; a renewal read either kind; a holding reply on a request due later; a broken promise with a live day; a promise replaced; Waiting on you's order and the header in en and es); both stores' conformance for any new read or field; e2e for the header, Waiting on you, Coming up's disclosure, the reader's top line on a two-item conversation, Remove's label; the Today perf gate (Coming up without a horizon reads the open set as now: no new walk); the dated eval (all cases, both models, both languages) with the new cases.

**Perf.** No new walk: the seven-day split and Coming up place the open items the projection already holds (bounded at 1,000); the link from a promise to its request is one column read with the promise. A migration only if `followups.answers` needs one (shape only, both stores).

**Out of scope (iteration 4):** mute and the preferences box (plan 206), correcting an item's date, handing an item to someone, "1 message looked like a scam", the place's own time for a trip, "checked since", a second-day capture.

#### Revisions after the Codex review of iteration 3

Codex (read-only, `/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/critique-it3.out`) raised 12 points, all taken. The first two name the refactor point 3 needs: who may settle a request on your reply moves from "a trusted send exists" to the follow-ups reader's checked verdict. It's the only clean way to build the owner's "carry the deadline onto the promise", and it stays inside the feature (no other caller settles requests), so it's built here, said here.

1. **A reply's verdict settles a request, not the send.** Both stores' `settleAnswered` / `datedAnswered` / `finishDated` close earlier `ask`/`respond` rows on any trusted send; the dated reader never reads sent mail. The follow-ups reader gives each reply a checked verdict on the requests it was shown (fulfilled; a concrete promise answering the request; a holding reply; unknown). Only *fulfilled* settles; *a promise* links and keeps the request's deadline; *holding* and *unknown* (or follow-ups off, a failed reading) leave the request open as it is. Whichever reader finishes first, the verdict applies when both are in (a confirmed local send too). Tests: both orders, restart, follow-ups off, a failed reading.
2. **The link is durable and reaches closed requests.** A shape-only migration (v62): the promise's `answers` (the request's id) with the reply message's identity; the follow-ups worker is given the conversation's labelled open and answered requests; either reader's finish reconciles. The projection reads the linked requests in one batch, and `pruneDated` keeps a request while a promise names it. No backfill (an old send isn't an answer).
3. **One effective promise day.** A core resolver gives a promise's own moment, its first broken moment, a linked request's deadline and the day it sits under: the person's new day when they gave one; else a linked request's day still ahead when the promise's own is past; else Missed. `promisePlace`, `promiseOver`, `promiseGroupAt`, `nextTodayChange` and `promiseWhy` all use it; Later and an undated promise's wake are unchanged. `answers` is not `sameAs`: Done on the promise's row finishes the promise and settles the request together (Undo restores both as they were); Remove and Later act on the promise alone.
4. **Replacement is its own result, checked hard.** The dated reader returns `replaced` (a promise label and the exact evidence), never through `settled`. It needs the promise's person, a later mail in the same conversation, words that name the old deliverable apart and say it's replaced ("skip the piecemeal version"), and a new request in the same mail that passes the checks; never on negation, quoted instructions or words to the app. Both stores' `finishDated` recheck person, conversation, revision and open state, and close the promise and write the request together. History says "Replaced" (`outcomeOf`, `ClosedPromise`).
5. **Paid is paid, narrowly.** Currency amounts are read whole from the original text before `namingWords` splits it, so "$8,432.17" is an amount, never "432" and "17"; real invoice and order numbers stay identifiers. The amount-free settlement applies only to the same sender's receipt in the same conversation with exactly one open `pay` there (counted in the store, not among the prompt's labels), and never on a negated receipt ("we have not received your payment"). Tests: different amounts, two bills, an unrelated invoice number, a negation, another conversation.
6. **The seven days are one predicate, shared with the clock.** The window is today and the seven local days after it; it applies after expiry and an active Later; a longer lead still brings something further forward; everything else eligible is Coming up. `nextTodayChange` uses the same boundary (midnight brings the eighth day in); the old horizon wakeups go. Tests: day seven and eight, DST, Later before and after the item's day.
7. **The bounds keep what they say.** Both readers' 1,000-row bounds are kept; what's refused or evicted is said in the plan's "Known limits", and a promise past its effective day retires (as dated items do) so its capacity frees. Conformance at 1,001 rows with a linked request.
8. **Waiting is a Today tier carried through the projection.** Today's tiers: deadlines and what happens today, then Waiting (dated requests by their day, soonest first, then undated by arrival), then the quiet rows. `TodayCounts` is `{ missed, today, waiting }`, disjoint; the badge and the notice stay `missed + today`. `todayCounted`, `countsOf`, `todayBadge`, the header and both projection paths change together; a row's id doesn't change when its tier does.
9. **Coming up's weeks and an attendance merge are data, not drawing.** Each Coming up row carries a display bucket (its day for the first two weeks, then its week, with the year when it differs) apart from its own day; the feed draws buckets in order. The matcher merges a promise to attend with the event it names (by its words and, when several events share the day, its time), the event leading the row.
10. **One reader model.** The reader resolves the opened row's item (its members, a closed target, the conversation's other items) once, and draws one primary line with selectable secondary lines; follow-up nudges stay apart; it works for an item not in the visible feed. Rows carry their item's source sender. The summary's repeated To do is filtered in this view only.
11. **Facts, not guesses.** `needsPresence` reads the quote's original paragraph; "lapses unless renewed" is a checked, stored fact on the item (a shape-only column; older rows default to off, no backfill); `dayBefore` as specified; the bare-day roll-forward only in a deadline clause; a clause-aware cut replaces `cutAtWord` for a new and a moved text alike.
12. **Measured.** The panel's log wraps the transport (as built: the completion before validation, allowlisted fields) and the harness also writes each message's checked outcome. The Today perf gate adds the combined bounds, a cold matcher, linked requests and Coming up open, the list then `threads.counts` in one turn, against the 60 ms ceiling; relationship reads are batched.

#### How iteration 3's reader line was built

Design point 5 with Codex revision 10, and point 8's reader, menu and settings parts. On `t3/what-matters-204`, from `26bf5f4cf`.

**One reader model** (`apps/web/src/lib/reader-items.ts`, `readerItems`, pure). Its input is what the reader reads once per conversation: the dated items (`dated.ofThread`, a history line's closed one through `include`), the conversation's promises (follow-ups' own store, a closed one read by `followups.get`), the feed's open row's items (`useFeedTarget`: a merged row's every member, lead first; a history line's closed item) and an item chosen in the reader. It resolves them to one top item and the others once each: the open row's members are one line (their lead's words), true duplicates (`sameAs`) are one line; the top is the chosen item while the open row is the same, else the open row's item, else the soonest (Missed first), so a conversation opened from the Inbox or behind Coming up has a top line too. Closed items show only as what a history line names. Nudges (waiting on a reply) aren't items. `useReaderItems` (`components/thread-items.tsx`) holds the reads: an answer is applied only if it's the latest asked for this conversation and item (a sequence per effect, the conversation tagged on the answer), nothing is drawn until this conversation's reads have answered (no line that swaps on arrival), and the last answer is kept through a re-read. `Thread` in `reader.tsx` calls it once and hands it to the line and to the summary.

**One line, one design** (`ThreadItems`, replacing `thread-dated.tsx`; `data-testid` kept: `thread-dated`, `-item`, `-text`, `-done`, `-later*`, `-dismiss`). On the AI card's surface: the top item's words with its day ("Due Saturday · Send Jess your draft", "Due today · You told Jess you’d send the script"), Done (outline), Later and Remove (quiet), for a dated item and a promise alike; Later's window is the item's (a dated item's `itemLaterWindow`, a promise's anywhere ahead) and its own moment uses the dated line's picker. A closed top says what happened ("Done Tuesday", `historyWhen`) and has no buttons. Under it, one quiet group, "Also in this conversation" (es "También en esta conversación"), each other item a link-secondary button in the muted ink (the ink on hover): beside the label from a 600 px reader, under it below that. Choosing one opens its row in the feed when the feed has one (`feedRowsOfItem`, shared with `today-task`'s acts), so the feed, the keys (D, b, ⇧D) and the reader stay on one item; elsewhere it leads in the reader alone. The keys go to the new top's Done (the button chosen has gone). The promise card (`ThreadFollowups`) now draws waits only; its tip stays.

**A promise names its person once** (`followupLine`, `withoutPerson` in `lib/followups.ts`). The person (full or first name) leaves the words when the sentence stands without them: after to/with/for/a/con/para, or before what's given ("send Jess the script" → "send the script"; "enviar a Valeria Ochoa el guion" → "enviar el guion"); where it can't ("email Jess", "review Jess's deck", "enviar a Valeria Ochoa"), the line is the no-name form and the words keep the name ("You said you’d email Jess"). Everywhere a promise is said; the reader's line says the person by first name (`short`).

**The summary's To do** (`repeatsItem`, `ThreadSummary`'s `said`). A To do item is left out of this view when every meaning word of one of the line's items (grammar words in both languages dropped, accents and case folded, word forms by a shared start of four letters; a name past the first word needn't be there, when two words or more remain without it) is in it. The saved summary and Copy keep every item. Questions ("Jess asks") aren't filtered.

**The menu** (`thread-menu.tsx`). What matters' rows are Done, Later ▸, then "Remove from What matters" (es "Quitar de Lo importante") with ⇧D, a note under it at its label's edge, "The email stays" (es "El correo se queda"; the item's `aria-describedby`), then the separator before Reply; Delete stays in the mail's group further down. The menu is 304 px rather than 256 while it holds that row (`has-data-[wide]:w-76`), so the label and its key fit in both languages; every other menu keeps its width (and `selection.spec`'s baselines).

**Settings → AI** (copy only). The group is "What matters" (es "Lo importante"), its hint opens with the feature and its provider ("What matters: OpenAI. It reads new mail to find bills, renewals, trips, other deadlines and what people ask of you."), its paused note names it, and the keys' routing line names it first: "What matters, summaries, follow-ups and the newsletter digest run on OpenAI; drafting, Ask your mail and rules on Claude (Anthropic)." The provider choice still says "Claude or OpenAI": it's the keys' choice, not a feature's.

**Shared code touched:** `useFeedTarget`'s module gains `feedRowsOfItem` (`state/today.ts`), which `today-task.ts`'s `rowsOfItem` now uses; `followupLine` gains `short` and the dedupe (lists too); `ThreadSummary` takes `said`; the thread menu's width rule.

**Tests.** Unit: the model (each row's item on top, the soonest with no row open, a dated item and a promise in one order, a nudge never an item, chosen and its fallback, a merged row and a duplicate as one line, a history line's closed item, an item not read yet), the words of both kinds, `withoutPerson` and `followupLine` in en and es, `repeatsItem`; the hook (nothing before the read, an older read answering last dropped, a conversation left before its answer, a choice with no feed row); What matters' settings line. E2E (`today.spec.ts`): the two-item conversation opened from each row shows that row's item on top with one Done and the other under it, choosing it swaps and opens its row with the keys on Done, Done and z from the top line, axe on the line in light and dark; a promise row's line in the one design; the menu's label, its note, its place and the separator; Settings' line. `followups.spec.ts`: a promise's line is the reader's line (its Done and Undo); the card's design sweep runs on a wait.

**Screenshots** (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it3-d-shots/`): the reader beside the feed on a conversation with two dated items and a promise, en and es, light and dark, 1440 and 1100, and with the other item chosen; the menu in en and es, light and dark; Settings → AI.

**Known limits.** A promise's line no longer shows its source ("Your message, Nov 6 →") or who found it, as the dated line never did; the wait's card keeps both. The To do filter is lexical: a To do worded with other verbs than the item stays. A history line's closed promise is read when it's opened (`followups.get`), so its line comes a read later than the conversation.

##### What the code review changed (iteration 3's reader line)

Codex's review of the reader line (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it3.out`, points 2 and 6; its other points are the reading's, placement's and promises' parts):

1. **The keys follow a choice made in the reader** (P1). An item chosen from "Also in this conversation" that has no feed row (behind Coming up, held) led the reader alone, but D, ⇧D and b still acted on the feed's open row, so D finished an item the reader no longer showed. The reader now shares its choice while it leads (`useReaderChoice` in `state/today.ts`, set by `useReaderItems`, cleared when the conversation is left or the choice no longer holds). `keyTarget` in `today-task.ts` resolves it before the open row unless the keyboard is on another row of the feed: D and ⇧D act on the reader's item (`actOnReaderItem`, which the line's own buttons use too), and b opens the reader's own Later menu (`openReaderLater`; it holds the app's keys while it's open, as the feed's Later does). The palette's Done, Later and Remove follow the same choice. The right-click menu is unchanged: it is opened on a feed row and acts on that row. Tests: `today-task.test.ts` (D, ⇧D and b on the chosen item, the open row untouched; another conversation open, the row's item again) and `thread-items.test.tsx` (the choice is shared only while it leads, and cleared when the conversation is left).
2. **A history line's closed item waits for its own read** (P2). The dated read's answer was tagged with its conversation only, so opening a closed item's history line beside an open sibling kept the sibling's answer "ready", and the sibling's Done briefly led until the new read (`include`) answered. Answers now carry the conversation and the item asked for. The reader is ready only for that pair, and the last answer is kept only through a re-read of the same pair. Test: `thread-items.test.tsx` (the history line opened beside an open sibling draws nothing until its own read answers, then the closed item leads).


#### How iteration 3's placement was built

On a branch from `26bf5f4cf` (design points 2's placement, 4 and 8's feed parts; revisions 6, 8, 9, 10's row sender, 11's stored facts and 12's perf). Agent A's promise day, C's dated reader and D's reader UI are apart; where this touches a function they also touch, it says so below.

**Seven days whole (revision 6).** One predicate, `shownFrom(day, leadDays, stored)` in `packages/core/src/dated.ts`, used by a dated item (`showsFrom`) and a promise (`promiseShowsAt`) alike: an open item shows from the midnight that brings its day within today and the seven local days after it (`SHOWN_DAYS`), or from 9:00 its lead before when that lead is longer (an expiry's 30 days, a lapsing item's), and never later than a moment stored from an earlier lead. One date is made either way (the list places the 1,000-item bound on every read). Expiry and an active Later apply after it, as before; a delivery that needs someone there no longer needs its own rule (its day before is inside the seven). `COMING_UP_DAYS` is gone: what's eligible and not shown is Coming up's at any distance (the open set's bound and `retire` keep it finite). `nextTodayChange` lost its horizon wakeups; the window's opening is the item's `showsAt`, a midnight, which the clock already wakes for. Agent A feeds an effective promise day into `promisePlace` / `promiseShowsAt`; the change here is the window inside `promiseShowsAt` only.

**Facts read once (revision 11).** `checkDates` reads the quote's paragraph (`contextOf(quote, paragraphsOf(text))`, at most 2,000 characters) and the quote, never the item's words, for `needsPresence(words)` and for `lapsesUnlessRenewed(words)`: auto-renew off or "won't renew" (`RENEWAL_OFF`), or "renew … before", "renew … by", "to keep … renew", "unless you renew", a domain, membership, registration, certificate, licence or passport that expires, "para no perder", "renueva antes" (`RENEW_TO_KEEP`), unless the words say it renews or is paid by itself ("automatically", "AutoPay is on", "se renovará automáticamente"). Only a `renew`, `expire`, `pay` or `respond` (`canLapse`) carries it. Stored as `dated_items.lapses` (**migration v63**, shape only: a constant default, no backfill; v62 is agent A's), IndexedDB with no version bump (a row without it reads off), `DatedWrite.lapses`; `showsFrom` takes an expiry's lead for it whatever its kind. The two lines in `checkDates` are the only change there (agent C edits the rest of it).

**Waiting on you (revision 8).** `waitsOnYou(why)` (`dayTier` 2: a person's ask, a conversation waiting on your answer, a request shown before its day) marks a Today row's `part: "waiting"` in the projection, list and count paths alike (the count's stub carries `early`). `ordered()` puts Today's own first (deadlines, your promises, then its quiet rows: a plan happening today, your reminder, as `sortInDay` has them), then Waiting on you: the requests with a day by it, soonest first, then the undated (asks and waiting conversations) as they came, newest first. *Choice:* the quiet rows stay above the sub-heading rather than after Waiting on you, since a sub-heading holds only its own rows. A request due today is no longer early, so it's a deadline. `TodayCounts` is `{ missed, today, waiting }`, disjoint; `todayCounted` no longer counts a day ahead; "this week" and `weekEndDay` are gone. The backend's list gives Waiting on you a section of its own (`TodaySection.part`); the feed draws it as a quiet `h3` (muted ink, no hairline, the headings' and rows' text line), Today's heading above it even when Today has nothing else (`drawn()`), the group `waiting` ranked after Today. Header: "1 missed · 3 today · 5 waiting" (es "1 se te pasó · 3 hoy · 5 te esperan", one "te espera"), each part unsaid at zero. *Decision to confirm:* the badge and the morning notice stay "Missed + Today" in the sense they had, Today's whole group, so `todayBadge` is `missed + today + waiting`: the sidebar keeps its number and reads as the sum of the header (read literally as `missed + today`, people waiting on you would leave the badge and the morning notice). An early row's column keeps its time and the mail's zone when it differs ("by Fri 5:00 PM (6:00 PM ET)", "by tomorrow 12:00 PM"); the row is quiet.

**Coming up by day, then by week (revision 9).** `displayBucket(day, today)`: the day itself through the Sunday that ends the week of today's fourteenth day after (no week split), then `week:<its Monday>`. Every row ahead (a day of its own and Coming up) carries `bucket` apart from its `day`; sections carry it; the feed groups by it (`week:YYYY-MM-DD` beside `day:`), a week's rows by their own days then the day's order, ids and the cursor unchanged. *Choice:* a row the main feed shows past the two weeks (an expiry's long lead) is under its week too, so headings never interleave; the column still says its day. The heading is "Week of Dec 7", the year when it isn't this one's ("Week of Jan 4, 2027"; es "Semana del 7 dic"). "N coming up" is the catalog's disclosure (`Collapsible` + `CollapsibleTrigger`, its chevron turning, the same words open or shut), `aria-controls` the feed's rows (they join their days there: no panel of its own; axe found the trigger's default pointing at none), open until closed (the existing guarded localStorage key). Coming up's sentences are in the row ink; its column stays quiet.

**Attending is the event (revision 9).** `attendsEvent(promise, events)` in `today-links.ts`: a dated promise whose words say attending (attend, be there, join, come, make it, show up, go to; asistir, ir al, estar ahí, llegar) and an event of the same conversation on its day, named by the promise's own words once the attending words are taken out (`namingWords`); several named, the promise's own time picks one; none or still several, nothing. The promise joins the event's canonical whichever came first (the event leads the row; a promise row already open re-keys to the event's id once). "I'll be there for the tech check" and that evening's keynote stay apart.

**The row's own words and sender (design point 8, revision 10's rows).** A merged row's other dated members add what their brackets say that its words don't (`withMembersDetails`: Priya's restaurant reservation keeps "(car pickup 7:05)" from Jess's note; a zone note is never one). A dated row names its own item's sender: the conversation's latest when that address is the item's (most rows: no read), else its message's, one batched read for those (`today.fromOf`, both stores, conformance), so Margaret's premium is Lakeshore Mutual's, never Gary's. "Today" in the when column is the ink and weight, no accent.

**Tests.** Core: day seven and eight, DST both ways, Later before and after the item's day, the Friday with tomorrow's delivery and Tuesday's keynote, an expiry's long lead, no horizon (Coming up at five weeks), the clock's next moment; `lapsesUnlessRenewed` en/es with self-renewing negatives, the lead it takes; the counts disjoint and the count path agreeing; Waiting on you's order and a row's id across tiers; `displayBucket` across a week and a year; a merged row's details and its source. Backend: `checkDates` reading presence and a lapse from the paragraph (the item's own "signature" doesn't count, a plan never lapses); the matcher (tech check vs keynote, the event leading either order, the promise's time picking, other days and conversations, Spanish); through the RPC the seven days and the buckets, a row's sender with Gary writing last, the tech check merged and the keynote apart; the earlier tests rewritten for the counts, the part and the window. Stores: `lapses` round-trips and defaults off, `fromOf`, v63 (and v57's, v61's expectations gained the column). Web: the header in en and es, the disclosure (a button with its chevron, open and remembered), the feed's groups (Waiting on you, weeks joining a day ahead), the early row's time, the week heading. E2E (`what-matters-feed.spec.ts`, new; `today.spec.ts`, updated): the header and the badge's sum, Waiting on you's order and times, the sub-heading on the rows' line, Coming up's disclosure by keyboard, its rows' ink, a day then two weeks' headings, axe light and dark.

**Perf (the Today gate, SQLite, 50k, load 1.8 to 2.3, three interleaved rounds against `26bf5f4cf` with the budget loosened to print every scenario).** Spread list p95 18.6 / 18.8 / 20.6 ms (base 19.1 / 18.3 / 19.5); all showing 16.4 / 16.4 / 17.6 (base 17.0 / 16.3 / 16.3); Needs you at its bound list 43.9 / 43.2 / 45.2 (base 44.5 / 44.9 / 46.5), its longest turn 60.6 / 58.3 / 59.5 (base 62.2 / 64.8 / 61.2); the new "Coming up far, Needs you at its bound" (1,000 items two weeks to a year out, every one Coming up's, under ~50 week buckets, with Needs you full) list 45.2 / 48.0 / 43.5, its longest turn 57.4 / 76.7 / 64.8. The count's added time 0.3 to 2.4 ms. IndexedDB (the final code, one run, load 4.4 to 6): spread 10.1 / 0.5 / 19.6 (list, added, longest turn), all showing 8.2 / 1.2 / 12.0, at the bound 12.5 / 1.9 / 16.6, Coming up far 14.5 / −0.5 / 17.0; every gate passes. `migrations.perf.test.ts` passes with v63. No new walk: the window and the buckets place the open items the projection already holds; the sender read happens only for a row whose conversation's latest sender isn't its own. The first build read every row's sender (IndexedDB spread list 25.7 ms) and spread three more fields a row: both changed before the numbers above. On this shared machine neither side holds the bound's 60 ms longest turn reliably (the base failed it in all three rounds); the far scenario shares that gate.

**Screenshots** (light and dark, `/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it3-b-shots/`): `e2e-top-*.png` (the header, Missed, Today, Waiting on you), `e2e-coming-up-*.png` (Coming up open: a day ahead, its own day, "Week of Nov 30", "Week of Jan 4, 2027").

**Known limits.** A promise row that a later event merges into changes its id once (to the event's). A row past the two weeks that the main feed shows sits under its week. The badge's reading (above) is a choice to confirm. "para el mañana" in Spanish for a request due tomorrow (the early column's day word, as before). Not run: `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review (the series' owner runs it).



#### How iteration 3's promises were built

Design point 3 ("promises that keep their deadline") with the Codex review's revisions 1, 2, 3 and 7, and follow-ups prompt v11, from `26bf5f4cf`, one commit each.

**Who settles a request (revision 1).** A send no longer settles anything by itself: both stores' `settleAnswered` and `datedAnswered` are gone (ingest, the flag/location reconciliation and the confirmed send called them), and `finishDated` stores a request read after your reply open, like any other, at the bound like any other. The only authority over a request is a reply's checked verdict, given by the follow-ups reader:

- **What it's shown.** With What matters on, the follow-ups worker gives a reply the conversation's open requests (`ask`, `respond`) from before it, newest first, at most five (`<requests>`, `q1…`, each with its day), as material like everything but your words. A request already linked to a promise is open, so it's shown too.
- **What it answers.** Prompt v11's `answers`: for each request, `fulfilled` (done now: the slip sent, the decision given), `promise` (a concrete promise that answers it, also in `promises`, its quote the evidence), `holding` ("let me check my calendar and get back to you") or `unknown`.
- **The checks** (`checkSentMail`): a verdict names a label it was shown; its evidence is your words, two or more, never words to the app (`plainEvidence`, the dated reader's own); a `promise` links only a promise kept above whose quote holds the evidence (or the evidence holds it), and never one that only holds the request (`onlyHolds`: "get back to you", "let you know", "te aviso", "lo reviso", unless it also sends, pays or signs something); a `fulfilled` whose words are a promise's is that promise. Everything else leaves the request as it is.
- **The store** (`followups.finish`'s `verdicts`, in its one commit): `fulfilled` settles the request (`answered`, at the revision it was shown); `promise` links this reply's promise to it (`followups.answers`, once); a kept promise (`kept`, "here it is") settles the request it answers. Only an open request of the reply's conversation from before it, and only from a reply that's yours for certain: Gmail's SENT label (`TRUSTED_SENT`, now follow-ups') or Terno's own confirmed send (its mark's `confirmed`, set by `followupSendConfirmed`). An IMAP reply from another client decides nothing, as before.
- **Either order.** Dated reader first is the normal case. Follow-ups first: a reply waits while the dated reader, on and running (not paused), still has a message of its conversation from before it to read (`dated.marks.pendingOn`), held as a reply waiting for a body is; the dated reader wakes follow-ups when a pass ends (`caughtUp`). A request read after a reply that followed it (the reader off then and its 14-day check now, or behind): the dated reader's finish marks the first three such replies again (`DatedFinish.replies`, follow-ups on: yours for certain by Gmail's label, sent since follow-ups went on, not marked now), in its own commit, so the follow-ups reader gives its verdict on the request. Restart-safe throughout: the hold is read from the durable marks, and each finish is one commit. A re-read is idempotent on its rows (`message_id`, `item`).
- **Off, or failed.** Follow-ups off, or a reading that fails (an invalid answer, three timeouts): the request stays open, the row, with its day.

**The link (revision 2).** Migration v62, shape only: `followups.answers` (the request's id), `ai_sent_mail.confirmed` (default 0), a partial index `followups_answers`. 2 ms at 57,000 conversations; v24 to v62 137 ms. IndexedDB: record defaults and an in-memory `answers` index, no version bump. The reply's identity is the promise row's own (`message_id`, `item`): the verdict links by it, so no column of its own. No backfill (an old send isn't an answer). `pruneDated` keeps a closed request an open promise answers. What matters reads the requests promises answer in one batch (`TodayService.needs`: those among the open dated items as they are, the rest by id through `today.members`; nothing when no promise answers one), and `withNeeds` (core) gives each promise its open, dated request's moment (`needed`). A request an open promise answers is that promise's row, not its own, nor is the request asked again that's one item with it (`withoutAnswered`); the matcher never merges a promise its reply linked with a request by words (`sameErrand`, one line).

**One promise day (revision 3).** `promiseDay` (core) gives a promise's own moment, the day it sits under, the day it was given and missed (`said`) and the day it's needed by (`needed`): the person's new day when they gave one (`brokenDueAt`: the day they moved it from is said); else, when its own day is over (or it named none) and the request it answers is still ahead, the request's (its own said as missed); else its own, Missed once over, from the later of the two. `promisePlace`, `promiseOver`, `promiseGroupAt` and `nextTodayChange` read it through one line each at the top (`effective`: the facts with that day), so their bodies are as they were; `nextTodayChange` also wakes at a linked promise's own day's end. `promiseWhy` says `said` and `needed` (`TodayWhy.promise`, replacing iteration 2's `promisedAt`), never in Missed. The when column (`today-sentence.ts`): "You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed Mon" (es "Dijiste el mar 10 (se pasó) · lo necesitan el lun"), "You said yesterday (missed) · needed today", a day a week or more ago by its date ("You said Nov 2 (missed)"), in Missed's weight under the live day; a promise ahead of its request's other day "Wed · needed Fri" ("… · needed tomorrow"). `pastDay` (`when.ts`) says the missed day so it can't be read as the next one. Done on the promise's row finishes the promise and settles the request it answers in the same act (`TodayService.act`, `closedReason` `ANSWERED_WITH_PROMISE`; Undo restores both, all or none); a request so closed has no history line of its own (the promise's says it). Remove and Later act on the promise alone: removed, its request is its own row again with its day.

**Retiring (revision 7).** `FollowupWorker.retire` closes a promise past its day for good (`promiseOver`, from the day it sits under, its request read in one batch) as `dismissed`/`expired` (history: "Over"), daily (`bounds`) and at Today's clock moments beside the dated reader's `daily`, so follow-ups' bound of 1,000 holds only what can still show. A promise whose request is still ahead isn't retired. Both bounds are kept as they were (the dated set keeps the soonest, follow-ups' a promise takes the place of the oldest wait and is refused with none); conformance fills follow-ups to 1,000 with one linked to a request, refuses the 1,001st, keeps the link and the request, and takes the next once one retires.

**Prompt v11 (follow-ups).** The verdict above; a soft day is its last day ("tonight" that day, "this weekend" its Sunday, "this week" its Sunday, "next week" the Sunday of the week after, with worked dates); being where they're expected isn't a promise ("will be there for the tech check Monday", "we'll be there at 7:30 with snacks", "see you Saturday"); one delivery in one sentence is one promise (things sent together, or one thing in steps: "te mando los tres ajustes y calibro el verde"); a promise's text says "your", never "my" ("send Jess your passport scan"), and names its person once. The ladder's overhead allows for the larger schema; a small window now needs a little over 2,900 tokens (v10 2,100). The fake and the UI rating's model stub answer the new field.

**The eval** (`scripts/ai-real/followups.real.ts`, 23 new cases `f11-01` to `f11-23`, fictionalized from round 11: María's slip answered with a Wednesday promise, Margaret's "get back to you", the slip sent and a decision given, a reply that doesn't answer it, an instruction to the assistant in what it answers, Sam's "this weekend" on a Thursday night, "tonight", "next week", Theo's tech check and María's snacks, Diego's two steps, Priya's passport scan; en and es), scored on what `checkSentMail` keeps, five readings each (`TERNO_AI_FOLLOWUPS_REPEAT`), the bar none wrong shown. The first readings gave "next week" as the coming Sunday (both languages, Haiku) and split "recalibrate the green and send them" and "the passport scan and the photos" in two: the words above. **Final prompt:** Haiku 112 of 115 found, none wrong shown (three "send Jess the passport scan" without "your"); Luna 115 of 115, none wrong. The base cases (`TERNO_AI_FOLLOWUPS_BASE` rounds): Haiku five rounds, no false promise or reply, one missed Spanish promise ("Legal va a preparar… y yo te la envío") and one "next Tuesday" left undated in 170 readings; Luna two rounds, all right. The ledger stood at $31.64 of $55.

**Tests.** Core: `promiseDay` (own, moved, a request's day still ahead, both over, named none, the clock at its own day's end), the projection (María's Friday carried, the request no row of its own, Missed from the later day and `promiseOver` a week after; a promise ahead saying the other day; the person's new day first; a closed request or an undated ask carrying nothing; a request its own row again once its promise closes; the matcher's one item). Web: the when column in en and es (missed and needed, yesterday and today, a date past a week, needed tomorrow). Backend: `checkSentMail`'s verdicts (done, a promise kept above, holding, unknown, a label not shown, not your words, one word, words to the app, a made-up quote, a promise that only holds, a "done" in a promise's words, `onlyHolds` en and es); through the RPC (`promise-answers.test.ts`): María's promise under Wednesday saying Friday, then under Friday saying "you said" (none Missed), Done on its row finishing both with no history line of the request's, Undo both; Remove leaving the request its own row; a holding reply leaving the request; done now settling, a failed reading, follow-ups off and a send alone settling nothing; follow-ups first (the reply held while the request's mail is read, then its verdict); a request read late by the 14-day check sending the reply back for its verdict; `retire` (one past its week, one whose request is ahead kept); the matcher's linked promise. Both stores' conformance: a send settles nothing (from sync, read late, an ask or a request), a verdict only on the request it was shown at its revision (not a later one, a bill, another conversation's), a promise linked once and a second reply's too, kept settling it, trust (an unlabelled `is_sent`, Terno's confirmed send), the re-marks (three, since then, labelled, not marked), `pendingOn`, prune keeping a linked request, the bound at 1,001. Migrations: v62 (shape, defaults, the index used), v58's and v61's expectations with the new column. The follow-ups prompt's words and snapshot, its `<requests>` block (labelled, dated, five, escaped).

**Ran:** typecheck (all packages) and oxlint on the changed files: clean. Unit and backend: `core/dated.test.ts`, `core/today-projection.test.ts`, `web lib/today-sentence.test.ts`, `today-task.test.ts`, `state/today.test.tsx`, `ai/followups.test.ts` and the whole `packages/ai`, `backend/followups.test.ts`, `promise-answers.test.ts`, `dated.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-links.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `promise-time.test.ts`, `model-stub.test.ts`, `db/migrations*.test.ts`: all pass. Both stores' whole conformance suites: 1,424 pass. The migration gate: v62 2 ms. **The Today perf gate passes** (load average 2.5 to 4): SQLite spread list p95 18.3 ms, the count's added 1.1 ms, the longest turn 54.0; all showing 16.7 / 2.1 / 29.7; Needs you at its bound 40.1 / 1.3 / 59.0 (gate 60). IndexedDB: 8.0 / 0.4 / 18.0, 7.5 / 0.7 / 9.6, 10.9 / 0.8 / 16.6. Three earlier runs, with other agents' evals and tests running (load 2 to 8), failed on SQLite's spread list (19.2–21.7 ms, gate 20) or the bound's longest turn (60.6–61.5 ms), and so did the base (`26bf5f4cf`) in the same minutes (spread list 20.1–24.5 ms). No linked promise is in the gate's mailbox, so what it measures of this change is `needs` (no read when nothing is linked) and one `effective` check a promise. No e2e (no flow changed; the when column's words are unit-tested), and no screenshots of the column's new words.

**Known limits.** A Gmail draft synced into Terno and then sent from another client stays one message that turns sent: follow-ups' live rule reads inserted mail only, so such a reply gets no verdict (the old rule settled its requests at the reconciliation); a confirmed Terno send and Gmail's own sent mail are read. A reply from another IMAP client never settles a request (as before). With follow-ups off, nothing settles a request but the user (by design). Requests settled `answered` before v62 stay settled; promises made before carry no request. Two replies' promises may both answer one request (each carries its day). `onlyHolds` reads English and Spanish only. A late request costs up to three re-readings of replies. The undated ask a promise answers is hidden with it until the promise shows (as the old settlement hid it for good).

**Shared functions touched outside point 3:** `promisePlace`, `promiseOver`, `promiseGroupAt`, `nextTodayChange` (one input line each), `placeItems`/`promiseWhy` in the projection, `sameErrand` (one line), `historyPage` (one line), `TodayService.act`/`project`/`armClock`/the clock's callback, the stores' `finishDated`/`pruneDated`/ingest and op completion (the answered calls removed), the UI rating's model stub (`answers: []`).



#### How iteration 3's reading was built

The dated reader's part of iteration 3 (design points 1, 6 and 7; Codex revisions 4, 5 and 11's `dayBefore`, roll-forward and cut), prompt v13, in nine commits on `t3/what-matters-204` from `26bf5f4cf`. Placement, the "lapses unless renewed" fact and `needsPresence` are iteration 3's other parts, not this one.

- **Paid is paid (revision 5, point 1).** `namingWords` reads a currency amount whole before it splits the words (`amountsOf`: "$8,432.17", "US$29", "MX$450", "8.432,17 €", "29 USD", a number grouped in thousands with cents), one token whatever its separators ("¤8432.17"), so it's never the identifiers "432" and "17"; an invoice's, a card's or an order's number still is one. Another amount than the item's (`otherAmounts`) settles a bill only as its own sender's receipt (`SETTLES.pay`) in its own conversation with exactly one open `pay` there, counted in the store (`dated.ofThread`, read only when the model settled something), never among the labels sent; a receipt said not to have happened ("we have not received your payment", "no hemos recibido tu pago", "aún no recibimos tu pago") settles no bill. Prompt: a card statement's `pay` is the amount that avoids interest, the minimum only in its words. Tests: Diego's card read as $1,105.00 and as $8,432.17 against "Recibimos tu pago por $8,432.17 … terminación 6631", two bills (the amount names one), an unrelated invoice number, a negation (en, es), another conversation, another sender, an uncounted caller, and the worker counting the store.
- **Replaced promises (revision 4, the owner's "Close replaced promises").** The reader returns `replaced` (a promise's label and the mail's exact words), never through `settled`. `checkDates` keeps it only on the promise's person's mail in its conversation (the only one it's offered to), on words that are the mail's own, plain (`plainEvidence`), undenied (`replacesPlainly`: never "don't skip", "let's not skip", "no olvides") and not retold in quotation marks (`insideQuotes`), whose paragraph names that promise apart from any other offered, and with a request kept from the same mail that isn't what was promised (by its text and its quote: one whose words name the old deliverable beside the new is still new). A replaced promise is never also moved, and a request is never turned into its move. Both stores' `finishDated` close it (`dismissed`, `closed_reason = 'replaced'`) in the same commit only when the request's row (`message_id`, `item`) is written, the promise still open or due at the revision the reader saw, on this thread, made to this sender before the mail came; the worker tells follow-ups (`promisesMoved`). History: `HistoryOutcome` "replaced", "Replaced {day}" (es "Reemplazado {day}"), in `outcomeOf` and the reader's closed promise. Tests: Sam's methods section (en) and Teresa's marco teórico (es), negation, quotation marks, words to the app, an unquoted evidence, two promises named alike, no request, a dropped request, a re-ask, an o label; both stores' conformance (a stale revision, no request row, another sender in the thread, mail from before the promise, then the close, history, and a second replacement changing nothing); the reader end to end.
- **Facts, not guesses (revision 11).** `dayBefore` moves only a `pay`, `respond` or `alert` (never an expiry or a renewal, whose day is the one named; "the end of the month" never matched), and reads the whole mail when its quote's paragraph says no day at all (Haiku quoted "No pudimos cobrar tu suscripción" for a bill to fix "antes del lunes 16", 5 in 5 in Spanish). A card's "11/26" is the 26th, the earlier reading, its words kept in the row (v11's end-of-month rule withdrawn in the prompt; a month alone, "11/2026", is still its last day). A deadline's bare day of the month read into the past (`bareDayAhead`, `pay` and `respond` only) is the next one when its paragraph gives that day alone in a deadline clause ("books close on the 20th", "by the 20th", "antes del 20", "vence el 20"), never one a month names, a date in figures or a time; "before the 20th" read as the 19th rolls with it. `cutAtClause` replaces `cutAtWord` for new and moved words: whole within 120 characters, else at the last whole clause that fits (a comma, a semicolon, a colon, a dash, a sentence's end, never a short word's "Dr."), or at a clause inside a bracket's first level, closed ("Partido … contra los Linces (llegar 7:30; llevar espinilleras)…"), with an ellipsis; a word only for a first clause longer than all of it. A cut alert says whose word first ("The email says: …", es "Según el correo: …", `alertSays`), never ", the email says" after an ellipsis.
- **What asks nothing, and the one ask an automated sender may make (point 7's local checks).** A courtesy that waits on the reader's visit or need (`courtesyOnly`: "Cuando vengas a Tepoz avísanos, aquí tienes tu casa", "if you're ever in Portland, let us know", "let me know if you need anything", "if something's in the way, tell me") is no ask; one with a question or anything else asked still is. Mail the reader is only copied on to know about it (`onlyCopied`: "te copio para que estés al tanto", "cc'ing you so you're in the loop", "FYI") gives them no ask or request unless its sentence speaks to them by name and isn't that line ("Diego las monta en las etiquetas esa misma semana" is nobody's task of theirs). An automated sender's mail that cancels the reader's appointment (settled in the reading, or said cancelled) may ask them to book again ("Please call … to choose a new time"), to the reader in To only; a reminder's "call us to reschedule" or a sale's "book a new appointment" never. The eval then asked for four more: a request with a day from the promise's person for what was promised (`sameObject`) is that promise's move (Haiku read David's "she needs both papers by Monday" as a request to send them, 2 in 5), never when the mail replaces it; a person's offer that waits on the reader's yes ("I can swing by Saturday… Just let me know", its "let me know" read from the paragraph) is an undated ask even when the model gave it a day (Luna, 1 in 5), and is never hidden by a request about the same papers; the sender saying what they can do with nothing to answer ("I can prep the usage story") is no ask; a sentence that only informs is no ask whatever part of it was quoted ("the caterer is confirmed", `onlyTells` on its sentence too). A renewal and an expiry of one day from one mail are one row (`itemKey`).
- **Rows that say what to do (point 6).** Prompt v13: a failed payment's `pay` is worded as its fix ("Update the card for Stratus ($1,186.40)" when the mail asks to update the card; an alert's words never are), expiries, trials and renewals as the reader's choice ("Cancel Tasklet Pro before Sunday or pay $9.99/month", "Renew bakehouse.dev (auto-renew is off)", "Book a CPR renewal class (certification expires Jan 12)"), one that renews by itself in the mail's currency; what the mail asks about an expiry is that one item, a signing request a `respond` even when its link expires; at most about fourteen words and one bracket; no file name or number that identifies an order, an invoice, a policy, an account or a service; an ask's text starts with answering or telling the person, a question never worded as its task ("Answer Rosa about bringing the salad", never "Bring the salad"); a date in figures read as the mail's language writes them ("08/12/2026" in a Spanish mail is December 8; Haiku read Dominios MX's as August 12). The app also strips what the model leaves (`withoutIdentifiers`: "invoice RS-77120", "factura EP-4471", "order #IH-55018", "policy HO-3-55120847", "(servicio 4517 2290 3381)", "(RS-77120)", "(Quillworks_…_2026.pdf)"; a card's or an account's last digits and a zone's note stay) and an alert's trailing instruction ("…payment failed; update the payment method to keep watching", Haiku 1 in 11 after v13 named a bill's fix).
- **Prompt v13's other words (point 7).** Someone who wants in on the reader's own company or work (an investor, a customer, a partner) asking for a call is an `ask`, even writing for the first time (recruiting and sales pitches stay out); a concrete offer that waits on the reader's yes is an `ask`; a cancelled appointment's "call to choose a new time" is an `ask`, even from an automated address; "reply C to confirm" is the event and a `respond` to confirm, due the day the mail arrived unless it gives one; a party's RSVP is the RSVP and the event on its day; a conference or a course the reader registered for, paid or to pay, is an `event` on its first day beside its bill; an expiry's or a renewal's day is the one the mail names.
- **The eval** (`dated.real.ts`, 195 cases, 31 new, `dated-165` to `dated-195`, fictionalized from the panel's round 11, en and es): Theo's investor (Marcus Lee as Mira Castell and Inés Barragán), the dentist's cancellation (Spanish; `dated-127`'s English now expects the ask), "reply C" (Spanish; `dated-036` and `dated-100` now expect the `respond`, on the arrival day or the next), David's chase (en, es), Ava's party (en, es), Pipeline Summit's registration (en, es), Spendly in Spanish, Diego's statement (es) and a statement balance (en), the receipt of another amount settling the statement read as its minimum, the Tepoz courtesy (es) and its English twin, Carla's Cc (es, en), Stratus's failed payment (en, es), the BLS expiry (en, es), Notably's trial (en, es), the domain with auto-renew off (en, es, the Spanish one "antes de la fecha de vencimiento" on its day), an MSA's file name, an order number, a policy number, Elena's tamales asked as a question (en, es). `dated-139`'s card is now the 26th with "11/26" in its words, `dated-140` and `dated-141` expect the promise replaced, `dated-003`'s trial says to cancel, `dated-126`'s offer is an ask. The eval scores `replaced` (`wrongReplaced`, in the bar, and `missedReplaced`), passes the conversation's open bills (`paysInThread`), reads the clause cut and the cut alert line, and notes a cut inside a clause (`midClause`).
- **Eval first.** On v12's words with `replaced` (one reading of the 37 new and changed cases, English): Luna 31 of 40 found, 1 wrong shown, 14 wrong words; Haiku 28 of 40, 2 wrong shown, 13 wrong words (the investor nothing, the cancellation's call and David's offer nothing, no `respond` for "reply C", no registration's event, the minimum on a statement, "Pay …" for a card to update, "BLS certification expires", "Notably Pro trial ends", invoice and order numbers, "Bring the tamales and arroz"; the replacements already right, the Tepoz ask and the Cc request already dropped by the checks). On v13's first words, one reading in both languages: Luna 40 of 40 both, Haiku 38 and 37 of 40.
- **What the readings changed on the way** (five readings a round of the new and changed cases, both models, both languages): Haiku kept invoice numbers (3 to 5 in 5; the stronger words and then the app's strip), quoted a bill's first sentence for its "antes del lunes" (the whole-mail fallback), split a domain into a renewal and an expiry (one a day), read "08/12/2026" in a Spanish mail as August (the figures rule); Luna wrote "Bring the tamales" (the example then named the case's own dish; it now names another and says how an ask's text starts), made David's offer a request due the Friday (the offer check), and lost a game's "arrive 7:30" to a cut before its bracket (the bracket's own clause); a request that named what it replaced was turned into the replaced promise's move (Haiku, "chapter 2 (replaces the marco teórico)": the replacement now wins and its request is judged by its quote too); the full round found Haiku's alert "update the payment method to keep watching" (2 of 2: the fix's words are a bill's, and the trailing instruction goes), and Nina's "I can prep the usage story" as an ask (the self-offer check).
- **Score, five readings each of the 47 new and changed cases, the final prompt** (235 expected items a model and language): Luna English 234 of 235 found, none wrong shown; Luna Spanish 235 of 235, 1 wrong shown (`dated-095`'s "snack this week" as a `respond`, v11's known flake); Haiku English 235 of 235, 2 wrong shown (David's "she needs both papers by Monday" as a request to Ms. Greer, `dated-169`, and a BLS "upload the new card" request beside its expiry, 1 in 5 each); Haiku Spanish 235 of 235, 1 wrong shown (`dated-170`'s papers as a request: the promise's words are Spanish and the request's text names "licenciada", so they aren't one object). No settlement, move or replacement wrong; times 15 of 15 each.
