Two checks landed after this round (the alert's instruction, the self-offer); five readings of the six cases they touch (`009`, `053`, `126`, `143`, `169`, `170`) on both models in both languages: none wrong shown, 3 of 5 of Haiku's English `dated-169` moves missed (it gives neither the move nor the request), 2 of 5 of Haiku's Spanish `dated-143` requests dropped (a quote joined across a clause, not the mail's), 1 of 5 of Luna's Spanish `dated-169` asks missed.
- **One full round, all 195 cases** (132 expected items), the final code but its last commit: Luna English 131 of 132 found and none wrong shown (`dated-126`'s offer dropped as hidden by the papers' request, fixed in the last commit); Luna Spanish 132 of 132, none wrong; Haiku English 132 of 132, none wrong, one move missed (`dated-169`); Haiku Spanish 130 of 132 with 2 wrong shown (`dated-053`'s "the caterer is confirmed" quoted without its lead, fixed in the last commit; `dated-128`'s prescription as a delivery, v12's known flake) and `dated-052`'s ask not given by the model. No settlement, move or replacement wrong; every time right (33 of 33) and every move's words (4 of 4, but one Luna reading in Spanish in the round before, its bracket since closed), no alert unsafe, attributed or with a made-up detail (one Haiku alert in each language in the round before, since stripped), no text cut inside a word or a clause. The ledger stood at $38.22 of $55.
- **Small windows** need a little over 7,200 tokens for v13 (v12 6,100): the dated test's window is 7,300. The app warns below 5,500 for the filter; a window between them reads only the mail's first 200 tokens, as before.
- **Shared code touched outside the reader:** `namingWords` (amounts whole; the matcher in `today-links.ts` reads it too), `settlesKind` (a denied receipt), `itemKey` (a renewal and an expiry one family), `DatedFinish.replaced` and both stores' `finishDated`, `HistoryOutcome` and `outcomeOf`, the reader's `ClosedPromise` outcome, `alertSays` in `today-sentence.ts` (used by `datedText`), the i18n keys `whatMatters.history.replaced` and `dated.alertSaysCut` (en, es).
- **Known limits.** A request for what was promised turns into its move only when its words are the promise's object (`sameObject`), so a Spanish request naming the recipient ("… a la licenciada Greer") stays a request beside the promise; Haiku sometimes gives neither (3 in 5 on `dated-169` in English). `dayBefore`'s whole-mail fallback moves a deadline back for a "before" its day anywhere in an asking sentence when the quote's paragraph says no day. The identifier strip takes an invoice's number out even when it's what tells two of one sender's invoices apart (the quote keeps it, so the checks still do). "Reply C" is due the day the mail came or the next (Haiku, 2 in 5, the next). A clause cut can leave a bracket's later clauses out. `bareDayAhead` reads only a deadline clause's bare day (a bare day elsewhere, "the 20th", stays past and is dropped). The courtesy, Cc and offer checks read English and Spanish words only.
- **Tests run:** typecheck (ai, backend and its lifecycle config, store, db, idb, core, i18n, web), `oxlint --deny-warnings apps packages scripts/ai-real`; unit: `ai/dated.test.ts` and its snapshot, all of `packages/ai`, `backend/dated.test.ts`, the new `dated-evidence.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-links.test.ts`, both stores' full conformance (`store.conformance.test.ts`, `idb.conformance.test.ts`), `today-sentence.test.ts`, `web/lib/dated.test.ts`, `i18n.test.ts`, `dated-checks.test.ts`; the dated eval as above. Not run: e2e (no screen changed but the history's and a cut alert's words), `pnpm test:all`, perf gates (no new read on a hot path: the bill count is one indexed thread read, only when the model settled something), the Codex code review (run on the merged series).

##### What the code review changed (iteration 3's reading)

The Codex code review of the merged series (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it3.out`) found three gaps in the reader's local checks, each fixed in `packages/backend/src/dated.ts` or `dated-evidence.ts` with a unit test that fails without it:

- **A denial before the quote (P1).** `replacesPlainly` read only the model's evidence, so "Don't skip the methods section; please send the full draft by Monday" quoted as "skip the methods section" closed the promise as replaced. It now takes the mail (subject and text) and reads the evidence where it is: each replacing word in it has no denial in the three words before it in its clause (a sentence's end, a line, a semicolon, a colon or a dash ends one, never a comma), at every place the words occur; words the mail doesn't hold replace nothing. Tests: the English case and "No olvides el marco teórico; envíame…" close nothing, the same words undenied still do (`dated.test.ts`, `dated-evidence.test.ts`).
- **A courtesy's "let me know" that asks for something (P2).** `courtesyOnly` dropped "When you're back, let me know what you think of the draft" (a visit's condition and a "let me know", nothing in `ASKS_MORE`), leaving no row and `asked: false`. A "let me know" now counts as a courtesy only when it asks for notice of the visit or need: nothing after it in its clause, the condition itself ("let me know when you're in town", "avísame cuando vengas a Tepoz"), or the sender's own "and we'll…"/"y lo…". Tests: the draft's feedback, "dime qué opinas del borrador" and "avísame qué día te queda mejor" are asks; "let me know when you're in town", "avísame cuando vengas a Tepoz" and "give me a shout and we'll sort it out" still aren't.
- **FYI over a direct request (P2).** `onlyCopied` refused any request whose sentence held the copy line, so "Diego, FYI, could you review the colors by Friday?" to Diego in Cc was lost. A sentence that speaks to the copied reader by name and asks them directly (a question, "could you", "please", "¿puedes…?", or a clause starting with what they're to do: "te copio para que estés al tanto: revisa los colores…") is theirs, the copy line or not; the line alone ("te copio para que estés al tanto; cuando Ana las entregue te las reenvío"), "FYI, the vendor ships the labels Friday" and a task said of the reader without their name still give them nothing.

Tests run: `pnpm --filter @terno/backend typecheck`, `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files, `vitest run --project unit` on `backend/dated.test.ts`, `backend/dated-evidence.test.ts` and `ai/dated.test.ts`. No prompt words changed, so no real-model eval.



##### What the code review changed (iteration 3's promises and placement)

Codex reviewed the merged series (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it3.out`). Seven of its findings are on the promises and the placement. Each fix has a test that fails without it.

- **A promise to attend is about attending, and nothing else (finding 3).** `attendsEvent` used to look for attending words anywhere in the promise's quote. So "send the slides for the tech check", quoted from "I'll attend the tech check and send its slides tonight", was merged into the event: Done on the event finished the slides, and the event's expiry hid them. Now only the promise's own words count. They must say it attends (`ATTEND`), and must not also hand something over (`DELIVERS`, en and es: send, bring, sign, pay, enviar, mandar, traer…). Test: `today-links.test.ts` (the slides quote, a promise that attends and sends, a Spanish one).
- **Done settles the whole request (finding 4).** The projection hides a promise's request together with every reading the matcher merged into it. Done used to settle only `answers`, so a second reading came back as its own row. `TodayService.act` now expands the request through its canonical membership (`today.members`) and settles every open member in the same act. Undo restores them all. Test: `promise-answers.test.ts` (the school asks twice).
- **A late verdict separates the promise from its request (finding 5).** If follow-ups read the reply first, the matcher could merge the promise with the request by their words before the verdict linked them. Setting `answers` left that merge in place, so Remove and Later then acted on both. Now, in the verdict's own commit, both stores (`apart`) undo the merge. A promise joined to the request's item leaves it. A promise that was the item's canonical keeps everything else, and the request becomes the canonical of its own readings. No revision moves, as with `link`. Conformance covers both canonicals.
- **A confirmed send stays trusted (finding 7).** Terno's confirmed send was trusted only through its sent mark, and finishing the read consumed the mark. So a request read later (with the dated reader paused) never got that reply's verdict. Migration **v64** (shape only) adds `messages.sent_confirmed`, set in the commit that confirms the send. IndexedDB treats an absent field as not confirmed, with no version bump. Both stores' `TRUSTED_SENT` now include it, so `remarkReplies` marks a confirmed reply again. A mark's `confirmed` from before v64 still counts. Tests: conformance (a confirmed reply, read, then a late request settles through it), `migrations.test.ts` and the shipped hash.
- **A verdict needs the revision it was shown at (finding 8).** Both stores' `finishFollowups` now compare the request's current revision with `v.request.revision` before either branch. A request put off with Later while the reply was being read is neither linked nor settled. Conformance.
- **Reading a reply again evicts nothing (finding 9).** At 1,000 active rows, SQLite evicted the oldest wait before discovering that `INSERT OR IGNORE` was replaying a row it already had. It now checks `(message_id, item)` first, as IndexedDB did. Conformance: a wait and 999 promises, the reply read again, the wait kept.
- **Members are read in one query (finding 12).** SQLite's `itemMembers` selected the ids and then read each row again: about 1,000 reads for 1,000 linked requests, on the list, the count and the clock alike. It now reads whole rows in one query a table. IndexedDB maps the records it already found. `needs()` uses a set instead of `includes`. Test: `packages/db/src/today-members.test.ts` (at most two reads for 1,000 items; 1,502 before).

**Perf.** The Today gate has a new scenario: linked requests at the combined bounds. Follow-ups are at their bound, 900 promises answer requests that have since closed, and the dated set and Needs you are both full. It ran in three interleaved rounds against `a7e1a5ad7` with the same scenario, budgets loosened only to print every number.

| SQLite, linked scenario | list p95 | added | longest turn |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Before (load 2–8) | 75.7–80.6 ms | 11.8–12.7 ms | 96–104 ms |
| After (load 2–3) | 55.6–70.9 ms | 9.4–10.3 ms | 92–134 ms (one spike) |

On IndexedDB, before and after are the same: list 15.0–16.6 ms, added 1.8–2.5, longest turn 21–22.

SQLite is over the 60 ms ceiling on both sides, and most of that isn't Today's work. In this mailbox, plan 145's own walk takes about 48 ms, against 35 in the scenario before it. The second Coming up seed alone, without the links, takes the list to about 60 ms and the longest turn to about 81. So the scenario has a budget of its own, explained in the gate:

- SQLite: list 85, added 12, walk 60, count 70, longest turn 120 ms.
- IndexedDB: 20 / 4 / 12 / 15, under the usual 60 ms turn.

At the real budgets, SQLite still fails on two earlier scenarios that this change doesn't reach:

- **Needs you at its bound:** longest turn 61.8 and 61.0 ms. The base measured 58.1–61.9 this session.
- **Coming up far:** 72.5 ms. The base measured 72.6–73.7.

IndexedDB passes every scenario. `migrations.perf.test.ts` passes with v64.

**Ran:** typecheck (all packages); oxlint on `packages/backend/src`, `packages/db/src`, `packages/idb/src` and the conformance suite; and the unit tests: `today-links`, `promise-answers`, `dated`, `today`, `followups`, `today-history`, `db/migrations*`, `today-members`, `portable`, `ingest`, `outbox`, `idb.store`, and both stores' full conformance. 1,735 tests passed. Not run: e2e (no flow's screens changed), `pnpm test:all`, a second Codex round.

##### What the code review's second round changed (iteration 3)

Codex reviewed the fixes above a second time (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it3b.out`) and found five more problems. Four are fixed here, one commit each. Each fix has a test that fails without it.

- **A denial reaches across the words between (finding 1, P1).** `replacesPlainly` looked for a denial only in the three words before a replacing word. So "Don't, under any circumstances, skip the methods section; please send the full draft by Monday", quoted as "skip the methods section", still closed the promise. Now any denial earlier in the replacing word's clause (not, never, -n't, no, ni, nunca) makes the replacement ambiguous, and an ambiguous replacement replaces nothing. A denial in another clause still doesn't count ("Don't worry about it. Skip the methods section…"). Tests: `dated-evidence.test.ts` (en, es) and `dated.test.ts` (through `checkDates`).
- **A reply's other reading of the promise goes with it (finding 2).** A reply read as "scan both papers" and "email both papers" could leave the first reading in the request's item when the verdict linked the second. After a restart, the matcher joined the linked one back through its twin. So Remove and Later again acted on the request. Two changes fix this:
  - **The stores.** In both stores, `apart` now takes every open promise of the reply that the matcher made one item with the request or with the linked promise. Each of them answers the request too (`answers`). They leave the request's item together. If one of them led the item, the request and its own readings go to an item of their own, and a reading that stood alone joins its twin. If something else led, the readings move under the linked promise's item. No revision moves.
  - **The matcher.** `findLinks` gives a promise's twin (`samePromise`) the same `answers`. It never joins a promise into the item of the request it answers, nor that request into the promise's item. Data stored before this fix can't bring the request back either.
  - **Tests.** `today-links.test.ts` covers the review's case, the request left in the twin's item, and another promise of that reply still matched as before. Both stores' conformance covers three cases: the first reading leads, both readings are in the item, and the request leads.
- **A copy line that asks in ordinary words (finding 3).** Two sentences to the reader in Cc produced no rows and `asked: false`: "Diego, FYI, let me know your availability by Friday" and "Diego, FYI, I need your approval by Friday". `asksDirectly` now also knows the request words the other checks use: a "let me know" (`LET_KNOW`), and the sender's need or want ("I need", "we'd like", "you'll need to", "necesito", "quiero que", "tienes que"). The copy line alone still asks nothing: "te copio para que estés al tanto; cuando Ana las entregue te las reenvío". Test: `dated.test.ts` (en, es).
- **"Let me know and…" is the sender's reassurance only when it says so (finding 4).** `noticeOnly` used to accept any clause that went on with "and", "so" or "y". So "When you're back, let me know and approve the final draft" produced no row. Now what follows must be the sender speaking for themselves: "and we'll work it out", "and I'll pick you up", "so I can…", "y lo arreglamos", "y te ayudo". A clause that asks the reader for more is an ask: "and approve…", "so the team can start", "y aprueba…", "y luego aprueba…". Test: `dated.test.ts` (en, es).

**Finding 5 is left to the owner:** the linked-requests perf scenario's SQLite budget (120 ms longest turn against iteration 3's 60 ms).

No prompt words changed, so no real-model eval was run.

**Ran:**
- typecheck (all packages)
- `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files
- unit tests: `today-links`, `promise-answers`, `dated`, `dated-evidence`, `today`, `followups`, `today-history`, `db/today-members`, `ai/dated`, and both stores' full conformance. 1,714 passed, 1 skipped.

### Round 12 and iteration 4's design

Round 12 (iteration 3, medians of two reviewers): Look 8.0, Missed 7.7, Deadlines 7.6, Plans 7.5, Content 7.4, Needs you 7.3, would use daily 7.1, Coming up 7.0, Acting 6.9. Cells at 8+: 21 of 63 (round 11: 12); none under 6 (5); 40 with a reviewer at 8+. Expected items visible by default 117 of 131, none settled shown open, no trap shown by default. Iteration 3's deterministic work is what the reviewers name: "You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed today" is called the best row on screen; the paid card and the replaced promise are gone; seven days whole gave Coming up +1. Acting fell because the walkthrough's palette shot had no row selected (the palette offers Later only for a selected item) and the brief said z where the toast says ⌘Z; both are fixed in the harness. A Fable 5.2 analysis (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/panel/fable-analysis-r12.md`), checked against the stored readings and the model's raw answers, found five faults iteration 3 introduced and a rule bug:

- the when column crushes the sentence when the reader is open ("You said / you'd…", 11 reviews);
- the reader's "Also in this conversation" lists the request a promise already answers (7);
- "needed Sun" for a mail that said "before Monday" (Jordan, Priya);
- a holding reply ("I'll get back to you") is still a promise row beside the request it holds (Margaret);
- v13 dates "reply C to confirm" on the mail's own day, so it's dropped as past (María);
- Priya's Spendly mail never reaches the model: the cost filter (`hasTimeCue`) has no cue for "on the 20th".

Also: one reply's promises as two or three rows (Diego, Priya, Jordan); far-dated requests above undated ones in Waiting on you, and Today's timed rows out of time order; Earlier says "Settled Tue" (the reading's clock, not the mail's); the return leg only as an Also line; travel times in the reader's zone with no place (Theo's two "Mon 4:00 PM"); a quote joined across subject and body refused (Pagewise); a same-day move's new words dropped (Theo's "by 8:00 PM"); the long right-click menu with a red Delete; "N coming up" too quiet (8 reviews).

The owner's answers (2026-11-15): a broken promise with a live day ahead sits **first in Today's Waiting on you**, in Missed's weight, counted as waiting, not missed ("Today, Waiting on you"); **one reply, one row** (promises from one sent message to one person are one row, the dated one leading, the others its Also lines, Done closing them together); **the stake in the row** when the mail states one; **AutoPay-only rows dropped** (a bill the mail says is charged automatically with nothing to do gets no row; this reverses iteration 3's keep); **Coming up as the feed's last heading**, closed, with its count; and three capabilities: **Change…** on an item, **Hand to someone**, and **Mute** this kind or sender. Declined: every bill 30 days ahead; a "Start here" ranking.

#### Iteration 4

**The panel.** The palette shot selects a row first; the brief says what the screen shows (z or ⌘Z; Remove on hover only if it is); one line in the brief says the mailbox was read in full; `scores.py` reads half points; `score-items.py` credits a request its promise's row carries and the keys follow the seven-day window; before reviewers run, a pre-panel look at `feed.py`, `score-items.py` and each persona's reader shots.

**1. Placement and order.** A broken promise with a live day ahead is first in Waiting on you, in Missed's weight, its column unchanged. Waiting on you: requests due within seven days by day, then the undated by arrival, then requests due later. Today's own timed rows by their time. The header's "today" counts what's under Today (or says "to do today" if quiet rows stay out). Earlier's line takes the day the settling mail came ("Paid Mon"), never the day it was read. A trip's return leg (any detail with its own day inside the feed) is a quiet row under its own day too. Coming up is the feed's last heading, closed, "Coming up · 3", opening in place (the catalog's disclosure), the header keeping missed · today · waiting.

**2. The reader and the row.** The sentence keeps at least two thirds of the row; in a narrow feed the when column takes its short form or drops under the sentence. The reader's Also line leaves out a request an open promise answers (the feed's rule). With nothing selected the palette lists Done, Later, Remove disabled, "Select an item first". One Undo key on the toast, the one the help names. The row's menu: What matters' three actions and Reply, the mail's actions under "More". After Done the next row is selected, its mail opened only if the reader was open by the user's own click.

**3. One reply, one row** (follow-ups and the matcher). A promise that only holds a request (`onlyHolds`) is never a row while that request is open. Promises from one sent message to one person are one row: the dated one leads (else the first), the others are its Also lines, Done and Undo act on all. A promise about what you'll bring to, or who comes with you to, an event of the same conversation is that event's detail (`attendsEvent` takes an undated promise and "bring … for/to").

**4. The reading (dated v14, follow-ups v12), eval cases first.** The cost filter reads a bare day of the month ("the 20th", "el día 20"), "by then", "close(s)"; a quote not found verbatim is taken from the one line of the mail (subject included) that holds the item's date, otherwise refused; "before <day>" is kept as a fact on the item and the column says "needed before Mon" (sorting still by the day before); "reply C to confirm" is an undated ask tied to its event, closed when the event passes; a bill charged automatically with nothing to do gets no row; a same-day move with checked new words updates the text; `needsPresence` and the lapse fact read the whole mail for their kinds; the stake, when the mail states one, in at most six words after the action, replacing the bracket, never lengthening past fourteen words ("Upload the W-9 ($18,000 held)", "Update the card for Stratus (deploys pause Mon)"); the object named ("the Railyard script", "funding extension"); a wish with no question ("I'd like to sit down before next year") gets no day; "within 7 to 10 business days" is no dated delivery; an alert says the step its mail names; a request's own time in its bracket; follow-ups: a second sentence about how or with what you'll do the first is one promise, "I'll bring Nina" beside a meeting is no promise, a morning promise is over by noon.

**5. Travel in the place's time.** An event at a named place away from the reader's zone is stored at the right instant from the mail's zone and shows the mail's clock with the place ("Mon 4:00 PM Austin"); the reader's own zone stays in the reader.

**6. Change…** On a dated item and a promise (menu, reader line, palette): change its day and time (the Later picker's date and time control), revision-checked, with Undo; a changed item is the user's from then on (a later mail's move doesn't override it without saying so); Earlier says "Changed". Not the words, not the kind.

**7. Mute.** After Remove, once per sender and kind, the toast offers "Don't show deliveries like this" / "Don't show <sender>". Muted senders and kinds live in What matters' settings (a bounded list, unmutable there); a muted sender's mail isn't read for What matters at all (no AI cost), a muted kind's items aren't stored or shown. Spam's rule stands.

**8. Hand to someone.** On an item (menu, reader line, palette): "Hand to…" opens an inline forward of its conversation to a person (autocomplete), prefilled with one line ("Could you take care of this: <item> (by <day>)?"), in the reader as composing always is. Sent, the item closes as handed ("Handed to Jess Tue" in Earlier, Undo until sent), and the forward is a follow-up awaiting their reply like any sent mail.

**Tests and perf** as before: unit and backend for every rule, both stores' conformance for new fields and reads, e2e for the header, Coming up's heading, the reader line's width, the palette with nothing selected, Change…, Mute and Hand to…, the Today perf gate, the dated and follow-ups evals.

**Out of scope:** a "Start here" ranking (declined); every bill 30 days ahead (declined); a second-day capture.

#### Revisions after the Codex review of iteration 4

Codex (read-only, `/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/critique-it4.out`) raised 12 points, all taken. Each part has its own shape-only migration, numbered so they apply in order: placement v65, one reply v66, the reading v67, Change v68, Mute v69, Hand to v70.

1. **Change is a schedule command of its own, owned by the user.** A revision-checked `schedule` act on a dated item and a promise (day, time, dated, timed), with exact snapshots for Undo (a promise's `dated`, `timed`, `brokenDueAt` too); the item records that its schedule is the user's, and both stores' finish transactions never move it on a later mail, keeping the mail's move as a visible proposal on the reader's line ("Elena says Mon now · Use"); an active Later is cleared by a change; the form is the shared `WhenForm`, not Later's window; duplicates change together, grouped promises keep their own.
2. **Mute is a store-owned table.** Normalized sender keys and enum kinds, bounded (refused at capacity, never evicting); the dated reader checks a muted sender before any body read or provider call (and aborts an in-flight read, rechecking at finish) on every entry point (`consider`, `scanPicks`, the hold, settings changes); a muted kind still lets settlements and moves through but writes no new item of it; `stillWaiting` (both stores) leaves a muted sender's conversations out without claiming a reading; existing items of a muted sender or kind are hidden (not closed), and come back on unmute; future-only reading after unmute; Spam's whole-thread rule ahead of all; mute addresses never in prompts or diagnostics.
3. **Hand to… lives through the send.** A supported forward option in the composer inserts the line through its draft machinery without overwriting authored work (resume, convert or focus a draft as it does), with the existing recipient autocomplete; the handoff (items, revisions, draft) is saved with the draft and the send; the item closes as handed only in the provider-confirmation transaction (beside `followupSendConfirmed`), never on queueing; cancelling the send leaves it open, a failure doesn't claim it; the recipient is the one actually sent to; the forward gets an awaiting follow-up seeded directly, not left to the model.
4. **One reply, one row is presentation grouping, not identity.** A group of a reply's promises to one person (`messageId`, normalized person) with a stable row id and its own lead; members keep their own `answers`; Done acts on the union of their answered requests and duplicates atomically, Undo all or none; the reader keeps grouped promises as Also lines. A holding promise is stored with its request (the follow-ups verdict keeps "holding" and its request) and is no row while that request is open. Attendance matching stays separate.
5. **No quote repair by date alone.** A quote not found verbatim is replaced only by one unique span (subject or body, never joined) that holds both the item's action or object and its date; ambiguous or unsupported, refused; every ordinary check applies to the span; never for settlement or replacement evidence.
6. **Perf against 60 ms.** Mute and grouping are batched reads, never per row; the waiting facts are batched; the Today gate measures cold matching and list-then-`threads.counts` at the combined bounds against 60 ms; any reconstruction in background slices.
7. **Earlier keeps events apart from closing.** A settlement records the settling message and its date (`src.date`), shown as the line's day, while the history's order stays the closing time; Change and Hand to record bounded events (before/after schedule; the recipient), so an open item can have a "Changed" line.
8. **The broken promise is placed once.** One fact from `promiseDay` ("broken, live day ahead", respecting Later) carried through both projection paths: Today's Waiting, first, counted once as waiting, Missed's weight. Waiting's order: requests within seven days by day, undated by arrival, later requests. Today's timed rows by time (the comparator ranks time before tier for timed rows). The header follows what it counts, reminders included.
9. **Coming up is a feed boundary.** The feed's last part is an explicit disclosure with its own nested buckets and stable ids; closed, its rows are out of j/k's sequence, with a defined cursor fallback on collapse; Earlier gets its own loading boundary so opening Coming up never pages history. A return leg keeps `detailOf` and also has its own quiet row on its day.
10. **Travel keeps its zone.** The checked wall date and time, IANA zone and place are stored beside the instant through writes, moves and both stores; the feed formats a travel row from them ("Mon 4:00 PM Austin"), the reader from the instant in its own zone; `instantIn` validates the round trip with a stated policy for DST gaps and overlaps; never a zone from an ambiguous abbreviation alone.
11. **"Before" and a confirmation are stored relations.** The stated boundary ("before Mon") is stored apart from the sorting day and carried through `withNeeds`, `TodayWhy`, moves and Change/Undo (no guessing for old rows). "Reply C to confirm" is an ask linked to its event, ending with the event, not `askEnd`, never merged with it through `sameAs`.
12. **v14's words and the app's cuts change together.** Action plus stake within fourteen words; the cut keeps a stated consequence whole and never leaves "unless", the amount or the action cut away; a routine automatic bill with nothing to do is dropped while a priced renewal or trial decision stays; `needsPresence` and the lapse fact look in the whole mail but only at sentences tied to that item; a same-day move with checked new words updates the text.

#### How iteration 4's Change… was built

Design point 6 with the Codex review's revisions 1 and 7 (Change's parts), on `t3/what-matters-204` from `460a4acc1`, beside agents A (placement, v65), B (one reply, v66), C (the reading, v67), E (Mute, v69) and F (Hand to, v70). Migration v68.

**The store (revision 1).** Change is a schedule act of its own, never Done/Remove/Later's `today.act`: the contract's `today.schedule(changes)` and its Undo `today.unschedule(snapshots)` (`packages/store/src/schedule.ts`), in both stores (`packages/db/src/schedule.ts`, `packages/idb/src/schedule.ts`), one transaction (one command) over both tables, every row at the revision it was read at and still open, all or none. Each row gets its new day, moment and `timed` (`dated` set), its Later cleared, any proposal taken away, `changed_at` the act's moment and `changed_from` its schedule as it was; a promise also its state (open when its new moment is ahead), its Inbox top (`none`) and its broken day gone (the day is the user's now). The snapshots hold every field it touches (a dated item's day, moment, `timed`, `dated`, `actAt`, words, Later, change and proposal; a promise's state, Inbox top, moment, `dated`, `timed`, `brokenDueAt`, Later, change and proposal), and the Undo puts each back only while every row is still at the revision the change left. **Migration v68**, shape only: `changed_at`, `changed_from`, `proposal` on `dated_items` and `followups`, nullable, no index (both tables are bounded and read whole); 10 ms at 57,000 conversations, v24 → v68 191 ms (load 6 to 10). IndexedDB: no version bump (the fields are a record's, unindexed; a record without them has none). Both keep `changed_from` and `proposal` as JSON text, so one reading (`parseSchedule`, `parseProposal`, core) serves both.

**The user's schedule against a later mail (revision 1).** `changed_at` set is "the schedule is the user's". In both stores' `finishDated`, a move (`moved`, `movedPromises`) of such an item is kept as its `proposal` (the new day, moment, `timed`, a dated item's `actAt` and its new words when the move gave some, who said it, the message and when), under the move's own guards (a dated item open and reached by the message; a promise at the revision the reader saw, on its thread, open or due), never applied; a move to the schedule it already has takes an older proposal away. A proposal moves the revision (something new to look at: an Undo across it is refused and says so). Items never changed move as before. `finishFollowups` needed nothing: the follow-ups reader never moves a promise's day (only the dated reader's `movedPromises` does), so there's nothing there to hold back.

**The act (backend).** `TodayService.schedule(seen, ask)` (`today.schedule` over RPC, renderer only) reads the named item and the items it's the same thing as (`today.members` of its canonical, never a reply's grouped promises: agent B's grouping is presentation, so each grouped promise keeps its own day) alone with the matcher, as an act does, and `scheduleChanges` (`packages/backend/src/today-schedule.ts`, pure) writes: a dated item at its kind's lead from the new day, as a mail arriving now would place it (`datedTiming`: a bill a week before, an event within the seven days); a promise at the moment, or 9:00 of a day alone. `ask` is a day and a time ("HH:MM" or none) or `{ accept: true }`, which takes the named item's proposal exactly (its words too) and gives the others its day. Refused, with words that say what to do: changed or closed since (stale; an accepted proposal no longer there too), a moment already past, a day more than a year ahead, a person's ask (it has no day; a day would make it another kind). An accepted proposal is a change like any: the item stays the user's. Its Undo (`today.scheduleUndo`, `s`-ids, the last 50) is its own and says so when something changed since.

**Earlier's "Changed" (revision 7).** One change event an item (the latest: `changed_at`, `changed_from`), so it's bounded with the item. The history gets a third source of lines: an open item the user changed, one a canonical item at its newest change, keyed at the moment of the change (`HistoryKey.src` 2 for a dated item, 3 for a promise; `historyAfter` generalized to four sources), "Changed today" / "Cambiado hoy" in the column, its row id `change:<canonical>` (never the open row's), read from the open items in hand (the cached dated rows, `followups.active()` when follow-ups are on), no store read of its own. Once the item closes, its closing line says what happened and the Changed line goes; the change's Undo takes the line away.

**The app.** One form, `ChangeForm` (`components/change-form.tsx`): the shared date and time fields (`WhenFields`, plan 87's, Later's own; it gains `noTime`, a day alone) and "At a time" (the catalog's checkbox) for an item with a time of its own, with no window but now (a day may be today, a time must be ahead; the clock read again on submit). It starts on the item's own day and time (a day over, or none, tomorrow). From the row's right-click menu ("Change…" right after Later in What matters' own group: one `Item`, so agent A's restructuring takes it with the group) and the palette ("Change day and time…", the open row's item or the reader's choice) it opens in a dialog for the item as it was (`openChange`, `TodayChangeDialog`, the item's words under the title); on the reader's line it's a quiet "Change…" beside Later, the form in a popover anchored to it. Every control is named for a screen reader ("Change day and time: Pay the Lumen invoice"). The snack says the new day and the task ("Changed to Nov 24 4:00 PM: Pay the Lumen invoice"), with Undo (z too); a refusal says why. A person's ask has no Change…. The proposal is a quiet line under the reader's line: "Elena says Mon now · Use" (es "Elena ahora dice lun · Usar"), the day as the row's column says it, the sender's first name; Use is in the line's ink (`link-secondary`: axe found the primary link under AA on the AI card's surface), named "Use Mon for Return the signed field trip slip". en and es throughout.

**Tests.** Conformance, both stores (`conformance/schedule.ts`, 6 × 2): a dated item and a promise changed at once with every field, and the Undo exact (`toEqual` on the whole row); a stale revision or a closed item changing nothing, all or none; an Undo after a Later since restoring nothing; a broken, due promise opened with its broken day gone, both back on Undo; a later mail's move as a proposal with its words while an unchanged item still moves, taking it and its Undo bringing the proposal back, a move to the user's own day clearing it; a promise's proposal only at the revision seen and on its thread; after reopening the store. Backend (`today-schedule.test.ts`): `scheduleChanges` (each kind's lead, a promise's 9:00, open members only, never an ask, every refusal, an accepted proposal's exact moment and words) and through the RPC (the row's new day, Later cleared, the Changed line and its key, Undo; a stale revision and a stale Undo saying so; Jess's later "Friday the 20th" as a proposal, Use, Undo; a duplicate changing with its canonical under one Changed line; a broken promise changed, then Jess's move kept as hers). Web: `lib/change.test.ts` (targets, where the form starts, the proposal's words en and es, the act's RPC and its own Undo, refusals said), `thread-items-change.test.tsx` (Change… on the line beside Later, named, none for an ask or a closed item; the form on the item's day; the proposal and Use). E2E (`what-matters-change.spec.ts`): from the row's menu (its place after Later, the dialog, "At a time", a day and time, the item moved, "Changed today" in Earlier, z), from the reader's line (the popover, the snack's Undo), a later mail's move as a proposal on the reader's line (Use, z bringing it back), the palette, Spanish; axe on the dialog and the line in light and dark. `today.spec`'s menu-order check gains Change….

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (all packages): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean. Unit: both stores' whole conformance (1,450 passed, 1 skipped), `db/migrations.test.ts` and `migrations-shipped.test.ts` (v68's own test; v57, v58, v61, v62 and v63's whole-row expectations gain v68's columns, `CHANGE_V68`), the backend's `today-schedule`, `today-history`, `today`, `dated`, `promise-answers`, `today-links`, `followups`, core's `today`, `today-history`, `today-projection`, web `change`, `thread-items-change`, `thread-items`, `dated`, `today-task`, `state/today` (a promise's task now carries its moment), `today-history-paging`, `today-sentence`, `command-palette`, `send-later-picker`, i18n's and the designs' source scans, `architecture.test.ts`: all pass. `migrations.perf.test.ts`: passes (v68 10 ms). E2E (`E2E_PORT=5531`): `what-matters-change.spec.ts` passes; `what-matters-feed.spec.ts` and `today.spec.ts` (every design) pass, but for `today.spec.ts:662`, which failed once in a scenario this doesn't touch and passed whole twice after; on the base tree (`460a4acc1`) the same test failed in its reader-line scenario under the same load: flaky there, not this. `idb/capture-repair-upgrade.int.test.ts` fails on the base too (its SQL35 fixture doesn't drop v64's `sent_confirmed`). Not run: the Today perf gate (load 6 to 10; no read was added to the list or the count: the Changed lines are read only with the history), `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review (the series' owner runs it).

**Screenshots** (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it4-d-shots/`, light and dark): `change-dialog-{en,es}-*.png` (the menu's and the palette's dialog, "At a time" on in en), `change-reader-popover-{en,es}-*.png` (the reader's Change… and its form beside the feed), `change-proposal-{en,es}-*.png` (the reader's line with "Elena says Wed now · Use", "Elena ahora dice mié · Usar").

**Known limits.** One change event an item: Earlier says the latest change, never a list of them, and only while the item is open. A proposal moves the revision, so an Undo of a Later or a change made before it is refused (it says the item changed). A proposal of a promise carries no broken day (a change takes it away anyway). The feed's row doesn't show a proposal; the reader's line does. A promise's day without a time is 9:00 that day, as follow-ups place one. With nothing selected the palette offers no Change… (agent A's "Select an item first" list may add it disabled).

**Shared code touched outside Change….** `DatedRow` and `FollowupRow` gain three optional fields; both stores' row readers read them (`scheduleFields`) and both `finishDated`s call `proposedMove` first in their two move loops; `TodayStore` gains `schedule`/`unschedule`; `HistoryKey.src` is `HistorySource` (0 to 3) in core, the RPC's `historyKey`, the web's list cursor and a backend test's helper type; `historyAfter` generalized; `HistoryOutcome` gains `changed`; `historyPage` takes `changed` and draws its lines; `TodayService` gains `schedule`, `undoSchedule`, `changedItems` (its `history` passes them); `WhenFields` gains `noTime`; the app's `DialogName` gains `todayChange` (the diagnostics' breadcrumb says "other"); `TodayTask`'s promise carries `dueAt` and `timed`; the row menu's `TaskItems` and the palette's What matters block one entry each; the migration tests' whole-row expectations; `today.spec`'s menu order.


#### How iteration 4's Hand to… was built

Design point 8 with the Codex review's revisions 3 and 7, on a branch from `460a4acc1`; migration v70. Agents A (placement and the row menu), B (one reply), C (the reading), D (Change…) and E (Mute) are apart; where this touches what they touch, it says so below.

**The hand-off is data, resolved once.** `Handoff` (`packages/core/src/handoff.ts`) is the row's items at the revisions they were resolved at: the item the row names (`named`), its other members, and a request a handed promise answers (`answered`), at most `HANDOFF_ITEMS_MAX` (32, refused past it, never cut). `today.handoff` resolves them as Done on the row would (`TodayService.rowItems`, the part of `act` that reads a row's members and what its promises answer, now shared by both, alone with the matcher), refuses an item changed since it was seen or in Spam or Trash (STALE), and gives the message the forward forwards (the named item's own) with its words and day for the line. Nothing changes there.

**Saved with the draft, carried by the send** (revision 3). The renderer saves the hand-off as a draft field (`drafts.handoff_json`, v70); both stores keep it only while the draft is a forward (a draft turned into a reply, or whose message went, drops it; `convertFields` drops it locally too, and a conversion's Undo brings it back with the reply's words); an agent's save can't set one. `compose.queueSend` reads it from the draft row at the revision being sent, in its transaction, and copies it onto the send op's target (`target_json`, no column). A scheduled send and Send's Undo window carry it the same way: their params fence the draft's id, generation and revision, and the draft row (with its hand-off) stays until the send is queued, so a cancel (`scheduled.cancel`, Undo send) leaves the draft, its hand-off and the item as they were. *Choice:* the hand-off isn't repeated in `compose.send`'s params: the draft at that revision is its one source, so the two can't disagree.

**Closed only when the provider confirms** (revision 3). Both stores' `ops.complete`, beside `followupSendConfirmed`, in the commit that records the send as done: `handoffConfirmed` (`packages/db/src/handoff.ts`, `packages/idb/src/handoff.ts`, the rule in `packages/store/src/handoff.ts`) closes the items only if every named one is still open at its revision (a Done, Later, Remove, settlement or Change since wins and nothing is claimed), then each member still open at its revision: `done` with `closed_reason` `handed` and `handed_to` (the first To of the message as sent, name and address, bounded), a request a handed promise answered `settled` as Done leaves it (`ANSWERED_WITH_PROMISE`). `scheduledSent` (queued), a failure (`ops.fail`) and an uncertain delivery (`markUnknown`) claim nothing. `ops.complete` says `handed`, and the backend reads What matters and Follow-ups again (`TodayService.handed`).

**The forward's own wait.** In the same commit, with follow-ups on, the forward gets plan 142's awaiting row for its person (`AWAITING_ITEM`, due after the user's nudge days, `followups.nudgeAfterDays` passed to `complete`), never left to the model's `expectsReply`; the follow-ups reader's later reading of the same message finds it there and adds none. At the active bound it's left out, as any wait is. It shows in Follow-ups until they reply (plan 142's own closing).

**History** (revision 7). The closing is the event: `HistoryOutcome` `handed` with `handedTo`, "Handed to Jess Tue" (es "Delegado a Jess el mar"), the person by first name, else their address's local part; a history line's reader line says the same. *Choice:* the recipient lives on the item (`handed_to`, one per item: a closed item is handed once), not in an events table, so it shares no table with Change's events (agent D).

**The composer's forward option.** `ComposeOpenOptions.handoff` (`compose.ts`): wherever Forward lands (a new forward, the conversation's draft converted or resumed, the pinned one), `handOffInto` saves the hand-off with it and puts its line above what's written through `editDraft` (saved like typing): alone in an empty body, a paragraph of its own over authored words (a rich body gains it as its first paragraph), never twice; a pinned reply isn't turned into a forward (r / a / f never convert it) and says so ("This conversation's draft is a reply. Send or discard it first, then hand this on."). To takes focus, so the recipient goes through the composer's own autocomplete; the forward's source is chosen as `replyFields` chooses it (`sourceId`, the item's message).

**The line** (`handOffLine`, `lib/hand-to.ts`): "Could you take care of this: sign the field trip form (by Fri)?" (es "¿Puedes encargarte de esto: … (para el vie)?"): the item's words without a zone note or full stop, an ordinary first word lower-cased; its day only when it names one still ahead (today, tomorrow, a weekday, a date); none for an undated item or one past.

**Where it's offered.** The row's right-click menu, "Hand to…" (es "Delegar…") after Later (`menu-dated-hand`; the composer keeps focus as Forward from the menu does); the reader's line, a quiet button after Later (`thread-dated-hand`, named "Hand to someone: …"); the palette (`palette-dated-hand`, found by "hand off, delegate, assign, forward to someone"). Hand to… opens the item's conversation first when it isn't open (as Forward from the list does) and lets its reader mount before the forward lands, so a forward left there as a draft is resumed in it.

**A held row leaves when put away** (`feed-pane.ts`). A row the feed holds only because its conversation is open (revision 9: it left the backend's answer meanwhile, handed on by the confirmed forward, or settled) now leaves when that conversation is put away (Esc, the reader's Close, a second click): the list reads again. Before, it stayed until some later reload.

**Shared code touched (outside this part):** `TodayService.act` split into `rowItems` and the write (no change in behaviour; agents D and E edit near it); `ops.complete`'s options and result (`nudgeAfterDays`, `handed`); `DatedRow`/`FollowupRow.handedTo`, `HistoryOutcome`, `TodayHistoryItem.handedTo`; the row mappers in both stores (one line each, beside agent C's); `drafts.ts`, `compose-fields.ts`'s `convertFields`, `compose.ts` (the option, `handOffInto`, one line in `composeLanding`); `thread-menu.tsx` (one item in `TaskItems`, which agent A restructures), `thread-items.tsx` (one button, and the closed line's outcome), `command-palette.tsx` (one command), `feed-pane.ts`; `today.spec.ts`'s menu order; the earlier migrations' tests gained `handed_to` / `handoff_json`; the dated conformance's full row; IndexedDB's `followups.own` exported.

**Migration v70** (shape only): `drafts.handoff_json`, `dated_items.handed_to`, `followups.handed_to`, nullable, no rewrite, no index. 21 ms at 57,000 conversations (gate 1 s); v24 → v70 234 ms (gate 3 s; the machine at load 7 to 11). IndexedDB: no version bump (a row without them reads none).

**Tests.** Store conformance, both stores (`conformance/handoff.ts`): a draft keeps a hand-off only on a forward (kept across saves, cleared by null, dropped by a reply or one naming no item); queued and held claim nothing, the confirmation closes the items as handed to the first To, with its wait, Earlier's read and a restart; follow-ups off, no wait; a change since claims nothing, nor its members; a member changed since stays; a failure and an uncertain delivery claim nothing; a handed promise settles the request it answers. Migration v70. Backend (`hand-to.test.ts`, the real backend with a scripted provider): `today.handoff` resolves and refuses a stale revision; held for Undo then cancelled, the draft is back with its hand-off and nothing closes; queued and on its way (the provider gated) nothing closes, confirmed it's handed with its wait and Earlier's line; a send failed for good or uncertain claims nothing; an agent's save can't set one. `today-history.test.ts`: the `handed` line with its person. Web: `handOffLine` en and es; `handOffInto` (an empty forward, over authored words never twice, a pinned reply refused); `composeLanding` with the option landing a new forward, and converting a written reply with its Undo taking the hand-off back; `historyWhen` en and es; a held row leaving when put away (`today-held.test.ts`). E2E (`hand-to.spec.ts`): the palette's Hand to… on the open item, then the menu's resuming that forward (its line once, To focused), axe on the composer light and dark; Undo send leaving the draft and the item open; sent and confirmed, the item leaves, Earlier says "Handed to jess today", the wait in Follow-ups; the reader line's "Delegar…" in Spanish to "Delegado a Jess hoy". `today.spec.ts`, `what-matters-feed.spec.ts`, `compose.spec.ts`, `followups.spec.ts`: 82 passed on the crowded first pass and two failed (today's dated-items test, the feed's inbox-zero setting), both passing on a second run alone.

**Screenshots** (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it4-f-shots/`): `e2e-forward-{en,es}-{light,dark}.png` (the forward under the item's line, its line written, the recipient chosen), `e2e-earlier-{en,es}-{light,dark}.png` (Earlier's "Handed to jess today", "Delegado a Jess hoy").

**Known limits.** The hand-off's members are resolved when Hand to… is chosen: a duplicate that joins the item later isn't closed with it. A Later or Change made between Hand to… and the confirmed send keeps the item open (the user's later choice wins), and nothing says so. Only the first To is the person handed to. The line uses the item's stored words (in the language they were read in). After the confirmation there's no Undo of the hand-off itself (Undo send is its Undo while it's held). The reader's line now has four controls (five with Change…): keeping them within the sentence's two thirds is agent A's width rule. Not run: the Codex code review (as asked), `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`.


#### How iteration 4's Mute was built

The owner's "Mute this kind/sender" (point 7), as revisions 2 and 6 have it, on `t3/what-matters-204` from `460a4acc1`, beside the other five parts.

- **The table** (`dated_mutes`, SQLite v69, IndexedDB version 36 / logical 69, shape only: an empty table, 5 ms at 57,000 conversations; v24 → v69 199 ms): `(target, key)` its key, `target` checked to `sender` or `kind`, a sender's name (cut to 200) and when. The store owns it (`dated.mutes`: `list`, newest first; `add`, "added", "already" or "full"; `remove`). Keys are normalized by one function in core (`muteKey`: a sender's `addressKey`, a kind only one of `DATED_KINDS`; anything else throws), so "Track+eu@Parcel.test" and "track@parcel.test" are one mute. Bounded at `DATED_MUTES_MAX` (200): at the bound an add is refused, never one dropped for it, and the backend says so in words ("What matters already hides 200 senders and kinds…"). IndexedDB: an early store with an in-memory index by `target`, as the feature's other stores. Clear AI data keeps it (it's the reader's choice, not what AI read).
- **The reader** (`DatedWorker`) reads the mutes where it needs them (a table of at most 200): `consider` drops a muted sender's mark right after Spam's whole-thread rule, before the untrusted-sender check, the open items, the body read or any provider call; the 14-day check neither counts nor queues their mail (`scanPicks`); the hold before a request leaves (`holdingUp`) stops one for a sender muted meanwhile; and a mute (`mutesChanged`, from the backend's `DatedMutes`) aborts the request on its way, which then finds the sender muted and consumes the mark. The store rechecks in the finish's own commit: a muted sender's reading keeps nothing (no item, settlement, move, "nothing asked") and its mark goes ("gone"); a muted kind's new rows aren't added while its settlements and moves go through (and its rows don't send a reply back to the follow-ups reader). Mail that came while muted is never read later: Unmute is for mail from then on.
- **Hidden, not closed.** `TodayService.datedRows` (the list, the count, the badge, the notice and the clock all read it) leaves out open items of a muted sender or kind, with the mutes read once per rebuild of that cache, which a mute or Unmute rebuilds (`today.changed()`); the reader's line (`dated.ofThread`) hides them too, a history line's own closed item kept. Needs you: both stores' walk (`needsRows`, the step `stillWaiting` is in) reads the muted senders once a walk and leaves out a conversation whose latest message is theirs, recording nothing, so Unmute brings it back as it is. Promises are yours and aren't muted.
- **The offer** (`DatedMutes.offer`, `dated.muteOffer`): after the user's own Remove of an item, its kind the first time an item of that kind is removed, else its sender the first time theirs is; each offered once, ever (a memory of offers in the store's small values, `whatMatters:muteOffered`, the newest 500), nothing already muted. *Decision:* a person's request (`ask`, `respond`) never offers "requests like this", only its sender: one click mustn't hide every request (`OFFERED_MUTE_KINDS`).
- **The snack.** Remove's snack carries the offer as its quiet second button beside Undo: "Don’t show deliveries" / "Don’t show Parcel Co" (es "No mostrar entregas" / "No mostrar a Parcel Co"). *Decision:* the owner's "…like this" is dropped and a long name is cut at 24 characters: the dock is 30rem, and with the longer words the message had about 125 px. With an offer, the first line says what happened ("Removed from What matters") and the task's words go under it. Taking it says so with its own Undo, an Unmute ("You won’t see deliveries in What matters", "Nothing from Parcel Co will show in What matters"); z runs it.
- **The list**, where What matters' settings are: the gear's popover (read only while it's open; nothing muted, no section) and Settings → General → What matters ("Hidden from What matters", found by Settings' search; empty, it says how muting comes about). A kind says it's "From any sender", a sender their address beside their name; "Show again" (named by its visible words, described by the item's name) unmutes at once with Undo that hides it again where it was, the keys moving to the next row's button. en and es.
- **Privacy.** Mutes stay in the store; the reader never puts them in a prompt (a muted sender's mail isn't read, and the list is never sent), and nothing logs them.

**Tests.** Conformance in both stores (`dated-mutes.ts`): normalization and refusals, newest first across a reopen, Clear AI data keeping them, the bound refusing without evicting, a finish of a sender muted meanwhile keeping nothing, a muted kind's rows left out while its settlement and move apply, a muted sender's conversation out of Needs you with nothing recorded and back on Unmute. The v69 migration test and an IndexedDB version-35 upgrade (`mutes-upgrade.test.ts`). Backend (`dated.test.ts`, through the RPC with a scripted model): a muted sender's mail reaches no model and isn't read after Unmute, their items hidden and still open, then back; the 14-day check counting none of theirs; a mute aborting the request on its way before the model answers (fails without `mutesChanged`); a mute written straight to the store refused at the finish; the hold stopping it; a muted kind with its settlement through and another sender's bill kept; the offer's order (kind, sender, nothing; a request only its sender); the bound refused in words. The worker tests' scripted model now ends its wait on an abort, as a provider's request does. Web: the offer's words (en, es, a long name cut), Remove's snack with the offer, Done and Later never asking, the mute's and Unmute's Undo. E2E (`what-matters-mute.spec.ts`): the kind offered, taken, Undo; the sender offered next, taken; the gear's list, axe in light and dark, Show again by keyboard; Settings' empty line and a kind from any sender, axe.

**Perf** (the Today gate now holds 200 mutes, none of them anyone in the mailbox, so every list, count and Needs you walk reads and tests against them). The machine was shared and loaded through the work (load 3 to 6, one spike to 32), and the gate's SQLite numbers swung by 2× between identical runs. At load 5.3 → 3.4: IndexedDB passed everything (spread list 10.3 ms, added 1.0, longest turn 23.4; all showing 9.8 / 0.9 / 17.6; Needs you at its bound 13.4 / 0.3 / 18.7; Coming up far 12.4 / 0.8 / 14.9; combined bounds 16.2 / 2.6 / 24.1). SQLite: spread 19.2 / 0.6 / 54.0, all showing 17.5 / 2.2 / 35.7, then Needs you at its bound over its gate (list 63.4 ms against 45, the longest turn 100.4 against 60, plan 145's own walk 53.3 against 40). The same build with and without the mutes, alternated, at ×3 budget (load 3.1 to 4.4): Needs you at its bound, list 83.4 and 81.7 ms without, 97.2 and 46.3 with; the longest turn 108.6 and 105.7 without, 129.5 and 123.5 with; plan 145's walk 72.0 and 69.9 without, 57.6 and 37.3 with: the noise is larger than anything the mutes add, which is one read of at most 200 keys a walk and a set lookup a row (the cache's rebuild reads them once more). Two more runs once the one-minute load fell under 3 (the 15-minute load still 7 to 9): SQLite's spread list 21.5 and 20.2 ms against its 20 (added 0.9 and 1.0, longest turn 53.0 and 55.5), so the run stopped there; IndexedDB spread 12.7 / 0.4 / 23.1 and 9.9 / 0.4 / 18.9, all showing 9.6 / 1.5 / 13.7 and 9.8 / 0.9 / 12.3, Needs you at its bound 14.0 / 5.4 / 25.4 and 16.9 / 1.0 / 21.6 (one over its added 3, one over its list 15). The waiting rule's own quiet-machine numbers (34–37 ms walk, 56–60 ms turn) are the reference this needs again on a quiet machine; until then the gate isn't shown green for this part.

**Shared edits outside Mute** (small, said here for the merge): `today.spec.ts`'s three Remove snack assertions read either form of the snack; `upgrade.test.ts` and `open-errors.test.ts` read the step before from the migration list (v65 to v68 land in parallel, so numbers skip) and vacuum before filling the disk; `idb.uncertain-upgrade.test.ts` lists the new store; `capture-repair-upgrade.int.test.ts` drops v64's `sent_confirmed` (it was failing on the base) and the new table; the backend's `dated.test.ts` model honours an abort. IndexedDB version 36 and logical 69 are taken here; a part landing beside it with its own store renumbers on rebase.

**Known limits.** The open row (held while open) stays in place after a mute until it's put away, as every held row does. A mute doesn't reach promises of yours to that sender. The offer's memory is per mailbox, not per window, and an offer read but never shown (a window closed in between) counts as offered. The kind list has no "renewals and expirations" pairing: each is its own kind.



#### How iteration 4's placement was built

On a branch from `460a4acc1`: design points 1 and 2 with the Codex review's revisions 7 (the settling date only; Change's and Hand to's history events are their parts'), 8 and 9, and the reader's width. One reply one row (v66), the reading (v67), Change (v68), Mute (v69) and Hand to (v70) are apart; where this touches what they touch, it says so below.

**The broken promise is placed once (revision 8).** `promiseBroken(r, now)` in `packages/core/src/dated.ts`: a promise of yours, open or due, not put off with Later, that `promiseDay` says was given a day and missed (`said`, from a person's new day or from the request it answers) and that sits under a live day, today or ahead. `promiseGroupAt` makes it Today's at any distance (a broken promise due in three weeks is someone waiting now); the projection (`projectRows`) marks it `part: "waiting"` and `broken`, the list's path and the count's alike (the count needs nothing more than the row it already has), so it counts once, as waiting, never as missed, and the badge (the header's sum) follows. Its column is as it was ("You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed Mon", Missed's weight); it leads Waiting on you, by its live day. Put off with Later it is Coming up's, neither shown nor counted; its live day over, Missed.

**Waiting on you's order (revision 8).** In `ordered()`: the broken promises by their day; then the requests shown early whose day is within the seven days (before the eighth midnight), soonest first; then what has no day (asks, conversations waiting on you) as they came, newest first; then the requests due later, soonest first (Margaret's Gary in December under the dentist and David).

**Today's own rows by time (revision 8).** `byDayKey` in `packages/core/src/today.ts`, Today's order only (`clock` false): what's for the whole day first, by tier (a deadline, your promise, your reminder, a plan), then what has a time, by that time whatever its tier (Priya's payroll at 1:00 PM, the 1:1 at 2:00 PM, Marcus at 4:00 PM), the tier only between rows at one moment; your reminder is at its time there. People waiting (tier 2) come after the rest and keep their order, so the comparator stays one order (Codex's "time before tier for timed rows" read literally isn't transitive across tiers). A day ahead's order is unchanged.

**The header follows what it counts (revision 8).** *Decision:* "to do today" rather than counting the quiet rows. "1 missed · 3 to do today · 5 waiting" (es "1 se te pasó · 3 por hacer hoy · 5 te esperan"): Today's own rows that are something to do: deadlines, your promises and (new) your reminders due today; never a plan or a delivery, which are drawn under Today but happen to you. `todayCounted` no longer leaves reminders out; the count path reads the reminders as stored (`store.reminders.list()`, a few rows at most), the list as the reminders' own look has them, and a change to your reminders (set, cleared, come due) tells What matters' list and the badge (`TodayService.remindersChanged`: the event and the count, never invalidating the cached items nor re-arming the clock; midnight, which brings tomorrow's reminders in, is the clock's already).

**The settling date (revision 7).** `dated_items` and `followups` gain `settled_by` and `settled_at` (**migration v65**, shape only: four nullable columns, no default written, no backfill; IndexedDB no version bump, a row without them reads null). Both stores write the settling message and its date (`src.date`) in the same statement as the closing: the dated reader's settlement (`finishDated`'s `settled`), a promise its person replaced (`replaced`), the follow-ups reader's verdict on a request (`answered`) and a promise kept by your send (`kept`). `DatedRow`/`FollowupRow` carry `settledBy`/`settledAt`; the history line (`TodayHistoryItem`) carries `on` (that date) and `reason` (the closing reason). `historyWhen` says that day and how: "Paid Mon" (a bill mail settled), "Answered Tue" (a request your reply settled), "Settled Wed" otherwise, "Done Tue" for a kept promise; the history's order and paging stay the closing time (`key`). The reader's closed line says the same. Change's "Changed" and Hand to's "Handed to Jess" (D's and F's) will add their own outcomes beside these in `historyWhen`.

**Coming up as a feed boundary (revision 9).** The header no longer says Coming up. The feed's last heading, after the open part and the automated section and before Earlier, is the catalog's disclosure (`Collapsible` + `CollapsibleTrigger`, rendered as the `h2`, "Coming up · 3", es "Más adelante · 3", its chevron turning, the same words open or shut, `aria-controls` the feed's rows), closed by default and remembered as left (the guarded localStorage key, as before). Open, its rows are its own buckets (`coming:<displayBucket>`: a day for the first two weeks, then `coming:week:<Monday>`), each a quiet `h3` keyed by its bucket (stable), never joining a day of the open part; the backend's sections and order are unchanged. Closed, its rows aren't in the feed's rows at all, so j/k never reach them. *The cursor fallback:* closing it while the feed is on one of its rows (open beside the feed, or where a conversation was put away) puts that conversation away, as Esc does, and puts the feed on the last row above Coming up (`leaveComingUp`). An empty feed with something coming up shows "Nothing needs you today." with the disclosure under it. *Earlier's boundary:* the history's next page is asked for when the drawn window comes near the feed's end (a boolean) or a page lands (the count of history lines), never because rows above it came or went: opening Coming up adds rows and moves no boundary. The digest's row stays the open part's last; Done's next row (below) skips it.

**The return leg (revision 9).** `placeItems` keeps a detail as its parent row's Also line while the parent shows and also places it as a quiet row of its own (a Plan: never counted) under its own day, or Coming up's past the seven days.

**The row's width (design point 2).** The item's button is a grid: the sentence in the first column; the when column at most a third of the row (`max-w-[calc(100cqw/3-1.5rem)]`, each feed row a query container), its words wrapping there (balanced); in a feed narrower than 30rem (the reader open beside it, at 1440 and at 1100) the when column goes under the sentence, on its own line above who, and the sentence has the row's whole width. Done and Later's room on the second line follows (`ROOM_BESIDE_WHEN` wide, `ROOM_NARROW` narrow). *Choice:* dropping under rather than a short form: no new words to keep in step with the reading's "needed before" (C's) and Change's; rows beside the reader are three lines, which the owner's airy rows allow.

**The reader's Also (design point 2).** `readerItems` applies the feed's own rule (`withoutAnswered`, now exported by core): a request an open promise answers (and the requests it's one item with) is no line of its own; its own again once the promise is closed; the matcher's one item stays that item's line. Agent B's grouped promises as Also lines are B's.

**The palette, the Undo key, the menu, after Done (design point 2).** With nothing selected in What matters, ⌘K lists Done, Later… and Remove from What matters, unavailable (cmdk's disabled), with their keys and "Select an item first" (es "Primero elige un elemento"). The snack's Undo pill shows one key, the one the shortcuts help names first: Z while single keys are on, the platform's undo when they're off (`countdown-pill.tsx`, shared with Undo send; both keys still work and are named in `aria-keyshortcuts`). A What matters item's right-click menu is its Done, Later and Remove (with "The email stays"), then Reply, then "More ▸" with the mail's actions (Reply all, Forward, Archive, Delete, read, star, pin, Move to, Category, and the old More's rows folded in, no submenu in a submenu); a list's menu is unchanged. After Done (Remove, Later) on the open row, the next row's mail opens only when the reader was open by the user's own choice (a click, Enter, j/k: `openedByApp` false), or a Done went on from such a one (`useFeedRow.carried`); a mail the app opened is put away instead and the feed is on the next row, the keys there. The row the feed was put away on moves on likewise. Done's next row is never the digest's page.

**Tests.** Core: `promiseBroken` (Later, its live day over, a closed one, a far day), the projection's broken promise first and counted once in both paths, Waiting on you's three bands, Today's rows by time, reminders counted (one on a conversation with a row of its own, one in Trash), the return leg's own row under its day and Coming up's. Backend: a reminder due today in the header and the badge, tomorrow's in neither, and its change emitted; the history line's `on` and `reason`. Stores: v65 (its own test, and v57's to v63's shapes gained the columns), the settling message and date for each of the four settlements in both stores, the reader's row shape. Web: Coming up's own buckets after the digest and its cursor fallback (`today.test.tsx`), the header without Coming up, `historyWhen`'s verbs and the mail's day (en, es), the reader's answered request, after Done by the user and by the app (`today-task.test.ts`), the Undo keycap. E2E (`what-matters-feed.spec.ts`, iteration 4's own test; iteration 3's and `today.spec.ts`'s updated): "to do today" and the badge's sum, the broken promise first in Missed's weight and Gary's December request last, the sentence's two thirds and the when column under it beside the reader, the menu's More, the palette with nothing selected, Coming up as the last heading with j inside it and its fallback when closed, j never reaching it closed, axe light and dark with Coming up open (iteration 3's test); `plain-actions.spec.ts`'s keycap.

**Perf (the Today gate, 50k threads; a shared machine, load 3 to 14).** SQLite at the gate's own budget, interleaved with the base (`460a4acc1`, these packages checked out over the branch's): spread list p95 19.0 / 19.4 / 20.4 ms (base 19.5 / 21.0), the count's added 0.8 to 1.2 (gate 2), longest turn 56.3 / 56.4 / 56.9 (base 57.3 / 59.9; gate 60); all showing 17.3 to 18.6 / 1.9 to 2.1 added / 31.6 to 34.6 (base 16.6 to 17.9 / 1.2 to 1.9 / 31.0 to 32.4); Needs you at its bound list 44.8 / 46.4, added 4.6 / 1.6, longest turn 61.2 / 63.0 (base 44.6 / 45.0, added 2.6 / 1.9, longest 63.1 / 63.8): over the 60 ms ceiling on both sides, as iteration 3 recorded for the base; Coming up far 50.4 to 55.6 / 0.7 to 1.2 / 64.0 to 75.1 (base 52.7 / 1.1 / 68.1); linked requests 56.1 to 58.1 / 9.9 to 10.5 / 79 to 132 (base 56.4 / 10.4 / 134; gate 85 / 12 / 120). IndexedDB at the gate's budget, every scenario passing: spread 9.6 / 0.5 / 16.4 (list, added, longest turn), all showing 8.1 / 0.6 / 11.2, at the bound 12.5 / 1.9 / 17.3, Coming up far 13.7 / 1.5 / 16.4, linked 16.1 / 1.7 / 22.4 (base 18.8 / 1.8 / 22.4). The first build had `today.changed()` on a reminders change: the gate's first list invalidated the cached 1,000 items and re-armed the clock, spread's longest turn 64.9 to 66.7 ms; `remindersChanged` took it back under the base. What's added on the hot path: `promiseBroken` per promise (an early return for most), one more date and three filters in Today's ordering, the count's read of the reminders (a few rows). `migrations.perf.test.ts` passes with v65.

**Screenshots** (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it4-a-shots/`, `WM_IT4_SHOTS`): `feed-top`, `coming-up-closed`, `coming-up-open`, `reader-long-row`, `menu`, `menu-more`, each `-{en,es}-{1440,1100}-{light,dark}.png`.

**Known limits.** In Spanish a broken promise's column wraps in the narrow feed before "· lo necesitan el dom" (a non-breaking space before the dot would keep it with what precedes; left for the "needed before" wording, C's, which changes those strings). The reader's line says "Due Sunday" for a broken promise whose feed column says "You said Tue 20 (missed) · needed Sun" (the reader line's wording is outside this part). j/k into Coming up pass the automated section's row, whose page takes j/k for itself (as before). Earlier's boundary has no test of its own (the feed's virtualizer isn't drawn in jsdom, and no e2e here pages history while toggling Coming up). Not run: `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review.


#### How iteration 4's reading was built

Design points 4 and 5 with the Codex review's revisions 5, 10, 11 and 12: the cost filter, the quote span, "before" a day, "reply C", travel on the place's clock, prompt v14 and the app's cuts and checks, on `t3/what-matters-204` from `460a4acc1`. Migration **v67** (shape only): `dated_items.before_day`, `confirms`, `wall` (JSON) and `followups.before_day`, nullable, no backfill; IndexedDB reads an absent field as none (no version change).

- **The cost filter (P3).** `hasTimeCue` reads a bare day of the month ("the 20th", "on the 5th", "el día 20"), "by then", "until then", "para entonces", and a closing ("close", "closing", "cierra"): Priya's Spendly mail ("October books close on the 20th. Please add receipts by then") reaches the reader. Test: the Spendly mail and four more cues.
- **A quote that is nearly the mail's (revision 5).** A quote not found verbatim is replaced by one span, the subject or one sentence of the text, never joined, that holds every naming word of the quote and the item's day (`quoteSpan`); none, two or more (ambiguous), a quote of a date alone, or an item with no day: refused. Every ordinary check then reads the span. Settlement, move and replacement evidence are never repaired. Confirmed in Priya's `readings.jsonl`: the model's "renews on November 18, 2026" joined the subject's "renews on November 18" and the line "Renewal date: November 18, 2026"; the subject is the one span with "renews" and the day. Tests: Pagewise kept with the subject as its quote; another day in the subject, a second sentence saying it again, a date alone, an undated alert, a span addressed to another recipient, a settlement's evidence: refused.
- **"Before Monday" kept apart (revision 11).** `beforeOf` (replacing `dayBefore`) returns the sorting day and the day asked before, whichever of the two the model gave ("before Monday" read as Monday or as Sunday), and reads "before then" / "antes de esa fecha" by the day its paragraph named before it; a question asks too ("¿Tendrías 20 minutos antes de esa fecha?"). It applies to items, to moves, and to a promise's move ("Could you get them to me before then?"). `checkDates` keeps it only as the day after the sorting day, rolls it with `bareDayAhead`, and carries it into rows, moves (a move clears it unless it says one) and moved promises. `withNeeds` gives a promise the request's `neededBefore`; `promiseDay` says `neededBefore` beside `needed`, and `before` when the day a promise sits under is the day before one its person named. The column: "You said Tue 10 (missed) · needed before Mon", "3:00 PM · needed before Sun", a request's own "before Mon" (es "antes del lun"), and plainly "Today" on the day itself. Old rows have none (nothing guessed).
- **"Reply C to confirm" (revision 11).** A request to confirm by replying ("reply C to confirm", "responde C para confirmar"), as an `ask` or as v13's `respond` on the mail's day or the next, in a mail with one kept event, is an undated `ask` linked to that event (`DatedWrite.confirmsItem`, stored as `confirms`, the event's id, in the same commit), kept even from an automated sender to the reader in To. `withEventEnds` gives it the event's end (`eventEnd`), read by `datedPlace`, `datedOver`, `nextTodayChange` and the reader (`askUntil`); an event no longer open ends it at once. Resolved where rows are read for placement: Today's `datedRows`, the worker's `retire` and `ofThread`. The matcher never joins it with another request or a promise. A confirmation with a day of its own stays a `respond`.
- **Travel in the place's time (revision 10).** `instantIn` reads its instant back on the zone's clock with one stated policy: a time a clock change skips (spring forward) names no instant (the day kept, the time lost, as for an unknown zone), a time shown twice (fall back) is the first; never an abbreviation. Prompt v14: an event at a place away from the reader whose time names no zone is on that place's clock, written "09:30 America/Chicago at Austin". The task keeps the mail's clock, read back from the converted instant (`DatedWall`: day, time, zone, place), for every zoned item and move; `checkDates` keeps it only when it reads back as the row's instant (`wallHolds`). The projection gives a timed event with a place `item.travel`; its column says "Mon 4:00 PM Austin" (es "lun 16:00 en Austin"), "4:00 PM Austin" today; the reader shows the instant in the reader's zone. A zone named without a place keeps the existing note form.
- **The app's checks (revision 12).** A same-day move with the mail's own new words changes them and keeps the moment (Theo's tees, "Expected: Today, by 8:00 PM"). `needsPresence` and the lapse fact read the whole mail's paragraphs, leaving out any that holds another item's quote and not this one's (Priya's "An adult signature is required", two paragraphs below). A routine bill charged by itself ("AutoPay is on", "will be charged automatically", "domiciliación activada"), with no plan, subscription, trial or change to decide and no failed charge, is no item (`routineAutoBill`, the owner's "drop AutoPay-only rows"). A delivery only estimated ("within 7 to 10 business days", "en 5 a 7 días hábiles") is no item unless its words say the day. A person's wish with no question and no day said ("I'd like to sit down with you before next year") is at most an undated ask. A reply to the sender asked by a day is the one reply: an undated ask to answer the same person, or a second reply that day, is no second row (Karin's outage, found on the eval). `cutAtClause` keeps a stake whole: a bracket that states an amount, a percentage or what happens, an "unless" clause, and an amount are never cut or dropped by a clause cut; when nothing fits, the words before the stake are cut instead ("Decide on the Creatix Cloud annual plan renewal before Thursday… (cancelling later costs 50%)"), and no cut ends on "unless" or "or".
- **Prompt v14.** The stake in at most six words, in the one bracket after the action, within fourteen words ("Upload the W-9 for Pingwire ($18,000 held)", "Update the card for Stratus (deploys pause Mon)", a renewal's "cancelling later costs 50%"); the object named as the mail does ("Send Jess the Railyard script", "a call on a funding extension", "RSVP to Lauren for Ava's party"; an investor's "extension" is a funding round's); a routine bill charged by itself is no item; "reply C" is an undated ask; travel on the place's clock; a request's own time in its bracket ("(COO briefing 3:00 PM CT)"); a delivery's contents, never the carrier; an estimate has no day; an alert whose mail names the step on the reader's own records by a day is that step, a `respond` with its stake ("Attach 4 receipts in Expensely (card suspended Nov 16)"), while a security notice's reply, click or verify stays an alert; a wish is no request; a same-day move's new words; "before then". Small windows need a little under 8,400 tokens for v14 (v13 7,200): the dated test's window is 8,400.
