- **The eval** (`dated.real.ts`, 222 cases, 27 new, `dated-196` to `dated-222`, fictionalized from round 12, en and es): the Spendly bare day, the Pagewise span, Theo's keynote and tech check in Austin (read in Pacific) and a Mexico City talk read in Madrid, the W-9 payout held (en, es), the Creatix commitment (en, es), Stratus, the Railyard script, the funding extension, Ava's party, Gary's wish (en, es), the debit card's estimate (en, es), the card suspension's receipts (en, es), Karin's COO briefing (en, es), the parcel's contents (en, es), the tees' same-day words, Marcus's "before then" (en, es). `dated-036`, `dated-100` and `dated-168` now expect "reply C" as an undated ask; `dated-153` to `dated-155` (AutoPay) allow no item at all. The eval scores the day asked before (`befores`) on what the app keeps and the mail's own clock (`walls`) on the reading.
- **Eval first.** On v13 with the new checks, one reading of the 32 new and changed cases, English: Luna 34 of 34 found, none wrong shown, 13 wrong words (no stake, no object, no place); Haiku 31 of 34, 3 wrong shown (Karin's twin ask, Marcus's "before then" as an ask), 10 wrong words, 2 of 5 times right, no wall (the tech check at 4:00 PM Pacific).
- **Score, five readings each of the 32 new and changed cases, v14** (170 expected items a model and language): Luna English 169 of 170 found (`dated-213`'s quote reworded in Spanish words, refused: no single span holds them), none wrong shown; Luna Spanish 170 of 170, none wrong; Haiku English 170 of 170, none wrong; Haiku Spanish 170 of 170, 1 wrong shown (Karin's numbered list as a second reply, fixed after: two replies to the sender on one day are one). Every time (30 of 30), day asked before (10 of 10), mail's clock and place (20 of 20) and move's words (5 of 5) right; no settlement or move wrong, no alert unsafe.
- **One full round, all 222 cases** (162 expected items), the final prompt with `dated-168` still expecting v13's respond (re-read three times each after its key changed: 12 of 12 right): Luna English 159 of 162 found, 1 wrong shown besides `168` (`dated-062`'s "out for delivery today" dated the 16th); Luna Spanish 160 of 162, 1 (`dated-163`'s question as a dated request); Haiku English 159 of 162, 2 (`dated-003`'s trial on the day before its Friday, `dated-147`'s newsletter event) and one alert whose stake read as an instruction ("card cancelled if no contact", `dated-099`); Haiku Spanish 161 of 162, 1 (`dated-190`'s domain also as a `respond`), one alert's trailing "se necesita actualizar el método de pago" not stripped (`dated-009`), two times off (`068`, `093`). No settlement, move or replacement wrong; befores 2 of 2, walls 4 of 4, move words 5 of 5 each; no text cut inside a word or a clause. The ledger stood at $40.80 of $55 (iteration 4's reading: about $2.30).
- **Shared code touched outside the reader:** `DatedRow` (`before`, `confirms`, `eventEnd`, `wall`, `DatedWall`), `FollowupRow.before`, `DatedWrite`/`DatedFinish` (`before`, `confirmsItem`, `wall`), both stores' `finishDated` and row mapping (dated and follow-ups), `withNeeds`, `promiseDay`, `PromiseFacts`, `TodayWhy.item` (`before`, `travel`) and `TodayWhy.promise` (`neededBefore`, `before`), `datedWhy`/`promiseWhy`, `datedPlace`/`datedOver`/`nextTodayChange` (`askUntil`), the backend's `datedRows`, `findLinks` (a confirming ask), `today-sentence.ts` `whenOf` (the "before" and travel words; agent A owns the column's layout), the web's `conversationEnd`, the i18n keys `whatMatters.before`, `beforeTomorrow`, `neededBefore`, `neededBeforeTomorrow`, `travel` (en, es), `instantIn` and `wallHolds` exported from `@terno/ai`.
- **For iteration 4's other parts.** Change… (v68) should clear `before` and `wall` on the user's own schedule and keep them in its Undo snapshot; one reply, one row (v66) also maps `followups` (`before_day` is v67's column).
- **Known limits.** A quote reworded beyond its naming words is still refused (Luna, `dated-213`, 1 in 5). "Before then" counts only when the words before it in its paragraph name the day. The travel form needs the model to name the place; a move of a travel item given in the reader's zone clears its wall. `routineAutoBill` and the estimate, wish and one-reply checks read English and Spanish words only. A stake bracket on an alert can read as an instruction (Haiku, `dated-099`, 1 in 4).
- **Tests run:** typecheck (ai, backend, core, store, db, idb, i18n, web), `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files; unit: all of `packages/ai/src`, `backend/dated.test.ts`, `dated-evidence.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-links.test.ts`, `followups.test.ts`, `promise-answers.test.ts`, `core/dated.test.ts`, `today-projection.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `web/lib/today-sentence.test.ts`, `web/lib/dated.test.ts`, `i18n.test.ts`, both stores' full conformance, `migrations.test.ts`, `migrations-shipped.test.ts`, `dated-checks.test.ts` (2,301 passed, 1 skipped); `migrations.perf.test.ts` passes with v67; the dated eval as above. Not run: e2e, `pnpm test:all`, the Today perf gate (no new read on a hot path: `withEventEnds` copies nothing when no ask confirms an event), the Codex code review.


#### How iteration 4's one reply, one row was built

Design point 3 with the Codex review's revision 4, and follow-ups prompt v12, from `460a4acc1`, one commit each (`48914f2c4`, `a929bc524`, `3f9383cb4`, the eval `c7cf1a555`, the prompt `ed73e593c`, `b3e5f7b9b`, the gate `52312089c`). Migration v66.

**One reply, one row (presentation, not identity).** `replyKey` (core): the message and the person (their `addressKey`, else their name; none with neither, so a reply to several people never folds). `placeItems` groups the placed items a promise leads (`byReply`) where they're placed, before the count leaves Coming up out, so the list and the count agree. The lead is the first dated promise that shows, in the order the reply said them (`item`), else the first that shows; it keeps its own place, row id (its item's canonical key: stable while it's open, and an act on another member from the reader leaves it as it was) and members. Every other promise of the group is its `with`, one not showing yet too (an undated promise before its day: Done on the row finishes it, so it never comes back alone). An item an event or a request leads (attendance, an errand read twice) is that item's row, never grouped. `samePromise` is unchanged: each member stays its own item, with its own duplicates and its own `answers`. Only a mailbox with two promises from one message pays for it (`sharesMessage`). The row says the others as Also lines (`TodayItem.also` is now a list: a return leg's one line, or a reply's others; the feed joins them with " · ") and names them for its acts (`TodayWhy.with`: each other item's lead at the revision shown). Its Done, Remove and Later go through `followups.act` with `with` (at most `FOLLOWUP_REPLY_MAX`, 10) into `TodayService.act` as named items: each expanded through its duplicates, and on Done the requests each answers, in one store act, revision-checked, all or none; one Undo puts every one back. `why.members` stays the lead's own item, so the reader's target is the lead and each other promise stays a line of its own there (acted on alone, which leaves the row and drops that Also line).

**A holding promise (revision 4).** Migration **v66**, shape only: `followups.holds` (the request's id), no index (it's read with the open promises), no backfill; IndexedDB reads an absent field as none. `checkSentMail` keeps a "holding" verdict whose words are a kept promise that only holds (`onlyHolds`), and turns a "promise" or "fulfilled" verdict on such words into "holding" (iteration 3 dropped both); a "holding" whose promise also sends or signs something links nothing. Both stores' `finishFollowups` store it on that promise, once, under a link's rules (yours for certain, the request open, of the conversation, from before, at the revision shown; a promise that answers holds nothing, and the reverse). A promise that holds is never a row nor a reply's Also line (`placeItems` leaves it out) and never a line in the reader; the request is the row. It ends with the request: `FollowupWorker.retire` reads the requests promises hold in its one batch and dismisses a hold whose request is closed or gone (`HELD_REQUEST_CLOSED`, "held"), which has no history line of its own (`historyPage`).

**What you'll bring, or who comes with you (attendance, apart).** `attendsEvent` takes "bring … for/to the <event>" (`BRINGS`, en and es: bring, traer, llevar): the event its words after "for" name, else the conversation's only event; what it hands over besides the bring is still its own (`DELIVERS` on the rest). It takes a promise with no day too, among the conversation's open events (`findLinks`' `eventsOf`); a dated one still only its day's. Jordan's "I'll bring our CSM, Nina, and our solutions engineer for the Memphis conversation" joins the QBR (the event leads); his agenda, from the same reply, stays its own row.

**Prompt v12 (follow-ups), eval first.** `followups.real.ts` gained ten cases (`f12-01` to `f12-10`, fictionalized from round 12, en and es: Diego's two sentences, Priya's call and the flexibility she'll come with, Jordan's people and agenda, María's cupcakes "Friday morning at drop-off", Margaret's questions and her calendar with the request it holds) and one scorer for v11 and v12 (a time no later than the morning's noon, `wordsNot` on every promise, a holding verdict expected where it's listed first). On v11 (one reading each): both models split Diego (en, es) and Priya, and gave the cupcakes no time or 8:00. The words: one promise for each thing they'll do, "how they'll do it, or what they'll bring to it, is part of that thing"; a sentence or clause about how or with what is part of the promise it serves, with an example in each language that isn't an eval case, and "ask whether they'd ever do it apart"; who comes along to a meeting that's set is no promise; a morning they name is over by noon ("by 12:00 PM", "antes de las 12:00" in the text, which `statedClock` reads as its time); a holding verdict's evidence is its promise's quote; "your" ("tu"), never "my" ("mi"). On the way: "bringing what someone asked for is a promise" made both models take the coach's snacks as one (f11-18, f11-19; 4 in 10 on Luna), so it went; the morning rule, wherever it stood, made Haiku read "next week" sent on a Monday as the Sunday after next (f11-15, up to 5 in 5; 0 in 10 without it), so the "next week" rule has a second worked date. Backstop (`yourWords`): a promise's "my", "mi", "mis" are "your", "tu", "tus" ("para mí" and "5 mi" stay), as Haiku still wrote "send Asha my script" about one reading in ten.

**The final prompt, five readings each:** Luna v12 50 of 50, v11 115 of 115, none wrong; its base round all right. Haiku v12 48 of 50 with **2 wrong shown** (Priya's "come to the call with flexibility on the equity" as a second promise, 2 in 5: the app shows it as the call's Also line on one row, not a row of its own), v11 114 of 115 with **1 wrong** ("next week" as the Sunday after next, 1 in 5); its base round: one missed date ("Next Tuesday I'll book the room" undated) and one reply not found. The bar (none wrong shown) holds on Luna, not on Haiku. The ledger stood at $41.13 of $55 (from $38.48; other agents' evals ran meanwhile). A small window now needs a little over 3,300 tokens (v11 2,900).

**Tests.** Core: the grouping (Diego's dated lead with the undated other as its Also and `with`, never a member; the counts; Priya's first said leads; another person, message or no one named apart; a name as the key; an event's member not grouped; a lead behind Coming up in the list and the count; an undated other not showing yet; one closed, the other its own row; a duplicate under its item; a hold never a row nor an Also). Backend through the RPC (`promise-answers.test.ts`): Done on a reply's row finishing both promises and the request, Undo all; a stale member failing the act with nothing changed; the reader's act on one leaving the row; Margaret's hold stored with the request, no row even when due, ended with the request with no line of its own; `checkSentMail`'s holding verdicts and `yourWords`. `today-links.test.ts`: the bring cases (Jordan, two events, a dated bring, what's sent besides, another conversation, a bring with no event, Spanish, an undated promise to attend). Web: a reply row's Also lines and `with` (`state/today.test.tsx`), the reader's lines for a reply and a hold (`reader-items.test.ts`). Both stores' conformance: a hold stored once, never from another client's send or at another revision, never on a promise that answers, never linked as answering. Migrations: v66 (shape, default), v61's and v62's expectations with the column, the shipped hash. The prompt's words, version and snapshot.

**Ran:** typecheck (all packages); `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files; both stores' whole conformance suites with the db migration tests (1,480 passed, 1 skipped); 903 tests across 33 other named files (core projection, web Today state, reader, task; backend today, promise answers, follow-ups, dated, links, history, model stub; the whole `packages/ai`). **The Today perf gate**, interleaved with the grouping on and off (`grouping = false`), two rounds each, load 1.7–7.8: no difference. SQLite spread list p95 17.9–18.6 ms off, 18.1–18.3 on; all showing 15.8–17.0 off, 16.6–16.7 on. IndexedDB passes every scenario either way (linked list 17.3–18.3 off, 16.4–17.5 on). SQLite fails both ways on the scenarios iteration 3 already named: Needs you's longest turn (61.0, 62.0 off; 59.0, 56.5 on) and Coming up far's (71.0, 71.2 on; a run stops at its first failure). No e2e (no flow's screens changed: the Also line is the existing one). No Codex code review (not asked of this part).

**Known limits.** A promise that brings or attends is the event's member: its words show nowhere on the event's row (the reader's line and the mail say them). Done on a reply's row writes a history line for each of its items, not one; a reply's row held in place (its items closed by mail) doesn't hide its others' lines. A hold ends at the next `retire` (daily, or Today's clock), hidden meanwhile, and stays on follow-ups' own surfaces (the Inbox's top) like any promise. No grouping without a person. The panel harness's `packet-feed.py` and `score-items.py` read a row's `also` as one object; it's a list now (`feed.py` reads both).

**Shared code touched outside point 3:** `placeItems`/`projectRows` (core: the grouping step, `also` as a list), `TodayItem.also`/`ProjectedRow.also` (a list; the feed's one line in `state/today.ts`), `TodayWhy.with`, the `followups.act` contract and handler, `TodayTask`/`actOnTask` (web), `FollowupFinish.verdicts` (store), `historyPage` (one line), `FollowupWorker.retire`, `readerItems` (one line), `what-matters-panel.real.ts` (`also` as a list), the Today perf gate's one assertion.


##### Merging iteration 4's parts

The six parts merged onto `t3/what-matters-204` (`373783f3e`) left 12 unit failures and five seams between parts. Each failure was judged against the design above, never by what the code does now.

**The failures.**
- *`migrations.test.ts`, v65 to v70 (test).* Each part's test migrated from v64 and expected only its own columns; migrating to today's schema adds every later part's too. The tests now start from the version just before their own and read the columns from one table of what v65 to v70 add (`ADDED`, `addedSince`), which the older tests (v57 to v63) share.
- *`capture-repair-upgrade.int.test.ts`, SQL35 (test).* Its fixture built SQL35 by dropping every later addition from today's schema by hand, so each new column broke it (v64's, then v70's `drafts.handoff_json`). It's now the exact v35 install from its own migrations, holding the same rows (`writeLegacyCopy`, as the migration tests build theirs).
- *`dated.test.ts`, Today's order (test).* "Inside a group … Today's, the timed by time, then the untimed" and "the count agrees with the list across mixed kinds" expected iteration 1's Today; revision 8 and the placement part's decision put the whole day's rows first (by tier: a task before a plan), then the timed by their time whatever their kind. "A thread with two items the same day shows the timed one" took the first row as the timed one; since iteration 2 each item is its own row, so it now checks both rows, the timed one with its time. The feed's iteration 1 e2e order test changed the same way.
- *`dated.test.ts`, the nudge-only conversation (test).* It expected "to do today" to leave out your reminder due today; the placement part counts it, as design point 1 asks ("today" counts what's under Today).
- *`dated.test.ts` and `promise-answers.test.ts`, a broken promise (test).* Both expected the promise under its live day; the owner's "Today, Waiting on you" (revision 8) puts a promise broken with a live day ahead first in Today's Waiting on you, counted once as waiting. The tests now say so, header counts included (`promise-answers`' section key gained the part, so a person's request there reads `today:waiting` too).

**The seams.**
- *Change… and the reading (code).* A change of the user's left v67's `before_day` and `wall` on the item, so the row could say "before Mon" or "4:00 PM Austin" for a day the user chose. They're the mail's words about its day: a change takes them away, its Undo snapshot keeps them, and a later mail's move kept as a proposal carries them (`ScheduleProposal.before`, `wall`; `proposalOf` in the store), so Use takes the move exactly. One reading of a stored wall clock (`readWall`, core) serves both stores and the proposal. Conformance in both stores; `scheduleChanges` tests.
- *Hand to… and one reply, one row (code).* `today.handoff` took the named item only, so handing a reply's row left its Also promises open. By the design (the row's act covers its members, as its Done does) the row's Hand to… names its `with` too (`today.handoff`'s `with`, each at the revision shown), so the confirmed forward closes all or none; one changed since is stale. From the reader's line a promise is handed alone, as it's done alone there.
- *Mute and one reply, one row (coherent; a test).* Mute hides a sender's or kind's dated items and never your promises; a reply's group is promises only. A muted sender's request a grouped promise answers is hidden, not closed; the row stays with its Also line, and its Done still settles the request, as before the mute. A backend test holds it.
- *The reader's line for a broken promise (code).* The reader said "Due Sunday" (and sorted it as Missed when its own day was over) while the row said "You said Tue 20 (missed) · needed Sun". The reader now reads each promise with the request it answers (`withNeeds`) and the day it sits under (`promiseDay`), and says the days as the row does (`promiseDayWords`, one function for the column and the line): "You said Tue 20 (missed) · needed Sun · You told Jess you'd send the script", "Due tomorrow · needed Sat · …".
- *The column's wrap in Spanish (code).* "· lo necesitan el dom" could wrap after the dot. A no-break space after the dot in the five `whatMatters.needed*` strings (en and es) keeps it with the words after it; a line breaks before the dot.
- *Esc with a popover just opened (code; found by `what-matters-change.spec.ts`, failing the same on the merged tree before these fixes).* Opening the reader's Change… and pressing Esc before focus had moved into the popover closed it and also put away the conversation: Base UI's dismiss and the app's keyboard both listen on the document. The app's Esc now yields to an open popover or menu (`popupOpen`, the composer's own test).

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (all packages): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean. Unit (`packages/db/src packages/idb/src packages/store/src packages/backend/src packages/core/src packages/ai/src packages/i18n apps/web/src`): 642 files, 10,354 passed, 2 skipped. E2E (`E2E_PORT=5561`; `today`, `what-matters-feed`, `what-matters-change`, `what-matters-mute`, `hand-to`, `followups`, `compose`, `plain-actions`): 119 of 120 passed; `today.spec.ts:662` failed once on the first crowded pass (load 10) and passed alone, as the Change part found on the base. *Still flaky:* `what-matters-feed.spec.ts:212` fails alone in one of two scenarios that follow a Done and its z (focus not where the next key expects it: after Enter on "Wire payroll" the keys were on "Pay the rent", the row the z brought back), about one run in three on this tree and four in six on the merged tree before these fixes, so it predates them; it needs its own look at where the keys go when an Undo lands late. Not run: `pnpm test:all`, the perf gates, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review (not asked).


##### What the code review changed (iteration 4's app)

Codex's code review of the merged iteration 4 (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it4.out`) raised eleven points. Points 1, 10 and 11 and a phishing regression are the reading's (`packages/ai/src/dated.ts`, the backend's AutoPay rule) and are fixed apart; the seven below are the app's, each with a test that fails without its fix (run against the reverted code before committing).

- *2, Change and its own Undo (P1).* At noon, tomorrow's open promise changed to today with no time had its 9:00 already past but stayed `open`; follow-ups' look right after made it due (another revision), so the change's Undo was always refused. `scheduleChanges` now says `due` for a moment already reached, and both stores write the Inbox top `makeDue` would (`scheduledInbox`: on top unless it's a scan's; one due already keeps its own), so the next look finds nothing to do. Tests: `scheduleChanges` (an open promise to a past 9:00 is due), the RPC (Undo after that change succeeds), both stores' conformance (the change is due and on top, `makeDue` then moves nothing, Undo exact; a due promise keeps its `seen`).
- *3, Unmute and mail from while muted.* Unmute deleted the only record, so a mark still queued while the sender was muted was read after Unmute, and the 14-day check counted (and would read) mail consumed unread while muted; the backend test expected the latter. Unmute now keeps when each sender was unmuted (`whatMatters:unmutedAt` in the store's small values, the newest 500, written before the mute row goes so the reader never sees the sender unmuted without it), and both the live path (`consider`) and the check (`scanPicks`) leave a message unread when its sender is muted or it's dated before their Unmute (`silenceOf`, `silenced` in `dated-mutes.ts`). The old test now expects the check's count unchanged by Unmute; a new one holds a sender's mark in the queue behind another reading, mutes and unmutes them, and the model is never asked. *Choice:* the boundary is the message's own date, as `enabledAt`'s rule; a message dated just before the Unmute that arrives after it isn't read (its sender's next mail is).
- *4, a second Hand to… into one forward.* The forward kept both lines but only the second hand-off, so the confirmed send closed one item. A hand-off is now one or more rows (`HandoffItem.row`, absent for the first; `parseHandoff` requires each row to name its item, the RPC schema takes it): `mergeHandoffs` (core) adds the new row to the draft's, the same row handed again replacing its old resolution and an item in both kept once as now resolved; past the 32-item bound it's refused in words ("This forward already hands on as much as one can. Send it first, then hand this on.", es "Este reenvío ya delega todo lo que cabe en uno…") and nothing changes. `handoffCloses` gates each row by its own named item, so one row put off since stays open while the other closes. Tests: core `handoff.test.ts`, `hand-to.test.ts` (two lines, two rows; the bound refused), both stores' conformance (two rows, one put off: the other closes; a row without its own item is no hand-off).
- *5, the forward's wait with a stale item.* Both stores returned before seeding the forward's awaiting row when none of its items could close (a Change, Later or settlement between Hand to… and the send). The wait is now seeded whatever closed; `handed` still says only whether items closed. Conformance (both stores): "a change since wins" now also expects the wait.
- *6, muted events and "Reply C to confirm".* The list's cache and the reader's line hid muted items before `withEventEnds`, so an ask whose event was muted got `eventEnd` 0 and vanished. Both now find each ask's event among every open item first, then hide what's muted (and what the suspicious-sender guard hides). Backend test: events muted, the dentist's confirm ask still shows in the list and on the reader's line with its window ahead.
- *7, IndexedDB and a muted sender's acknowledgments.* IndexedDB's Needs you walk chose which "Thanks…" bodies to read ahead before leaving muted senders out, so their bodies were read on Today's path (SQL's walk filters first). The mute filter moved into `needsWalk`, before `acks` is built. Test (`needs-you-bodies.test.ts`): a muted sender's acknowledgment body stays unread after a reopen.
- *8, Esc's late focus (the cause of `what-matters-feed.spec.ts:212`'s flake).* `closeFeedConversation` queued two frames that put the keys back on the row put away, whatever happened meanwhile: Esc from the rent, the keys on payroll before the frames, and they went back to the rent (Enter opened it). The frames now land only while nothing moved since: no later put-away (a token), no feed selection (`moves`), nothing open, and the keys nowhere or where they were at Esc; `focusFeedItem` takes the same check for its extra frame. The spec waits for each Undo to land whole: the snack dock says `data-undoing` while one is on its way. Tests: `feed-pane.test.ts` (the keys moved before the frames stay; a row opened meanwhile isn't overridden). Ten runs of `:212` (`--repeat-each=10`): 10 passed; the same ten with the guard reverted (the spec's new wait kept): 2 failed, in "Tab to an item, Enter opens it" (the keys not on payroll).
- *9, Undo and the reader's opening.* A Done's Undo restored the row with `setAppOpened` and left `carried` on the successor, so click A, Done, Undo, Done put the reader away instead of going on to B. The act now snapshots whether the open mail was the user's or the app's and `carried`, and Undo restores both. Test (`today-task.test.ts`): click A, Done, Undo, Done goes on to B with its mail; one the app opened comes back as the app's.

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (all packages): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean. Unit: both stores' whole conformance, `idb/needs-you-bodies`, core `handoff`, backend `today-schedule`, `dated`, `hand-to`, `today`, `today-history`, `promise-answers`, `followups`, `architecture`, web `hand-to`, `today-task`, `feed-pane`, `feed-focus`, `state/today-held`, `change`, i18n, the designs' source scan: 1,851 passed, 1 skipped. E2E (`E2E_PORT=5571`, load 4 to 6): `what-matters-feed`, `what-matters-change`, `what-matters-mute`, `hand-to`, `today` (every design): 31 of 31 passed, twice; `what-matters-feed.spec.ts:212` ten times: 10 passed. Not run: `pnpm test:all`, the perf gates (no read was added to the list's path: the mutes' order changed, and IndexedDB's walk filters earlier, not more), `pnpm ui:rate`.


##### What the code review changed (iteration 4's reading)

Codex's review of iteration 4's reading found three defects (an AutoPay check that ate real bills, a travel instant lost in the reader's repeated hour, "before November 20" without its boundary), and round 13 of the user panel found two regressions of prompt v14's. Each fix has a test that fails without it; the eval cases are fictionalized from the panel's mail.

- **Phishing shown as alerts (P0, a regression of v14's).** v14's "a security or fraud notice that asks the reader to reply, click, sign in or verify stays an alert, never that instruction" turned phishing into alerts: round 13 stored Theo's "Third copyright strike on Theo Marsh channel" (no-reply@yt-partner-review.com, "your channel will be PERMANENTLY TERMINATED in 24 hours unless you verify your identity") and Priya's "Lumora mailbox password expires; account suspension…" (it-helpdesk@lumora-io.co, "Failure to validate within 12 hours will result in your account being suspended"); round 12 (v13) had none. On the eval, v14 showed them 15 of 15 times on Haiku and 5 of 15 on Luna. Fixed in three layers:
  - *Prompt v15.* A scam or a suspicious message is no item at all, not even an alert: links or an address on a domain that isn't the brand or organization it names or is made to look like it, a loss threatened within hours, a demand to verify, validate or confirm an account, a password or an identity, to sign in or to click. A security notice from the reader's own provider that only reports what happened stays an alert, never an instruction. The model sees the sender's name only, never the address (`DatedInput`), so the domain is read from the mail's links.
  - *A local backstop (`scamPressure`, `dated-evidence.ts`).* An alert, a request, an expiry or an ask whose words (the subject and the paragraphs tied to it) ask to verify, validate or confirm an account, a password or an identity, to sign in or to click, or threaten a deletion, suspension, termination or block still to come, within a count of hours, is no item, en and es. A bill, a booking and a parcel keep their own checks (a utility's "suspended in 48 hours unless payment is received" stays a bill); a report of what happened ("we froze your card after a $412.18 charge") and a deadline given as a day ("by 1:00 PM on Wednesday") stay.
  - *The sender guard (`suspicious-sender.ts`).* A site named like one of the reader's accounts' sites with a hyphenated word (`impersonates`: lumora-io.co to priya@lumora.io) is never read, before a model is reached; a hyphenated site with a helpdesk's or a credential's word (helpdesk, validate, appeal, recovery, password; validar, validación, recuperación, contraseña) is a security desk's. Whoever the user has written to stays trusted. Theo's yt-partner-review.com names no word of either kind: the prompt and the backstop catch it.
- **Sam's saved abstract dropped (a regression of v14's).** "Your abstract draft … is saved but has NOT been submitted. Abstract submission closes 20/11/2026 at 23:59 CET": round 12 (v13) kept it as a respond due Nov 20, round 13 (v14) returned nothing. No one v14 sentence dropped it: v13 itself missed it 2 of 8 times on each model, v14 3 of 5 (Luna) and 2 of 5 (Haiku); a congress's mail reads as a list's optional call ("submit your story", "register by the 20th"). v15 says what the reader already started is theirs: their own draft, application or registration saved or incomplete but not submitted, with the day it closes, is a respond due then, its quote the words with that day, even from a list or a no-reply address ("Submit the Brightwater Fellowship application"); the newsletter line points to it. A first wording quoted the panel's own sentences and Haiku copied them (the quote without the day, the zone lost, 4 of 5); the second gives the rule without the mail's words.
- **Real bills taken for AutoPay (P1, Codex).** `routineAutoBill` took a bare "we will charge" as an automatic payment: "Your invoice is due November 20. We will charge a $25 late fee if payment is not received" lost its row. It now reads sentence by sentence and counts only an unconditional charge of the bill itself: "we will charge" with what it's charged to ("we'll charge your Visa"), and no sentence with a fee, a penalty, interest or a condition ("if", "unless", "si", "recargo").
- **Travel in the reader's repeated hour (P2, Codex).** The reader converted the mail's time to the reader's day and time and then rebuilt the instant from those, so in the hour the reader's clock shows twice the first was taken: for a reader in Los Angeles, New York's 4:30 AM on November 1, 2026 (the second 1:30 there) became 3:30 AM in New York. `converted` now carries the instant read on the mail's clock (`at`) for items and moves (dropped when "before" moves the day); the mail's own clock is read from it, and `checkDates` stores it as the row's moment (`atInstant`) when the reader's day and time name it. The backend test sets the reader's zone (`TZ`, as the date tests do). Not changed: `promise-time.ts`'s stated clock of a promise still rebuilds from the reader's day and time.
- **"Before November 20" (P2, Codex).** `beforeOf` read weekdays and bare day numbers only. It now reads a named calendar date, month first or after ("before November 20", "before Nov. 20th", "before 20 November", "antes del 20 de noviembre"), and checks the month, so "antes del 20 de diciembre" no longer counts as November 20's boundary (it did through the bare day number); a bare "may" after a day stays a verb.
- **Small windows** need a little under 8,800 tokens for v15 (v14 8,400): the dated test's window is 8,800.
- **The eval** (`dated.real.ts`, 228 cases, 6 new, `dated-223` to `dated-228`): Theo's copyright strike, Priya's password (en), a Spanish bank's "validar tu identidad en las próximas 12 horas", a genuine frozen debit card from the bank's own domain (the counter-case), Sam's abstract (en, CET read in New York; es, Lima read in Madrid). `dated-073` (a bank phishing mail) now expects nothing, where it allowed an alert. After the full round, `dated-010` ("Actualiza tu método de pago antes del 9 de marzo") accepts the step as a respond, as v14's alert rule says (v15 gives it 11 of 12 times, v14 6 of 12), and the abstract cases accept the draft called a draft.
- **Five readings each** of the new cases and the alert cases beside them (`dated-039`, `073`, `074`, `099`), both models, the app in English and in Spanish: 30 of 30 found and none wrong shown in each of the four; the phishing (`073`, `223`–`225`) never shown, 80 of 80, and the model gave none of them an item at all (the backstop had nothing to catch); the genuine alerts found every time; the abstract's time right 20 of 20 in each language. After the re-keying, `010`, `227` and `228` five more times each: 57 of 60 clean (one invented quote, two texts naming the draft by its title only).
- **One full round, all 228 cases** (165 expected items): Luna English 162 of 165 found, 1 wrong shown (`dated-062`'s "out for delivery today" dated the 16th, as in iteration 4's round); Luna Spanish 162, 2 (`dated-020`'s customs scam as a 2,99 € payment, `dated-221`'s "before then" as an undated ask); Haiku English 165 of 165, none; Haiku Spanish 162, 2 (`dated-039`'s "Reply YES" as a respond, `dated-131`'s "by then" a day early). Re-read three times on each model and language, v14 against v15: `020`, `039` and `131` none wrong on either (0 of 12 each), `221` 1 of 12 on v15 and 0 on v14. No settlement, move or replacement wrong, no alert unsafe, befores and walls all right, no text cut inside a word. The eval's own bars failed in three of the four runs: wrong words over 1 (Luna English 3, Luna Spanish 6, Haiku Spanish 3, mostly a pattern the text misses, such as a card's "11/26", "Folio", "Laura"), and wrong shown over 1 in both Spanish runs. The round cost about $1, the whole review's evals about $1.65; the shared ledger stood at $43.06 of $55.
- **Tests run:** `pnpm typecheck` (all packages), `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files; unit: `packages/ai/src`, `backend/dated.test.ts`, `dated-evidence.test.ts`, `suspicious-sender.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `promise-time.test.ts`, `scripts/ai-real/dated-checks.test.ts` (596 passed), the prompt snapshot updated; each commit of the series typechecked and its touched unit files run. Not run: e2e (no flow changed), the perf gates, `pnpm test:all`, a Codex review of these fixes.
- **Known limits.** The read guards in Today and the history don't pass the reader's addresses, so a lookalike sender's item stored before this keeps showing until it's done. `scamPressure` reads English and Spanish, and only a count of hours ("within 24 hours"), never "today" or "immediately". A scam that asks for money as a bill ("pay 2,99 € before tomorrow") is still the prompt's alone.

### Round 13 and iteration 5's design

Round 13 (iteration 4, medians of two reviewers): Look 8.1, Missed 7.9, Content 7.6, Deadlines 7.4, Acting 7.4, Needs you 7.3, would use daily 6.9, Plans 6.8, Coming up 6.6; 19 cells of 63 at 8+ (round 12: 21). The reading improved (120 of 131 expected items visible by default; no trap, no phishing after v15, nothing settled shown open) and the scores didn't follow: iteration 4's six parallel parts fixed most of round 12's complaints and brought about eleven new visible faults where they met (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/panel/fable-analysis-r13.md`, checked against the stored readings): the return leg twice on two clocks (its own row in the place's time, the outbound's Also line in the reader's); Coming up as the last heading below far rows shown early, so it opens on earlier dates; a broken promise needed *today* moved from Today's top into Waiting on you; a promise for "Friday morning" shown "by 12:00 PM"; a stake written as a fact ("(parcel removed from plan)"); Remove's offer "Don't show events" or a person's name; the palette's "Remind me… (later)" above Later; the reader's line cramped by five controls. Prompt v14 also let two phishing mails through as alerts mid-iteration (fixed in v15 with a local backstop and a lookalike-domain guard, before reviewers ran).

The owner's answers (2026-11-16): repairs only, then stop. The far rows shown early go under one undated **"Ahead"** heading after the seven days, **Coming up last** after it ("'Ahead' heading, then Coming up"); Today puts **things to do before what happens to you**; a request due more than seven days ahead sits **under its own day**, not in Waiting on you; the header says a broken promise with a day ahead: **"… · 5 waiting, 1 overdue"**; the mute offer is **a sender-and-kind pair, never a person you've written to**; a **second look** at a dated sentence that got no item; **relayed asks** (a named person asking the reader by name through a service). And: "for things that are auto-renewed like a plan or an invoce that was paid already, those things should not even be on the feed, nobody cares": **a plan or subscription that renews automatically, and an invoice already paid, get no item at all**; what still needs the reader stays (auto-renew off or lapsing, a failed charge, an expired card). After round 14, **the loop on the feed stops**: the bar becomes a ledger of verified claims (zero verified wrong or missing items per persona) and a second-morning capture for "would use daily"; "order it by what matters to me" belongs to the preferences plans (205–207).

#### Iteration 5

**The panel.** The walkthrough removes a delivery or a renewal (never an invitation or a document the persona wants), and shoots Change…'s form and Hand to…'s forward; the brief's step 3 names Coming up's new place; a pre-panel check script fails on any persona whose feed has one item at two clock times, a row that is also an Also line, a dated heading above an earlier date, the same words open and in Earlier, a promise whose words hold a clock time its quote doesn't, a bracket without a condition, or a trap; the round's reading is captured twice and the flips reported beside the scores, and the reviewers' same-way/opposite count with them.

**1. Regressions.** A detail with a row of its own isn't also its parent's Also line, and every travel time is the place's clock where it has one. A broken promise whose live day is today is Today's own first row (round 12's), counted in "to do today"; one whose day is ahead stays first in Waiting on you, the header adding "1 overdue". A promise for a part of the day keeps that part ("this morning", "tomorrow morning"; missed after noon; never "by 12:00 PM" in its words), as does a request ("by tomorrow morning"). A stake is what happens if it isn't done ("(or the parcel leaves the plan)", "(policy ends Dec 1 if unpaid)"), and the app drops a bracket on a pay, renew, expire or respond that holds no condition and no amount. The palette, with an item selected, leads with its Done, Later, Change…, Hand to… and Remove under the item's words; "Remind me…" sits with the email's actions and never offers a time past the item's moment. The reader's line puts the sentence on its own line and the controls under it.

**2. Placement.** Today: what to do first (timed by time, then the untimed), then what happens (plans, deliveries, by time). A request due more than seven days ahead is its day's (or Ahead's), not Waiting on you's. Far rows shown early go under "Ahead" (es "Para ir resolviendo"), each row's date in its column; Coming up follows, last, closed. An attendance promise is its event's Also line ("You said you'd bring Nina and the SE"). Two questions from one sender in one mail stay two unless the ask's quote is in the request's own sentence or list.

**3. What needs no row.** A plan, subscription or membership that renews automatically, and an invoice the mail (or a later one) says is paid, are no items (prompt and a local check); a renewal that lapses unless the reader acts, a failed charge, an expired card stay.

**4. The reading.** Prompt v16 (eval first, five readings both models, en and es): the stake as a condition; a part of the day; relayed asks (a named person asking the reader by name, forwarded by a service: "Owen requested your review on #812"; never a social notification's generic call); an event's end day ("Thu–Fri"); a hotel's check-in; v15's three lost items checked against v14 (V1). Local backstops: an event whose quote states one start time takes it when the model gave none; a delivery of a thing already open in its conversation moves it; Earlier says "Cancelled" or "Moved" for an event a mail cancelled or moved. **A second look**: a sentence of a read mail that holds a day and a time or a closing and that no returned item quotes is asked about once more, alone (bounded per mail and measured; its cost in the plan).

**5. Mute's offer.** After Remove: "Don't show <kind> from <sender>" (a pair; the mutes table takes pair keys, shape only), and a sender alone only for an automated address, never someone the user has written to; whole kinds stay in Settings.

**Small things.** Change… and Hand to… get keys and a hint each in the menu ("sets its own day", "forwards it to someone"); "before the 19th" on a renewal takes the `before` relation; Settings says in one sentence what the provider keeps and for how long.

**Tests and perf** as before; the Today gate against 60 ms.

**Out of scope:** a "Start here" order by stakes (the preferences plans); bills 30 days ahead (declined).

#### Revisions after the Codex review of iteration 5

Codex (read-only, `/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/critique-it5.out`) raised 10 points; 1 to 9 are taken. Point 10's first half (the combined-bounds scenario's 120 ms budget back to 60 ms, by slicing the waiting walk) is the owner's open decision and plan 145's work; its second half (shape-only storage, no backfills) is taken. Migrations: part of the day v71, mute pairs v72, the second look's attempt state v73.

1. **Suppression is affirmative and tied to the item.** Replace `routineAutoBill` and v15's priced-renewal line together: no item only for an affirmative, unconditional automatic renewal of this plan or a completed payment of this invoice, in its own words (not an unrelated AutoPay footer from `tiedWords`); negation, auto-renew off, lapse, a failed charge, an invalid card, a balance outstanding, or a trial that asks the reader to decide veto it. A later receipt settles through `tiedTo` and both stores' revision checks, never every invoice from its sender. Tests: mixed invoices, a partial payment, "we will charge a late fee unless paid".
2. **A pair mute is its own identity.** A normalized sender–kind key across the store contract, RPC, offer memory, Settings and Undo, in an empty companion table (v72) behind the mutes API (v69's CHECK and key untouched). `dated.written([senderKey])` is checked before any offer and again on accepting; a sender alone needs positive evidence it's automated. In both stores' `finishDated` a pair suppresses new items of that kind from that sender, letting settlements, moves and other kinds through; it never enters `mutedSenders`, never silences the message, never removes Needs you conversations. Parity tests: two kinds from one sender; a send between offer and accept.
3. **The second look is bounded and inside the worker.** In `DatedWorker.consider`, before the one `finish`: at most one extra call, at most two uncovered sentences and 1,000 characters, the same provider, signal and accounting, the item and output limits kept. A sentence is covered by any returned item's, settlement's or move's evidence, or a local rejection (receipts, AutoPay, scams), so nothing is asked twice; the attempt is recorded (v73) so a retry or restart doesn't loop. Spam, a muted sender and the settings are rechecked before it; the text already read is reused; no rescan. `nothingAsked` is never written while it's incomplete. Its measured cost is recorded in the plan.
4. **A part of the day is stored, not written.** `partOfDay` (morning, afternoon, evening) on promises and dated requests (v71, nullable, no backfill), `timed: false` unless the quote states a clock; the follow-ups prompt returns the part instead of a noon in its words, and `promiseMoment` no longer reads one there. One cutoff resolver (morning: noon) for placement, the broken-promise day and `nextTodayChange`; carried through both stores, the projection and the reader, moves, proposals and Change/Undo (Change clears it). Tests: 11:59→12:00 with no mail, an answered request with a later live day, Later, reopen.
5. **"Ahead" is a presentation group of its own.** Eligible open rows past the seven days go to an explicit `ahead` group, by their own dates, Coming up's nested buckets kept apart after it; `datedGroupAt`'s `shownEarly` branch stops sending far requests to Today; `rankOf`, `Heading` and `todayWhen` learn Ahead; row ids, closed Coming up out of j/k and `leaveComingUp`'s fallback (to Ahead's last row) kept. Tests: day +7/+8, a far request, collapsing with the reader open, Earlier's paging unchanged.
6. **"Needed today" and "needed ahead" resolved once.** From `promiseDay`: a live day today is first in Today, counted as today; ahead, first in Waiting, counted as waiting; `overdue` a subset of waiting in both projection paths; `TodayCounts` and the header line gain it, `todayBadge` stays missed + today + waiting. Tests: grouping, a non-live canonical with a live duplicate, Later, the midnight change from ahead to today.
7. **Today's order is one lexicographic key.** Broken-needed-today first; then things to do before things that happen; to-dos timed by instant then untimed; happenings timed by instant then untimed. Waiting keeps its own. Test: a 09:00 delivery, a 16:00 promise and an untimed payment.
8. **A relayed ask passes one local predicate.** A named human, a request to the reader by name, and a concrete action or object in the same sentence or a tight block, shared by `consider`, `askAllowed` and `scanPicks`; "Owen posted", "people you may know", "join the conversation" fail; the service is never marked a person. Prompt v16 drops "at most one ask a mail"; two questions from one sender stay two unless the ask's quote is in the request's own sentence or list.
9. **The stake is checked before the cut.** Item-specific evidence that the bracket is a condition ("or" as a condition, not "card or bank account"), en and es, negation, percentages; for pay, renew, expire and respond an unsupported bracket is dropped keeping the action and the amount, moved text too; the examples that teach the old phrasing ("deploys pause Mon") change; a long text's cut never keeps a consequence without its condition.

#### How iteration 5's part of the day was built

Design point 1's part of the day with the Codex review's revision 4, on a branch from `54dda80e7`, beside agents A (placement and the feed: Today's order, Ahead, overdue), B (the dated reading, v16), D (the second look, v73) and E (mute pairs, v72). Migration v71.

**One cutoff resolver (core).** `packages/core/src/part-of-day.ts`: `PartOfDay` (`morning`, `afternoon`, `evening`) and `dueCutoff(day, part)`, the moment an item due on a local day is over: a morning at 12:00, an afternoon at 18:00, an evening (and a whole day) at the next 0:00, by wall clock across DST. It's the only change in the placement functions agent A also edits, one input each: `datedPlace`'s Missed moment (a deadline's day end becomes its cutoff); `promiseDay` (its own day over at its own part's end; the request's live day at the request's part's end; `PromiseDay` gains `part`, the part of the day it sits under, and `neededPart`, the request's beside it); `effective` carries `part` into `promisePlace` (Missed at the cutoff), `promiseBroken` (a live day ends at its part) and `promiseOver`'s reading of the day; `nextTodayChange` wakes at a dated item's or a promise's cutoff (noon for a morning, with no mail in between). `missedUntil` (a week after the day's end) is unchanged. `DatedRow.partOfDay`, `FollowupRow.partOfDay`, `PromiseFacts.partOfDay` / `neededPart`; `withNeeds` carries a request's part (`neededPart`). The projection gives the row `why.item.part` (an untimed item) and `why.promise.part` / `neededPart` (`TodayWhy`), so the feed, the count and the clock read one rule.

**Stored, not written (v71).** `dated_items.part_of_day` and `followups.part_of_day`, nullable text, no index, no backfill (rows before it are the whole day; one that doesn't read as a part reads as none: `readPartOfDay`). IndexedDB: no version bump, absent reads as the whole day. Through both stores: a new row (`DatedWrite.partOfDay`, `FollowupWrite.partOfDay`), a move (`moved[].partOfDay`: the new day's part, or none, which clears it), a promise's move (`movedPromises[].partOfDay`), Change (the user's own day has none: it's cleared, like the day asked before and the mail's clock), its Undo (the snapshots keep it: `DatedScheduleSnapshot.partOfDay`, `PromiseScheduleSnapshot.partOfDay`), and a proposal (`ScheduleProposal.partOfDay`, so taking it takes the move's part; a move to the user's own day and moment that adds a part is a new schedule, kept as a proposal, not "the same"). 

**Promises: follow-ups prompt v13.** v12 wrote "by 12:00 PM" into a morning promise's words (María's cupcakes, Margaret's key and library), which the app then read as a clock. v13 returns `partOfDay` ("morning", "afternoon", "evening" or null) on each promise, with its day in `due`; the text never gets a clock for it; a clock they state is the time, no part; "this afternoon" and "esta tarde" are that day (beside "tonight"). The wire field is required and nullable, as every other; the UI rating's model stub and the backend tests' stubs answer null. The ladder's overhead allows for the schema's new field (1,800 characters); a small window now needs a little under 3,500 tokens (v12 3,300). `promiseMoment` (`packages/backend/src/promise-time.ts`) no longer reads a clock from the generated words alone: a clock counts only when the quote states one (`statesClock`: "4:00", "4 pm", "at 4", "noon", "a las 10", "mediodía"); a clock the words have and the quote doesn't is the reader's own, taken out of the words, and the quote's part (`partOfDayIn`) is what was meant (a backstop for v12's habit). The reader's part is kept only when the quote names it (`saysPart`) and states no clock, on a dated promise; it's stored untimed at the part's moment (`partMoment`: 9:00, 13:00, 18:00; a part already begun when it was sent, an hour before its end, so it's never due the moment it's sent; one already over, none).

**Requests: read locally.** Agent B edits `packages/ai/src/dated.ts` for v16, so the dated prompt is untouched: a request's part is read in `checkDates` from its quote (`partTiming`: a `respond` with a day whose quote names exactly one part and states no clock: "by saturday morning", "el sábado por la mañana", "this afternoon", "esta tarde", "tonight", "esta noche", "first thing"; "mañana" alone is tomorrow and "más tarde" later, never a part). Such a request is untimed whatever clock the reader gave it. A move of a request takes the part its evidence names; a same-moment move with new words keeps the row's. A promise's move (`movedPromise`) takes the part its person's words name ("Monday morning works for me") at that part's moment, and its broken day is read at its old part's cutoff (moved after Friday's noon, a Friday morning promise was broken). The changes there are one helper and one line in each of the row push, the move and the promise move.

**The words.** `partDay` (`apps/web/src/lib/when.ts`): "This morning", "This afternoon", "Tonight", "Tomorrow morning", "Tue morning", "Nov 21 afternoon" (es "Esta mañana", "Esta tarde", "Esta noche", "Mañana por la mañana", "mar por la mañana"). The column (`todayWhen`): the part for an untimed row; "by tomorrow morning" / "by Mon morning" for a request shown early (es "para mañana por la mañana", "para el lun por la mañana": `whatMatters.byNear` keeps "para el mañana" away); "Was due this morning" in Missed after its noon the same day ("Venció esta mañana"), the day's words after that; "Today · needed tomorrow morning" ("lo necesitan mañana por la mañana"). The reader's line (`datedWhen`, `promiseWhen`): "Due this morning", "Due tomorrow morning", "Due Saturday morning" (es "Vence el sábado por la mañana"), "Was due this morning". *Choice:* "by noon" is never made from a morning: it's said only when the mail says noon, which is then a stated clock (12:00, timed, as before). The web's own missed checks read the same cutoff (`conversationItem`'s end, the follow-ups line, the reader's promise line). en and es together.

**Tests.** Core (`dated.test.ts`, `today-projection.test.ts`): the cutoffs; María's Friday morning promise Today's at 11:59 and Missed at 12:00 with no mail, the clock waking at noon; Kai's Saturday morning request likewise; Later over noon keeping it from Missed until the Later ends; an answered request with a later live day (past its morning, under the request's day said missed; the request's own morning ending that live day); a move past its morning keeping the new day's part; `partMoment`; `partOfDayIn` en/es (never "mañana" alone, "más tarde", beside a clock, or two parts); the row's `part`, the promise's `neededPart`, both Missed at noon in the counts. Stores (both, conformance): a request's and a promise's part round-trip through a write, a move, a promise's move and a reopened store; Change clearing it, its Undo putting it back exactly, a later mail's same-day morning kept as a proposal with its part and taken with it; v71's migration test (shape, both tables) and its shipped hash. Backend: `promiseMoment` (a clock the quote states; v12's made-up noon out of the words with the morning kept; the part only when the quote names it, never beside a clock or without a day; tonight written at 10:00; this morning written after noon), `checkSentMail` storing the part with words that never gain a clock, `checkDates` (Kai's request untimed with its morning, a clock in the quote timed, a bill's "morning" no part, a move's new part and a plain day clearing it, María's promise moved after her noon broken with Monday morning, before noon not), `scheduleChanges` (Change clears, a proposal's part taken), and through the RPC (`today.test.ts`) a promise for this morning Today's at 11:59, Missed at 12:00 with no mail, the clock armed for noon. Web: the column and the reader's line in en and es, the reader's promise missed after its noon. The prompt's words, its frozen snapshot, the wire refusing anything but the three parts or none, the fake reading a part.

**The eval** (`scripts/ai-real/followups.real.ts`, "v13", cases `f13-01` to `f13-13` with `f12-07`/`f12-08` rescored for the part; fictionalized from round 13: María's cupcakes, Margaret's spare key "Saturday morning before the plumber comes" and her library morning before Wednesday's email (the part on Tuesday's promise only, never on Wednesday's), "tonight", "this afternoon", "first thing tomorrow", a clock beside a part, a promise that names none; en and es), scored on what `checkSentMail` keeps: a part on the wrong day or one never said, or a clock the words didn't state, is wrong; none where one was said is missed. Five readings each. The first reading on Haiku left "I'll read it this afternoon and send you my comments" undated (4 in 5): v13 names "this afternoon" and "esta tarde" as that day. **Final prompt:** Haiku 73 of 75 found, none wrong shown (Spanish "lo leo esta tarde y te mando mis comentarios" twice without the part, the afternoon read as the reading's); Luna 75 of 75, none wrong. 245 readings in all cost $0.12 (Haiku $0.08, Luna $0.04) on the shared ledger, which stood at $44.39 of $55 after them (other agents' evals ran in the same minutes).

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (every package): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean (`scripts/ui-rating/model-stub.mjs` has two warnings on lines this didn't touch). Unit: core's whole `src`, the whole `packages/ai`, the backend's `dated`, `dated-evidence`, `followups`, `hand-to`, `promise-answers`, `promise-time`, `today`, `today-history`, `today-links`, `today-schedule`, `model-stub` (1,720 tests); both stores' whole conformance, `db/migrations.test.ts`, `migrations-shipped.test.ts`, the web's `lib` and `state/today`, `packages/i18n` (3,412 tests): all pass. `migrations.perf.test.ts` passes with v71 (two `ADD COLUMN`s; load average 11 to 13). Not run: the Today perf gate (the load average was 11 to 13; what's added on its path is one cutoff where a day's end was read, the same cost), e2e (no flow changed; the words are unit-tested), screenshots, `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review (the series' owner runs it).

**Known limits.** Promises and requests stored before v71 keep v12's "by 12:00 PM" in their words and their noon (no backfill); a reading after it writes the part. A request's part is read locally, so only a `respond` whose quote names one part (en, es) has one; a bill, an event or a plan due "in the morning" is still the whole day. The v12 backstop reads the quote's part when the reader invents a clock, which on a quote naming two days' work ("Tuesday morning … Wednesday") could give Wednesday's promise the morning; the eval's library cases never did. A part already begun when the promise was made is due an hour before its end (or the moment before it, in its last hour). Spanish "lo leo esta tarde y te mando…" sometimes keeps no part on Haiku (missed, never wrong).

**Shared code touched outside the part of the day.** `datedPlace`, `promiseDay`, `promiseBroken`, `promisePlace`, `effective`, `nextTodayChange` (the cutoff input only; agent A's ordering is untouched); `PromiseDay` gains two fields (the core tests' whole-object expectations gained them); `TodayWhy.item` and `.promise` one or two optional fields; `withNeeds` one; `checkDates`'s signature, its row push, its move and its promise move one expression each (agent B's `packages/ai/src/dated.ts` untouched); `movedPromise` its broken check; both stores' row readers, `finishDated`'s insert and two move statements, `finishFollowups`' insert, `scheduleItems`/`unscheduleItems`, `proposedMove`'s "same schedule"; `ScheduleProposal`, `proposalOf`, the schedule snapshots; `scheduleChanges`' three writes; `followupWhen`, `conversationItem`'s end, the reader's promise missed check; the UI rating's model stub; the backend tests' follow-ups stubs (a promise with no part unless it says one); the migration tests' table of what each migration adds (agents D and E add v72 and v73 beside it).


#### How iteration 5's placement was built

On a branch from `54dda80e7`: design points 1 (the return leg, the broken promise today or ahead, the palette, the reader's line) and 2 (Today's order, far requests, Ahead, attendance's Also line) with the Codex review's revisions 5, 6 and 7, and the small things (Change…'s and Hand to…'s keys and menu notes; what the provider keeps). The reading (prompt v16, suppression, relay, stake: B), part of the day (v71: C), the second look (v73: D) and mute pairs (v72: E) are apart; no migration here.

**Today or ahead, resolved once (revision 6).** `promiseBroken(r, now)` in `packages/core/src/dated.ts` now says which: `"today"` when the live day `promiseDay` gives is today, `"ahead"` when it's later, null otherwise (Later, its live day over, a closed promise). `promiseGroupAt` still makes either Today's at any distance; the projection (`projectRows`, both paths: the list's and the count-only) marks the row `broken` with that value: `today` is Today's own (no part), counted in "to do today"; `ahead` is Waiting on you's, first, counted as waiting and as `overdue`, a subset of waiting (`TodayCounts.overdue`, new; `countsOf`). `todayBadge` is unchanged (missed + today + waiting), so overdue never adds to the badge. The header (`todayCountsLine`): "1 missed · 3 to do today · 5 waiting, 1 overdue" (es "… · 5 te esperan, 1 con retraso"; `today.line.overdue` takes the waiting part as `{waiting}` so the language orders it). The midnight that turns ahead into today is already a wake-up of the clock (`nextTodayChange` wakes at midnight while anything shows). A reply's grouped row and a duplicate shown for a canonical in Trash read the lead they show, in both paths.

**Today's order is one key (revision 7).** `byDayKey`'s branches are gone: `todayOrderKey(why, first)` in `packages/core/src/today.ts` is a lexicographic key, in turn: the broken promise needed today (`first`); what you do (deadlines, your promises, your reminders) before what happens to you (plans, deliveries); within each, what has a time by its instant (your reminder at its time), then the untimed by tier and when it came due. People waiting (Waiting on you's part) come after all of it and keep the order they came in. A day ahead, Ahead and Coming up use `dayOrderKey` (tier, then the timed by time of day, as before). `sortToday` and `sortInDay` sort by them, each key read once; `inDayOrder` (only tests used it) went. Revision 7's case: a 9:00 AM delivery, a 4:00 PM promise and an untimed payment are the promise, the payment, the delivery.

**Ahead (revision 5).** A new group, `ahead`, in `TODAY_GROUP_ORDER` between `day` and `comingUp` (`TaskPlace`'s `day` | `ahead` | `comingUp`). `datedGroupAt` and `promiseGroupAt` send a shown item whose day is past the seven days (after the seventh day after today, `lastShownDay`, one cached entry: the list places the 1,000-item bound on every read) to `ahead` with its own day; within them, its day's (`day`). The `shownEarly` branch sends a person's request to Today's people only within the seven days: a request due later is Ahead's like anything that far (Jordan's RFP and call no longer make "6 waiting"), so Waiting on you's "due later" band went from `ordered()`. Ahead is never counted (`todayCounted`), has no buckets (a row's `bucket` isn't set for it), and is ordered by its rows' own days (`sortInDay`). In the feed it's one `h2`, "Ahead" (es "Para ir resolviendo"), after the days and before the automated section's row and Coming up; each row's column is its date ("Nov 24"), quiet (`todayWhen`'s `later` tone). `rankOf` ranks it after the days and before Coming up's buckets; ids, j/k (Coming up closed is still out of the rows) and `leaveComingUp`'s fallback are as they were, which now lands on Ahead's last row.

**The return leg (R1).** `placeItems` no longer says a detail as its parent's Also line: a detail always has a row of its own while it shows (iteration 4's quiet row under its day, Ahead or Coming up), so the line only said the same leg twice, on two clocks. The detail machinery in `placeItems` went (the fast path no longer leaves for `detailOf`). Every travel time is the place's clock where it has one: `datedWhen` (the reader's line, its Also lines, a row's screen-reader words) reads the stored wall (`DatedRow.wall`) or Today's `item.travel` and says "Monday 4:00 PM Austin" (es "Lunes 16:00 en Austin", `placeClock`, shared with the feed's column), never the reader's zone for a place's time.

**What you said you'd bring (R9).** A promise to attend or bring someone is the event's member (`attendsEvent`); on that event's row its words are now the row's Also line, flagged `attends` (`TodayAlso.attends`): "You said you'd bring Nina and the SE" (es "Dijiste que ibas a …", `whatMatters.also.attends`, no full stop: a line, not a sentence). Only an event leads such a line; an errand read as a bill and as your promise (the same thing twice) says nothing more.

**The palette with an item selected (R7).** A palette group of its own, `item`, first at the root (before the email's), headed by the item's words (the feed row's sentence; the reader's chosen item in the same words), holding Done, Later…, Change day and time…, Hand to… and Remove from What matters, in that order, with their keys; `IN_HAND` ranks it with the conversation while typing, and Recent never takes from it. Remind me… stays with the email's actions; while an item is in hand it answers "later" no more (its aliases and keywords lose the words of the item's own Later, en and es, so "later" finds Later… and no "(later)"), and its presets, the remind page's and the root's search-only ones, stop before the item's moment (`remindLimit`: a timed item's time, an untimed one's day's end; none once that has passed or for what names no day): "Later today 1:00 PM" is no longer offered for a 12:30 reservation.

**The reader's line (R8).** `thread-items.tsx`: the item's line is a column: the sentence on a line of its own, the card's whole width (`text-pretty`), and Done, Later, Change…, Hand to…, Remove under it on the sentence's edge (Done's outline on its first letter), wrapping rather than shrinking on a narrow card; a history line's outcome sits under it too.

**Change… and Hand to… (small things).** Keys beside their siblings: ⇧B Change… (b puts the item off; ⇧B sets its own day) and ⇧F Hand to… (f forwards the email; ⇧F hands the item to someone), on the same target as D, b and ⇧D (`changeOnSelected`, `handOnSelected` in `today-task.ts`: the key's row or the reader's chosen item; an ask with no day leaves ⇧B alone), in the shortcuts help, the palette and the menu. The menu says what each does under it, as Remove's "The email stays": "Sets its own day" (es "Le pones tú el día"), "Forwards it to someone" (es "Se lo reenvía a alguien"), each the item's `aria-describedby` (`MenuNote`, shared with Remove's).

**What the provider keeps (small things).** Nothing in the app or the plans (97 included) states what a provider keeps or for how long, and it varies by account and changes, so Terno claims no period: Settings → AI's privacy note adds one sentence after where mail goes, "How long OpenAI keeps what it receives, and what for, is set by its own API data policy, not by Terno: read it on its website." (plural for two providers; es "Cuánto tiempo guarda OpenAI lo que recibe, y para qué, lo decide su propia política de datos de la API, no Terno: consúltala en su sitio web."), `keepsLine` in `lib/ai.ts`; nothing where nothing leaves the Mac.

**Tests.** Core: `promiseBroken`'s today and ahead and the midnight between (and the clock's wake-up), Ahead's day +7/+8 boundary for an expiry and a person's early request, never counted (`dated.test.ts`); the projection's broken promise today first and counted to do today, ahead in Waiting and overdue, both paths, Later, the midnight, a reply's row and a duplicate for a canonical in Trash; Ahead's one group by days with no bucket and Coming up after it; Waiting with no far band; a far request under Ahead, never counted; the return leg with no Also; the attendance line, and none for an errand's duplicate (`today-projection.test.ts`); the order keys, revision 7's case, a day ahead's unchanged order (`today.test.ts`). Backend: the counts' new field and the new order through the RPC (`dated.test.ts`, `today-schedule.test.ts`: a request changed past the seven days is Ahead's, `promise-answers.test.ts`). Web: the header's overdue en and es (`today-line.test.tsx`), Ahead's place, column and Coming up's fallback to its last row (`state/today.test.tsx`), the travel clock in the reader's line and the screen reader's words en and es, the attendance line en and es (`lib/dated.test.ts`), the palette's item group and its arrangement (`palette-commands.test.ts`), `remindLimit` and ⇧B/⇧F's targets (`today-task.test.ts`), the reader's line's structure (`thread-items-change.test.tsx`), `keepsLine` en and es (`ai.test.ts`), the keys (`resolve.test.ts`). E2E (`what-matters-feed.spec.ts`, iteration 5's own test): the header "3 to do today · N waiting, 1 overdue" and the badge, Today's order with the slip first, Ahead after the days with its rows' dates and Coming up after it (closed, open in its buckets, its fallback onto Ahead's last row), the reader's line (the controls under the sentence, Done on its edge, axe light and dark), the palette with an item selected (its group first under its words, Later found by "later" with no alias, Remind me's page offering nothing past the 7:00 PM dinner), the menu's notes and ⇧B; iteration 1's and 4's tests, `today.spec`'s menu order, `what-matters-change.spec` and `hand-to.spec` follow the new order, keys and notes.

**Perf (the Today gate, 50k threads; a shared machine, load 5 to 17).** Interleaved with the base (`54dda80e7`'s `packages/core/src` and `packages/backend/src` checked out over the branch's), base, branch, base, branch, at three times the budget so every scenario reports (list p95 / count added / longest turn, ms). SQLite: spread base 375 (load 14: noise) and 22.9 / 1.5 / 63.8, branch 21.8 / 0.6 / 63.1 and 22.8 / 1.0 / 62.6; all showing base 18.3 / 2.3 / 34.3, branch 18.7 / 3.8 / 35.9 and 19.7 / 2.3 / 35.2; Needs you at its bound base 55.6 / 2.2 / 147.6, branch 78.5 / 1.9 / 147.1 and 44.4 / 2.2 / 148.3; Coming up far base 47.6 / 4.6 / 66.0, branch 45.8 / 2.1 / 83.7 and 48.4 / 1.3 / 60.4; linked requests at the combined bounds base 61.4 / 9.6 / 141.7, branch 100.6 / 10.9 / 142.5 and 79.0 / 12.7 / 137.9. IndexedDB: spread base 12.2 to 12.5 / 1.5 to 2.0 / 22.9 to 24.3, branch 13.2 to 13.4 / 0.4 to 1.2 / 22.3 to 24.2; at the bound base 14.7 to 15.2 / 1.5 to 1.9 / 19.5 to 24.0, branch 14.8 to 18.1 / 1.8 to 2.4 / 21.4 to 22.6; linked base 20.5 to 21.0 / 3.0 to 5.4 / 26.5 to 27.1, branch 22.0 to 22.2 / 3.1 to 5.0 / 26.9 to 27.2. At the gate's own budget (load 5) the branch fails as the base does on this machine: SQLite spread's longest turn 64.1 ms against 60 (base 63.8 interleaved), IndexedDB Needs you at its bound's list 16.4 ms against 15 (base 14.7 to 15.2). The branch is within the base's spread run to run; nothing measured points at the change. What's added on the hot path: `promiseBroken` returns which day (the same work), one five-number key per Today row instead of a branchy comparator, one cached string compare for Ahead per placed item, and the detail pass in `placeItems` gone (a mailbox with return legs now takes the fast path).

**Screenshots** (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/it5-a-shots/`, `WM_IT5_SHOTS`, 1440 wide): `feed-top`, `ahead-coming-up`, `reader-line`, `palette`, `menu`, each `-{en,es}-{light,dark}.png`.

**Known limits.** Remind me's "Pick a date…" (its own picker) isn't bounded by the item's moment; only the presets are. A return leg behind closed Coming up is no longer hinted on its outbound's row (the trip's own day shows it once it's within the seven days). A request shown early that lands in Ahead says its date ("Nov 24"), not "by Nov 24" (the people's wording stays Today's). Coming up opened under Ahead can show a day earlier than Ahead's last row (Ahead's heading names no day, the owner's option (a)). The attendance line is only on an event's row: with the event over or settled, the promise is its own row as before. The reader's line said "You told Ms. you'd send the signed slip" for "Ms. Ruiz" (the person's first word; the reader line's wording, not this part's). `design-axe.spec`'s composer test failed once under load (load 13 to 17, the ⌘J button's name) and passed on a rerun and on the base: nothing here touches the composer. Not run: `pnpm test:all`, `pnpm ui:rate`, the Codex code review.

**Shared code touched (for the merge).** `TodayCounts` gained `overdue` (core; every literal in backend and web, tests included); `promiseBroken` returns `"today" | "ahead" | null` (was boolean); `TODAY_GROUP_ORDER`, `TodayGroupKind` and `TaskPlace` gained `ahead`; `placeItems` lost the detail-as-Also path and `PlacedItem.detail`; `inDayOrder` and `byDayKey` were replaced by `todayOrderKey`/`dayOrderKey`/`sortToday`/`sortInDay` (`sortInDay`'s signature lost `clock`); `TodayAlso` gained `attends`; `DatedFacts` (web) gained `wall`/`travel`; `FeedGroupKind` gained `ahead`; the palette's `PaletteGroup` gained `item`; key `Command` gained `today.change` and `today.handTo` (⇧B, ⇧F); the thread menu's What matters items gained notes (`MenuNote`); i18n en and es keys added only.


#### How iteration 5's mute offer was built

Point 5 and revision 2 (the owner's "a sender-and-kind pair, never a person you've written to"), on its own branch from `54dda80e7`, beside the other four parts.

- **One identity.** A pair is a third mute target, `pair`, keyed `kind|sender` (`pairKey`: the kind first, so the first bar splits it; `pairOf` reads it back, `muteKey("pair", …)` normalizes the sender as `addressKey` and refuses a kind that isn’t exactly one of ours). The same key goes through the store contract (`dated.mutes.list/add/remove`), the RPC (`dated.mute`/`dated.unmute` take `on: "pair"`), the offer's memory (`pair:<key>`), Settings' list and both Undos. `mutedOf` gains `pairs`, and `isMuted` tests a row's `pairKey` against them, so What matters, its count and the reader's line (`withoutMuted`, read once per rebuild as before) hide an open item of a muted pair, never close it, and show it again on Show again.
- **The table** (`dated_mute_pairs`, SQLite v72, IndexedDB version 37 / logical 72, shape only: an empty `WITHOUT ROWID` table keyed `(sender_key, kind)`; 0.1 ms at 57,000 conversations, v24 → v72 226 ms). v69's `dated_mutes`, its CHECK and key are untouched: a `pair` row there is still refused. Its own bound, `DATED_MUTE_PAIRS_MAX` (200), refused at it in its own words ("…hides 200 kinds of mail from particular senders…"), nothing evicted; the senders' and kinds' 200 apart. The list reads both tables in one statement (`UNION ALL`, newest first, then target, then key), the same order in IndexedDB.
- **The reader's commit.** Both stores' `finishDated` read the finishing sender's pairs (by the key's prefix in SQL, an index by sender in IndexedDB) beside the muted kinds, and leave out that kind's new rows from that sender; settlements, moves, their other kinds, the follow-ups re-mark of their other requests and the reading itself (no "gone") go through. A pair never enters `mutedSenders`, `silenceOf` or `mutesChanged`: nothing of theirs goes unread, no reading is stopped, and Needs you's walk never sees it.
- **The offer** (`DatedMutes.offer`). After the user's own Remove: nothing at all if they've written to the sender (`dated.written([senderKey])`) or the sender is muted; else that kind from that sender, the first time; else the sender alone, the first time, only with positive evidence it's a machine (`automatedSender`: list or bulk headers on the removed item's mail, or a no-reply address, plan 17's level 2; a role address like `orders@` isn't enough, a person may answer it); never a whole kind. Taking it (`dated.mute` without `at`) checks `written` again: a send between the offer and the click refuses it in words ("You've written to Parcelo, so What matters keeps showing their mail."). An Unmute's Undo (`at`) puts back what was, unchecked, as Undo is exact. *Decision:* a person's request kinds (`ask`, `respond`) may be a pair: one sender's, never everyone's, and never offered for someone you've written to.
- **Whole kinds from Settings only.** Settings → What matters gains "Hide a kind from every sender…" (BoardUI's Dropdown on a `link-secondary` button, under the list): the kinds mail tells you about not hidden yet (`WHOLE_MUTE_KINDS`, which was `OFFERED_MUTE_KINDS`: never `ask` or `respond`), hidden with Undo in the snack. The gear's popover lists, as before, and adds nothing.
- **Words** (en, es). "Don't show deliveries from Parcelo" / "No mostrar entregas de Parcelo"; the machine alone "Don't show anything from Parcelo" / "No mostrar nada de Parcelo"; listed "Deliveries from Parcelo" with the address beside it (never twice); done, Show again, Unmute and its Undo each name both ("You won't see deliveries from Parcelo in What matters"). *Decision:* with the longer offer the first line had about 110 px and cut "Removed from What matters" to "Removed from …"; with an offer it now says only "Removed" / "Quitado", the item's words under it as before (the name is still cut at 24). The empty list says "When you remove an item, you can hide that kind of mail from its sender."

**Tests.** Conformance in both stores (`dated-mutes.ts`, and SQLite-WASM): a pair normalized, listed with the others newest first across a reopen, never removed as its sender or kind, a half pair refused; the pairs' own bound; two kinds from one sender (only the muted kind's new item left out; the existing one moved and kept; their bill and another sender's delivery kept; the settlement through the pair); a pair never silencing (the reading kept, the mark consumed, the conversation still waiting on you); `written` changing once a sent message reaches them. The v72 migration test (v69's table and SQL untouched, a `pair` refused there) and an IndexedDB version-36 upgrade (`mutes-upgrade.test.ts`). Backend (`dated.test.ts`, through the RPC with a scripted model): the offer's order (pair, the machine alone, nothing; another kind its own pair; never a kind); a person offered their kind, never alone, and someone written to offered nothing; a send between offer and accept refused in words, for a pair and a sender; a pair hiding and suppressing that kind only while their mail is still read, their other kind kept and a settlement through, Unmute and its Undo; a pair never reaching Needs you; the pairs' bound in words. Web (`dated-mutes.test.ts`): the offer's words en and es with a long name cut, the list's names and addresses, Settings' kinds, the pair's mute and Unmute each with Undo. E2E (`what-matters-mute.spec.ts`, port 5591): the pair offered, taken, z; the machine alone next; a shop that may be a person offered its kind, never itself; nothing on a third Remove; the gear's list with axe in light and dark and Show again by keyboard; Settings' empty line, the kind menu by keyboard (no requests in it), a kind and a pair listed, axe. Run: typecheck, oxlint on the changed files, both conformance suites and the upgrade tests (1,615 and 703 passed), the migration tests, `migrations.perf`, the backend's `dated.test.ts` (165) and `promise-answers.test.ts`, the web units, `what-matters-mute.spec.ts` and `today.spec.ts` (21 passed; one load flake on the first run, passed alone and on the rerun).
