**Perf** (the Today gate holds 200 pairs, none of anyone in the mailbox, beside the 200 muted senders). Load 5 to 8 (`uptime` 6.1 → 5.2 over the runs; the 15-minute average 11 to 12): alternated with and without the pairs, SQLite spread list 21.9 / 21.1 ms with, 22.3 / 23.9 without; count 12.5 / 11.2 with, 11.6 / 11.5 without; the longest busy turn 64.6 / 66.7 ms with, 65.1 / 67.5 without, over its 60 both ways, so the gate failed on SQLite before and after this part on this machine. IndexedDB spread 12.6 / 12.2 with, 13.4 / 11.5 without; all showing 18.6 (one 91 ms spike in that run) / 11.0 with, 12.4 / 11.0 without; Needs you at its bound 17.0 with, 17.7 / 16.5 without, over its 15 both ways. What a pair adds on the path is one more `UNION ALL` of an at most 200-row table in the list already read once per rebuild, and a string key and set lookup a row; the noise is larger than that. The gate isn't shown green: SQLite's busy turn and IndexedDB's Needs you list need the quiet machine the waiting rule's numbers came from.

**Shared edits outside the offer** (small, said for the merge): `apps/web/src/lib/dated.ts`'s `said` uses "Removed" when there's an offer; `today.spec.ts`'s three Remove snack assertions read either first line; `promise-answers.test.ts`'s merge test mutes a correspondent as an Unmute's Undo would (`at`), since a new mute of someone you've written to is refused; `idb.uncertain-upgrade.test.ts` lists the new store. IndexedDB version 37 and logical 72 are taken here: v73's attempt state, if it adds a store, renumbers to 38 on rebase (and the logical schema follows the highest step).

**Known limits.** Mail of a muted pair's kind that arrived while it was muted isn't stored, so Show again brings back only what was there before. The machine test is per item (the removed item's own mail): a sender whose one message carried a list header is offered alone even if another of theirs didn't. Taking a sender alone rechecks `written`, not the machine evidence (the offer had it, and nothing else offers it). Iteration 4's offered kinds and senders stay in the offer's memory, so a sender already offered alone then isn't offered again.



#### How iteration 5's second look was built

Revision 3, the owner's "a second look at a dated sentence that got no item". It lives in a module of its own (`packages/backend/src/dated-second-look.ts`) that `DatedWorker.consider` calls between its one reading and its one `finish`.

- **The trigger** (`lookAt`, pure, en and es). A sentence of the mail as read (quotes stripped) holds a moment when it names a day from the mail's own day on (`namesDayFrom` in `core/dated-days.ts`, `saysDay`'s reader: a date judged by itself, a weekday, "the 17th" or "tomorrow" alone counted as ahead; never an amount like "$12.10" or a street like "20th St") and a clock time or a closing ("closes", "due", "deadline", "expires", "vence", "cierra"; never "closed" or "ends"), and isn't a schedule ("every Monday at 6:00"). It's covered by any returned item's quote or settlement's, move's or replacement's evidence (its sentence), by a day an item or a move returned (the model read that day: a statement's "Payment due date" beside the balance it quoted), or by the day of an open item or promise the reading was shown (a reminder of it). A local rejection covers it too: a receipt or something already done ("Payment received", "Delivered: …", "se cobró"), a sale's end, and, for the whole mail, a scam's pressure (`scamPressure`) or a charge or renewal made by itself that the mail doesn't say is off or failed. An automated mail with more than two moments, or one on a list's line ("- RevOps Live — Thursday, Dec 3, 1 PM ET"), is a listing and is never asked about.
- **The bounds.** At most two sentences, in the mail's order, 1,000 characters in all, each whole (`DATED_AGAIN_SENTENCES`, `DATED_AGAIN_CHARS`, applied in the backend and again in the prompt); one extra call per message, ever.
- **The call.** `readDates` with the same input, provider, signal and accounting (the limiter's `holdingUp` sees it as it does the first), fitted again with `again`: the first reading's prompt word for word, then our own paragraph and a `<look_again>` block after `</mail>` (the mail can't open or close it); the system prompt and its rules unchanged, so the version stays the reading's. Only items that quote an asked sentence are kept from it; its settlements, moves and replacements never (the first reading had the whole mail). What's kept joins the first reading's items in one `checkDates`, so every check, the item bound (first reading's items first) and the output limit hold.
- **Rechecked before it leaves:** the settings and the AI gate (off: the mark stays, unclaimed, for later), and Spam, Trash, a moved thread and a muted sender (the mark is dropped, nothing written, as for the first reading).
- **One claim, recorded (v73, shape only).** `ai_dated_mail.looked_at`, claimed in one statement (`marks.claimLook`, both stores; IndexedDB reads an absent field as unclaimed, no version change) after the rechecks and before the call. A failure, a retry, a cancel or a restart reads the mail again, but never asks the second look again: that reading is written as it is. 1 ms at 57,000 conversations; v24 → v73 236 ms.
- **When it fails** (a provider error, a limit): the first reading is still written, the failure's hold or pause applies (`failed(null, …)`: the mark is already gone), and the message is never "nothing asked". "Nothing asked" is written only when no look was needed or the look was asked and answered.

**Measured on the panel** (`scripts/ai-real/dated-second-look.real.ts`, replay mode, offline: the panel's 331 recorded first readings of round 13 as the model, through `lookAt`): **25 of 331 mails (7.6%) get a second look**, 25 sentences (one each), by persona Diego 7, Sam 6, Priya 4, Jordan 3, Margaret 2, Theo 2, María 1. Among them Margaret's book club and Sam's spring registration; the rest are mostly a person's dated sentence the reading saw as no item (a technician's visit, a workshop invitation quoted from the subject, a student's own deadline), single-line seminar and webinar announcements, a hotel's check-out line, a fraud alert's transaction line. The first version of the trigger asked about 65 (20%): amounts read as dates, past days, newsletters' calendars and street names, each now refused locally. **The cost** (provider-counted tokens in the eval): a look sends about what a first reading sends, Luna 6,553 input and 172 output tokens a look (6,284 and 352 a first reading), Haiku 9,533 and 356 (9,171 and 360): about $0.0007 a look on Luna and $0.0011 on Haiku, so at the panel's rate about **+7.6% requests and +7.5% spend** on the dated reader. The bound: one look per message, at most two sentences and 1,000 characters on top of the first reading's prompt.

**The eval** (corpus/dated/second-look.jsonl, 9 cases, five readings each, both models, real money under the shared ledger). Two modes: *fresh*, a first reading of its own then the look when needed; *recorded*, the panel's own first answers (the book club lost, the registration empty), then a real look, which is the panel's miss itself.

- Luna, final prompt: fresh 30 of 30 found, 27 looks, 2 recovered by the look; recorded 30 of 30 found, all 20 recovered (10 book clubs at 19:00, 10 registrations due December 9 at 23:59), none wrong. One wrong row in the fresh mode, from the first reading itself with no look (`sl-02`'s "Preparar las preguntas", Margarita's own promise, as a `respond`), 1 in 5.
- Haiku: fresh 30 of 30, 25 looks, none wrong; recorded 30 of 30, all 20 recovered, none wrong.
- Traps, both models, 5 of 5 each: a newsletter's two listed events (en, es) and a webinar asked about and left out; a student's own lab report deadline and another person's room booking asked about and left out.
- What the eval changed: the look's first words ("read them again, as if alone") made Luna return nothing for the book club 7 in 10; naming a group's gathering a person tells the reader about fixed it (10 of 10), and "7:00" read as 07:00 twice in 10 until the paragraph said a time without AM or PM is read at its kind's likely hour.
- Spend: $0.49 for all the eval's runs (377 requests); the shared ledger stood at $45.12 of $55.

**Tests:** the trigger (covered and uncovered sentences, en and es; an item's quote, its day, a settlement, a move, an open item's day; the rejections; listings; past days, amounts, streets, schedules), the bounds, and the call with fakes (no call for what's covered, the rechecks before the claim, one claim, a failure kept incomplete, an abort left to the worker) in `dated-second-look.test.ts`; through the backend: Margaret's book club asked once and both rows kept, a covered sentence never asked, Sam's registration "nothing asked" only after the look, a failed look keeping the reading without "nothing asked" and holding the provider, a look cut off by a stop never asked again after a restart, and Spam or a mute between the reading and its look. The prompt's block and bounds in `ai/dated.test.ts`, `namesDayFrom` in `core/dated-days.test.ts`, the claim in both stores' conformance (across a reopen), v73 in the migration tests. Two of `dated.test.ts`'s request counts now count readings, not looks.

**Known limits.** The look is lost, not retried, when it fails or is cut off (one claim, by design); the reading it followed is written. A listing rule that skips automated mail with three or more moments can skip a real deadline the first reading missed in a booking or a long notice. The holdingUp check already stops a look whose mail went to Spam or was muted meanwhile, so the recheck before it mostly saves a claim and a trip through the limiter.

**Shared code touched (outside this part):** `consider`'s reading call and its "nothing asked" line, and `failed`'s mark made optional (`packages/backend/src/dated.ts`); `DatedInput.again`, `datedPrompt`'s tail and `dataText`'s tag list (`packages/ai/src/dated.ts`, beside agent B's prompt words, which it doesn't touch); `namesDayFrom` added to `core/dated-days.ts`; `marks.claimLook` in the store contract and both stores; two request counts in `dated.test.ts`.


#### How iteration 5's reading was built

Design points 3 and 4 (but the part of the day and the second look, which are their own parts) with the Codex review's revisions 1, 8 and 9, V1 and V2, and "before the 19th" on a renewal; prompt v16, on `t3/what-matters-204` from `54dda80e7`, one commit each (`41126d4ae` the stake, `06fe0b8e4` a renewal's "before", `b0bde07c9` revision 1, `eeb911f13` revision 8, `0c64affa0` and `442987073` V2, `1c474d81c` an alert's instruction, `b716730d9` the prompt and the eval). No migration.

- **Revision 1: what renews or is paid by itself is no item** (`handledItself`, `dated-automatic.ts`, replacing iteration 4's `routineAutoBill` and v15's priced renewal). A pay, a renewal or an expiry is no item when its own words say, affirmatively and on no condition, that this plan, subscription or membership renews by itself ("will renew automatically on December 1", "billed to Visa ending 4417"), that this bill is charged by itself ("AutoPay is on. We'll charge your Visa on the due date"), or that this invoice is paid ("has been paid in full", "ya está pagada"). Its own words: a sentence of its quote's paragraph, or one that names it (its amount, its plan's name, "your bill", "the due date"), never one that names another invoice's number or amount and never a footer about other customers ("Customers enrolled in AutoPay…"). Vetoes, read on every sentence not about another item: a negation ("is not paid automatically"), auto-renew off, a lapse unless renewed (`lapsesUnlessRenewed`), a failed charge, an expired or invalid card, a balance still owed ("A balance of $215.50 remains", "saldo pendiente"), a trial, and a request to pay it in its own sentence. A condition, a fee or an offer is no affirmation ("We will charge a $25 late fee unless it's paid", "If you have automatic renewal turned on, no action is needed", "Set up AutoPay so…"). A later receipt still settles only through `tiedTo` and the stores' revision checks: a receipt for invoice 2231 settles 2231, never 2240 from the same sender (a test holds it). Theo's Loomcast, Priya's Pagewise and Framewell, Diego's Creatix and María's Brightline get no row; Margaret's policy (pay by Nov 25), Theo's Appvault (renew unless automatic) and the domains with auto-renew off keep theirs; Tipotec's trial stays. *Decision:* Creatix's "cancelling after the 19th costs 50%" is an automatic renewal with a fee for changing later, so it goes with the others, as the owner's "auto-renewed like a plan… nobody cares" reads; the eval's `dated-204`/`205` (iteration 4's Creatix and Canvas) now expect nothing.
- **Revision 8: relayed asks** (`relayedAsk`, `dated-relay.ts`). One predicate: a named human (a handle, or a name of two or three capitalized words) right before a relay verb ("requested your review", "asked you to", "sent you a message", "invited you to <something concrete>", "te pidió", "te envió un mensaje"), asking the reader (the relay's own "you", or, for a message, a request in it to the reader by name or as "you"), for a concrete thing (a reference like `#233` or `owner/repo`, or a word that isn't a network's own), in the relay's sentence or the block it introduces. Nobody ("Someone requested…"), the service itself ("Linkwell sent you a message"), "posted", "people you may know", "join the conversation" and "invited you to connect" fail. The worker computes it once, from the subject and body, never for a person's own mail: Social and Promotions mail is read only for a relay (its body waits only when the subject may carry one, `mayRelay`), the automated cost filter lets a relay through, the 14-day check (`scanPicks`) counts one, and `checkDates` gets the relation (`relay`): `askAllowed` takes an automated sender's ask only when its quote is the relay's own words and names no word of the service's ("Reply on Linkwell" isn't Tomás's ask). The service stays no person (`src.person` untouched).
- **Two questions stay two** (revision 8, María's Elena). `requested`'s blanket "both answer the sender" merge (Karin's, iteration 4) is replaced: an ask is that request's only when its quote is in the request's own sentence or the list the request introduces (`partOfRequest`: "I need a reply from you today with: 1. An ETA…"), or when it answers the sender and its own sentence asks nothing ("I have to brief our COO at 3pm Central"). Two replies on one day merge only on the same terms. "Can you bring the tamales AND the arroz?" now stays beside the head count. An old question moved by a sentence that doesn't name it keeps only whom it answers (`whomOnly`: "Answer Elena", by Sunday, never "about working Thanksgiving"). Found on the eval: with two asks allowed, Haiku made Dolores's "Margaret, you said you'd do the discussion questions this time" an ask (1 in 5); a reminder of what the reader said they'd do, whose sentence asks nothing, is no ask (`yourOwnWord`, en and es).
- **Revision 9: the stake held to the mail before the cut** (`heldStake`, `@terno/ai`'s `dated-stake.ts`, run in `readDatesTask`'s validation on items and on an o-item's move words, before `withoutIdentifiers` and `cutAtClause`). Only a pay's, a renewal's, an expiry's or a request's brackets; each clause apart (";" and ", ", never "$1,186.40" or "Dec 1, 2026"). A clause that says nothing happens, or states an amount or a percentage, stays. A consequence (held, paused, suspended, removed, ends, flagged, shown, lapses, fee, "se libera", "se pierde", "no hotel room"…) stays when it carries its condition ("or …" at the clause's start, never "card or bank account"; "if", "unless", "after", "late", "missing", "si no", "después", "faltantes"; or a condition said in an earlier clause of its bracket) and the mail says something happens on a condition and never denies it; or when the mail says it plainly with no condition ("certification expires Dec 10"). A fact the mail says only on a condition goes: "(parcel removed from plan)", "(policy ends Dec 1 at 12:01 AM)", "(flagged to your manager)", "($10.99 failed; switches to Free, playlists removed)" → "($10.99 failed)". A condition left alone ("(si no)") takes its bracket with it. Cross-language (a Spanish bracket on English mail) a conditional clause is kept on any conditional sentence of the mail, a plain one dropped. **The cut** (`cutAtClause`) never keeps what happens without its condition (`orphanedConsequence`): no clause end, and no word cut, that leaves "the parcel leaves the plan…" without the "if it's missed" after it; a bracket with a consequence word is kept whole. Not fixable locally: Sam's "(unit shown if no reply)" has its condition; it read as odd, which only the words can change.
- **A renewal's own "before"** (`renewalBefore`): a renewal whose words say "before" its own day ("Cancel Tipotec Pro before Sat", "Cambiar o cancelar Creatix antes del 19 nov") sorts under the day before and keeps the day asked before, as a deadline's do; the mail's "renew before it expires on the 19th" still never moves it, nor a negated or lower-bound "before". `dated-003` now accepts the Thursday.
- **V2, an event's start from its quote** (`startClock`, `startOf`): an event the model gave no time, whose quote states exactly one start, takes it on the reader's clock: "Monday Nov 16, 2026 · 4pm – 5pm (Pacific Time)" is 16:00 Pacific, converted when the reader is elsewhere and dropped when that moves it to another day. Never a bare "7:00" (morning or evening), two starts, a zone that reads two ways ("CST", "IST") or isn't a region's ("hora de Lima"), a window ("12–2 PM", "between…", "entre…": found on the full round, the furnace service read as 14:00, fixed), or an event the model put on a place's clock (travel).
- **V2, a parcel's later mail moves it**: a delivery whose words name the one open delivery of its conversation (a word of what's coming, never "arriving", "order" or the sender's name; the carrier's other address in the same thread counts) is that item moved, with its new words, when the model neither moved nor settled it; checked as any move (`tiedTo`, `saysWhen`). One the checks refuse stays the item it was unless it says the open one's own day. Diego's monitor arm (`dated-258`): moved 24 of 24 readings (12 of them a new item the model gave, turned into the move), never a second row. *Not done:* an estimate already expired into Earlier while its parcel is open (rows stored before this) still shows its history line; the move keeps new ones from expiring.
- **An alert never instructs, in a bracket either**: "(update your payment method to keep watching)", "; falta actualizar el método de pago" and "(hay que actualizar…)" leave an alert's words (Haiku, 4 in 10 on v16's first words for `dated-009`); a bracket of details stays.
- **Prompt v16** (eval first). A bill already paid ("paid in full", "payment received") is no item beside AutoPay's; a plan, subscription or membership that renews by itself, even at a price the reader may change or with a fee for cancelling later, is no item at all; a free trial ending is always a "renew", even one that turns into a paid plan by itself. The stake is said as what happens if they don't ("or …", "if unpaid", "after Thu"), never as if it had happened; the examples now teach conditions ("(or deploys pause Mon)", "(if unpaid, it lapses Dec 1)", "(or card suspended Nov 16)"); a fee as it is; a failed payment's bracket says what failed first. "At most one ask a mail" goes: two things a person asks are two items unless one is part of the other's request. A person's ask a service relays to the reader by name is that person's "ask", named for them, never the service, and never a network's own call (examples that aren't eval cases: "@mara-lin…", "Ines Duarte…"). An event over several days says its last day ("Pressline Summit in Denver (Thu–Fri)"); a hotel stay is an event on its check-in day at its check-in time, its words the day it ends. An invitation's own line ("Thu Oct 8 · 2pm – 3pm") states its start. Two words tried and taken back on the way: "one whose charge failed" under renew (and later "is never a renew: it's that failed payment") made both models file a failed subscription as a renew or an undated pay, which the app drops (`dated-009`, `184`: up to 4 in 5 on Luna). Small windows need a little over 9,200 tokens (v15 8,800): the dated test's window is 9,300.
- **V1: did v15 lose three items?** Five readings each, the panel's own three mails, v14's words against v15's, the app's English, Luna and Haiku (60 readings). Margaret's book club: Luna v14 4 of 5, v15 5 of 5; Haiku 5 of 5 on both. Sam's registration: 5 of 5 on all four. Theo's all-hands time: Luna v14 5 of 5, **v15 2 of 5** (time null, as the panel saw); Haiku 5 of 5 on both. So v15 lost only the all-hands' time, on Luna; the book club and the registration were chance. Fixed with the invitation's line in the time rule and the start-time backstop (V2), which gives 16:00 even when the model doesn't. All three are in the eval, fictionalized (`dated-250` to `253`).
- **The eval** (`dated.real.ts`, 259 cases, 31 new: `dated-229` to `dated-259`, fictionalized from round 13, en and es): renewals by themselves (Ridgeway, Pincel), an invoice paid, mixed invoices (en, es), a partial payment, "a $15 late fee unless it's paid", an automatic renewal with an expired card, Appvault's conditional "no action needed"; Codeforge's review request, Linkwell's message (en, es), a network's post and "people you may know"; Rosa's two questions (en, es); the stake as a condition (the tax installment en and es, the policy, the lease, the receipts in Spanish); the book club, the registration and the all-hands (en, es); a summit over two days (en, es) and a hotel (en, es); the carrier's mail for an open parcel; a trial's "before". `dated-004`, `066`, `198`, `199`, `204`, `205` (renewals by themselves) now expect nothing; `196` is held to a condition (`textNot`); `003` accepts the Thursday; `134` allows Fer's second ask; patterns widened where a right text said "Visa" for a card. The runner passes the relay as the worker does and checks a case with its own reader's zone on that clock.
- **Eval first, v15's words with the new checks**, one reading of the 38 new and changed cases: Luna English 27 of 29, Spanish 28 of 29; Haiku English 26 of 29 (the trial missed), Spanish 28 of 29; the wrong rows were the multi-day cases' first keys (the reader's talk on the second day, rekeyed to a registration) and one Spanish fact-stake.
- **Five readings each of the 38 new and changed cases, v16's first words** (145 expected items a model and language): Luna English 143 of 145, none wrong shown, 2 wrong words; Luna Spanish 142 of 145, none wrong shown, 2 wrong words (a Spanish fact-stake, "(faltantes al gerente)", then read as a condition); Haiku English 145 of 145, none wrong, no wrong words; Haiku Spanish 145 of 145, 2 wrong shown (the book club reminder as an ask, fixed by `yourOwnWord`; a badge pick-up as an event of its own, 1 in 5, left), 1 wrong word. Every time found was right (25 of 25, Luna Spanish 24 of 24).
- **One full round, all 259 cases, v16's second words** (187 expected items): Luna English 184 of 187, 2 wrong shown; Luna Spanish 181, 2; Haiku English 183, 2; Haiku Spanish 185, 3. Each model's two: `dated-003` (the trial now sorted under the Thursday by its own "before Friday": rekeyed) and `184` (a failed subscription as a renew: the renew words); besides, `061`'s window read as 14:00 by the backstop (fixed), `101`/`102`'s failed payments bracketing the late fee instead of what failed, `009` as an undated renew (dropped), Fer's second request as an ask (`134`, allowed). After those fixes, five readings of the renew and pay cases (19 cases, 65 expected items a model and language): Luna English 62 of 65, Spanish 61, Haiku English 65, Spanish 65, none wrong shown; the misses all `009`, Luna giving the undated failed subscription as an undated pay (dropped by the app), 7 in 10, and Haiku's alert for it keeping "(update your payment method…)", 4 in 10. Five more of the failed payments after the alert fix and the last renew words (5 cases, 25 each): Haiku English 25 of 25, Spanish 25, Luna 23 of 25 each (`009` as an undated pay 3 in 10, and `184` once as a respond, on the words before the last; the last words took that renew sentence out, and the final round's `009` and `184` were right on all four).
- **The final full round, all 259 cases, the final words**: **all four found 187 of 187**. Wrong shown: Luna English 0; Luna Spanish 1 (`dated-020`'s customs scam as a 2,99 € payment, as in iteration 4's round); Haiku English 1 (`dated-149`, a newsletter's workshop as an event); Haiku Spanish 1 (`dated-246`, the tax installment also as a respond beside its pay) and 1 alert's bracket the eval reads as an instruction (`dated-099`, "(se cancelará si no responde)", iteration 4's known limit). Times 44 of 44, befores 2 of 2, walls 4 of 4, no settlement, move or replacement wrong, no text cut inside a word. Wrong words over the eval's bar of 1 on Luna (English 4: `206`'s stake dropped to its amount, `203`'s amount, `106`'s zone note, `010`; Spanish 3: `139`'s card month, `206`, `209`'s "Laura"), and the eval's `unsafe` bar on Haiku Spanish. **Spend:** these evals cost about $4.70 of the shared ledger (V1 about $0.07); it stood at $48.05 of $55.
- **Tests:** `pnpm typecheck` (all packages), `oxlint --deny-warnings` on the changed files; unit: all of `packages/ai/src` (with `dated-stake.test.ts`), `backend/dated.test.ts`, `dated-evidence.test.ts`, `dated-automatic.test.ts`, `dated-relay.test.ts`, `today.test.ts`, `today-history.test.ts`, `today-links.test.ts`, `today-schedule.test.ts`, `followups.test.ts`, `promise-answers.test.ts`, `suspicious-sender.test.ts`, `hand-to.test.ts`, `scripts/ai-real/dated-checks.test.ts` (691 passed); the prompt snapshot updated. Each intermediate commit's changed source file typechecked; its tests ran on the final tree only. Not run: e2e (no flow's screens changed), the perf gates (the relay reads the body the worker already reads; `scanPicks` now reads Social mail's bodies within its existing walk and slices), `pnpm test:all`, a Codex review (not asked).
- **Known limits.** The relay reads English and Spanish words only, and a message relayed with a question in it and a concrete word passes even when it's a generic invitation dressed up ("Join our webinar on X?"). `handledItself` is the item's own words: an automatic renewal said only in a paragraph that names nothing of the item and points to nothing ("Your plan renews soon. Amount: …" with no "automatically" or card) stays. A Spanish bracket on an English mail can't be matched word for word: a fact clause there is dropped (safe), a conditional one kept on any conditional sentence. A model's misquoted relay ("@kofi-a-a") is refused like any invented quote. The start-time backstop reads the quote only, so an event its quote dates and the next line times keeps no time. Rows stored before this keep their old brackets and renewals until done.
- **Shared code touched outside this part:** `checkDates`' input (`relay`), `askAllowed`, `requested` and the two-replies rule in `checkDates`, the worker's `consider` gates and `scanPicks`, the move of an ask (`whomOnly`), `alertText` (`@terno/ai`), `cutAtClause`/`stakeSpans`; `scripts/ai-real/dated.real.ts` (relay, zone). Nothing in the stores, core, the web or i18n.


##### What the code review changed (iteration 5)

Codex's review of the iteration (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/review-it5.out`, 8 points) and two second-look faults round 14's readings showed (`<persona>/readings.jsonl`, the entries with `<look_again>`). All taken; each has a test that fails without it (checked by putting the file back to its last commit and running the test).

1. **A paid invoice pays only the invoice its words pick out** (`dated-automatic.ts`). "Your October invoice has been paid in full. Your November invoice for $300 is due November 30" dropped November's: the shared "invoice" made October's sentence November's. A "paid" sentence now settles the item only when it's the item's own sentence, the one right after it going on about it ("It has been paid in full", "Ya está pagada"), or one with its number, its amount or a word of its own (`namesSpecifically`: never a generic noun like "invoice", "payment", "the due date", nor "your …"); and any affirmation that names a month the item's words don't is another period's bill (`otherMonths`). AutoPay and renewals by themselves keep their rule (the item's paragraph, a word of it, "the due date"). Tests: October and November in both orders, "your previous invoice", "thank you for your payment" a paragraph away, es; the amount named still settles.
2. **AutoPay rejects only the obligation it's about** (`dated-second-look.ts`). The trigger rejected the whole mail when any sentence said AutoPay or a renewal by itself, before the vetoes: "AutoPay is on. Your card has expired. Please pay invoice 3318 by November 25 at 5 PM" got no look and could be written "nothing asked". A candidate sentence that talks about a bill or a plan is now rejected only when the checks would drop it as that item (`handledItself` on the sentence, with the whole mail as its words), so every veto applies: a failed charge, an expired card, a balance owed, a trial, a request to pay. Tests: those four, AutoPay for another account beside a lease deadline, and a plan's own automatic renewal still never asked. One existing case changed: "AutoPay is on: we'll charge your Visa" a paragraph from the bill said nothing that ties it to that bill (the checks keep that pay too); with "on the due date" it's rejected, as `checkDates` reads it.
3. **A day covers only a sentence about the same thing.** A returned item's day covered every sentence saying that day, and an open item's day did too, so a report due Friday hid "The lease renewal closes Friday at 5 PM". A day now covers a sentence when it names the item apart (`namesSpecifically`), or, in the returned item's own paragraph, names nothing else (`namesOtherThan`: a statement's "Payment due date: November 25" beside the balance it quoted); an open item it was shown only when the sentence names it (the worker passes the open items' words, not only their days). Tests: the report and the lease in one paragraph, an open bill on the lease's day, the statement, a reminder naming the open item; two existing cases now give the item's words (a book club named by its book, the registration by its enrollment window).
4. **A same-day move keeps a changed part of the day** (`checkDates`). "You can send me the budget on Monday afternoon instead" after a Monday-morning request was no move: the comparison read the day and the clock only. A part the mail names that isn't the row's is a change, moved with that part (the store already writes it); a part it doesn't name, or its own again, is none. Test beside revision 4's.
5. **A stake's condition needs this consequence's support** (`dated-stake.ts`). "(or policy ends Dec 1)" stayed on "Pay … by November 25. If you have any questions, call us": any conditional sentence of the mail supported any conditional clause. The fallback for a clause in another language than the mail now needs one sentence that says something happens (a consequence word) on a condition, holding the clause's own numbers (with the sentence after it: "12:01 a.m. on December 1" splits in two); the same-language support is unchanged. Tests: the case in en and es, and a Spanish bracket on Lakeshore's English policy mail still kept.
6. **A relayed ask is what the person asks** (`dated-relay.ts`). A quote anywhere in the relay's block passed if it didn't name the service, so "You can reply directly to this email" or a pull request's description could be an ask. `Relay.asks` now holds what the person asks: the relay's own sentence ("@kofi-a requested your review on …"), or the message's sentences that ask the reader something, never the service's call or footer (`SERVICE_LINE`: reply/view/open/join/unsubscribe/notification settings/"you're receiving", en and es); `relays` takes a quote inside one of them, or holding one whole, with nothing of the service's. Tests: a footer with no service name, the notification settings line, Codeforge's description, the review sentence and Marta's Spanish ask still passing.
7. **Remind me stops at a part's end** (`remindLimit`, web). A morning item offered afternoon presets: an untimed item's limit was its day's end. Each branch now carries the part (the feed row's `item.part`, a promise's task, which now carries `part` from `why.promise.part`, the reader's `partOfDay`, the reader's promise's `promiseDay().part`) and reads `dueCutoff(day, part)`. Tests: the four branches, and `taskOf` carrying a promise's part.
8. **The 14-day check reads the part** (`checkDates`). It dropped what was over at the day's end before the part was read, so a morning request already past noon came in from the scan. The part is now read before the check, which uses its cutoff (timed items as before). Test: Kai's Saturday-morning request scanned at 13:00 (out) and at 11:00 (in).
9. **No second look at a list's post the reader isn't named on** (round 14: Diego's "[tipógrafos-mx] … la reunión mensual de la lista es el jueves 19 a las 20:00", which the first reading had rightly left out). `lookAt` gets the list header (`DatedSource.automated`: List-Id or bulk) and how the reader is addressed, and asks nothing when it has one and the reader isn't in To or Cc. *Decision:* not every list-header mail, as the finding first said: Sam's registrar mail (round 13's lost registration, the look's reason for being) carries a List-Id and a bulk header too, and is sent to Sam by name; it still gets its look. And the look's own words keep the reading's listing rule: "of a group they're in that a person writes to them about, never a gathering a mailing list's post or a newsletter announces to everyone on it", and its leave-out list names "a mailing list's" listing. Tests: the trigger (a list post unnamed: none; the registrar's: asked), the worker through the backend (a list post read once, never looked at; the registrar's looked at), the look's words in `ai/dated.test.ts`.
10. **What a look returns that's already there, or gone, is dropped** (`secondLook`): an item that names, apart (`namesSpecifically`), an item the first reading returned or an open item of its conversation or sender on the same day (Priya's PC 7 flight, read again from its own line); another moment of an event already there (a flight's arrival, Diego's "Salida de Casa Malasaña" beside the stay); a check-out as an event of its own (Priya's Hotel Marlowe, `CHECK_OUT`: "check-out", "checkout", "salida del hotel"); and a day already past its Missed window at the look (Jordan's "Written questions due: November 6", read on the 16th; one still in its window is kept). Tests for each with the panel's mails, fictionalized.

**The eval** (second-look, the look's new words, five readings, both models, real; the cap lifted by the owner mid-run). Two new traps, `sl-10`/`sl-11` (a person's post to a mailing list naming its monthly meeting, es and en; the local gate isn't in the eval, so these test the words alone). On the old words, those two cases gave a wrong event in all 10 readings on each model, 17 of them from the look. On the new: **Luna** recorded 30 of 30 found (20 recovered), fresh 29 of 30, 1 wrong shown; **Haiku** recorded 30 of 30 (20 recovered), fresh 30 of 30, 1 wrong shown. Neither wrong row came from a look: Luna's first reading read the book club's "7:00" as 07:00 once (prompt v16's, as in its own eval), and Haiku's first reading took sl-11's meetup once (v16's listing rule on a list post; the app's gate never looks again at such mail, but the first reading is v16's and unchanged here). Every look on sl-10 and sl-11 (20 of 20) left the meeting out; the recovered book clubs and registrations held. Spend: about $0.28 for the final run (Haiku $0.12, Luna $0.15) on the shared ledger, which then stood at $48.68, and $0.09 for the old words' baseline on the new ledger (`/tmp/terno-ai/spend-panel-2.jsonl`).

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (every package): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean. Unit: all of `packages/ai/src`; the backend's `dated`, `dated-automatic`, `dated-evidence`, `dated-relay`, `dated-second-look`, `today`, `today-history`, `today-links`, `today-schedule`, `followups`, `promise-answers`, `promise-time`, `suspicious-sender`, `hand-to`; `scripts/ai-real/dated-checks.test.ts` (727 passed); the web's `lib` and `state/today` (1,834 passed). No store changed, so no conformance run. Not run: e2e (no flow's screens changed; Remind me's limit is unit-tested), the perf gates (the trigger reads a few more regexes per sentence of a read mail, the look's filter a handful of items), `pnpm test:all`, the panel's replay of the trigger.

**Known limits.** Diego's October declaration came in the other order in round 14: the look's respond was written first and a later mail's pay of the same declaration beside it; the look's filter drops its item only when the other is already there, so the reverse is still two rows (the later mail's first reading saw the respond as an open item and didn't move it: v16's). A paid sentence that names the invoice only by a word the item doesn't have ("Status: paid" two lines below the invoice's own) no longer settles it: kept as a bill, the safe side. The month check reads full month names and the short forms that are no English words ("Oct", "Dec"; never "may" or "mar"). A list's post to which the reader is copied by name still gets a look, held by the look's words.

### Round 14 and where the loop on the feed ends (2026-11-16)

Round 14 (iteration 5, medians of two reviewers): Missed 8.3, Look 8.1, Deadlines 7.7, Plans 7.7, Content 7.6, Needs you 7.1, Coming up 7.1, would use daily 7.1, Acting 6.8. Cells at 8+: 26 of 63 (rounds 11–13: 12, 21, 19), under 7: 11 (25, 13, 13). Iteration 5's repairs did what they were for: Plans 6.8 → 7.7 (one return leg, one clock), Missed 7.9 → 8.3 (a broken promise needed today first in Today), Coming up 6.6 → 7.1 (Ahead, then Coming up). Before reviewers ran, the pre-panel check (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/precheck.py`) caught the second look turning a mailing-list post into an event and adding repeats; both were fixed in the code review's pass and the reading taken again. The round's reading: 120 of 132 expected items visible by default, no trap or phishing shown, nothing settled shown open.

What the reviewers still found, verified against the round's data (not built; the loop stops here as the owner decided):
- Priya's card shown twice in Coming up (Dec 26 and Dec 31 from one "expires 12/26" mail; the second look's repeat filter compares days); her Coming up 5.0 and Needs you 5.5 rest mostly on it and on the next point.
- An ask the reader already answered stays open (Priya's Hannah, a filler conversation: her reply had no follow-ups verdict that settled it).
- Remove's toast with a long sender's mute offer crushes its first line to "R… 2 La…".
- Hand to…'s line drops a timed item's cutoff ("by 1:00 PM").
- The walkthrough opens Change… from a row other than the open one, so the form names an item the reader isn't showing (correct behaviour; reviewers read it as a mismatch).

The owner's bar from here (2026-11-16): a ledger of verified claims (zero verified wrong or missing items per persona) and a second-morning capture for "would use daily"; "order it by what matters to me" belongs to the preferences plans (205–207). The speed budget at the combined bounds (SQLite's longest turn 64–67 ms against 60; the linked scenario's own 120 ms allowance) is the owner's open decision: keep the allowance, or slice the waiting walk (plan 145).

##### Round 14's faults, fixed

The first four faults above, each traced in the round's data (`/tmp/terno-scratch/p204/panel/db/priya/`) and fixed at its cause, with a test that fails without it (checked by putting the file back to its last commit). No prompt word changed, so no eval ran and nothing was spent.

1. **Priya's card twice in Coming up.** The first reading took Dec 31 from "before your current card expires on December 31, 2026"; the trigger then asked about "expires at the end of December (12/26)" (its "12/26" read as Dec 26, not the item's day), the look answered Dec 26 by the card rule, and the repeat filter (`repeats`, `dated-second-look.ts`) kept it because it compared days. Now a look's item that names an existing one apart (`namesSpecifically`) is a repeat on any day when both are of one family (a pay, renew or expire; a respond or ask; an event; a delivery; an alert) and its words, beyond its kind's verbs ("expires", "renew", "update"), name nothing the other's don't; another part of one thing stays ("Submit written questions" beside an RFP's response, Jordan's), and across families only the same day counts, as before (Diego's declaration). *The card rule, one rule:* a card's figures alone ("expires 11/26") are the 26th, the earlier reading (iteration 3's revision 11, kept in v16); a day the mail says in words for that card is that day; either way the figures and the words are one card. So the trigger reads a card's "MM/YY" beside an expiry word as its month (`cardMonths`): a day of that month returned for the same card covers the sentence with the figures, and an item quoting the figures covers a sentence that says a day of that month. Priya's mail gets no look whichever reading the first one took (both tested), and the look's Dec 26 would now be dropped anyway.
2. **Hannah's 409A ask stayed open.** The reply was read for its verdict (the follow-ups 14-day check's mark held until What matters' check had read Hannah's question, as iteration 3 meant), and the model answered "fulfilled", with the evidence "We've used Calder Valuation for our last two. ... Yes, I'd use them again." The evidence check wants their words whole, which no text holds across the ellipsis, so the verdict was refused and the ask stayed. The order, the scan and the filler were not the cause. Now a verdict's and a kept promise's evidence is read as its pieces when it isn't theirs whole (`ownPieces`, `followups.ts`): split at "...", "…" or "[...]", each piece two words or more, all theirs in that order; words with their own ellipsis ("Well... yes") are still read whole first. The one such answer in round 14's readings was this one. *Not built:* when follow-ups' own check read your reply before What matters' check read the request (the user said yes to follow-ups' check first), the request comes too late for that reading, and `remarkReplies` doesn't mark a reply sent before follow-ups was turned on, so that ask stays open until Done. Reading it again needs a verdict-only mark, so a reply the user didn't let follow-ups read never becomes their promises. That's a migration (`ai_sent_mail.source`'s CHECK) and the store contract, so it's the owner's decision, not part of these fixes.
3. **Remove's toast crushed to "R… 2 La…".** The snack's message was `flex-1` from zero and the offer a button that never shrinks or wraps, so a long offer took the whole row. With an offer the message now holds at least 8rem ("Removed" / "Quitado" and the item's words under it), and the offer wraps beside it in balanced lines, two at most (`line-clamp-2`, its whole words in `title`); Undo's countdown pill and the close button are unchanged (`snack-dock.tsx`). Every snack with an offer gets this, Reload's "Reload anyway" too (short, one line). Pictured en and es, light and dark (`WM_MUTE_SHOTS`).
4. **Hand to…'s line lost "1:00 PM".** `today.handoff` returned the item's moment but not whether it was a clock time or a part of the day. It now returns `timed` and `partOfDay` beside `dueAt` (the contract and `TodayService.handoff`), and `handOffLine` says them: "by today 1:00 PM", "by Wed 1:00 PM", "by tomorrow morning", "by this afternoon", "by Wed morning" (es "para hoy 13:00", "para mañana por la mañana", "para el mié por la mañana"), the clock in the reader's zone as the Also line says it.

**Tests.** `dated-second-look.test.ts`: Priya's mail with either first reading and an open item of the card shown (no look), nothing returned (both asked), `cardMonths` (en, es, never a party's "12/26" or "12/26/2026"); the domain's expiry beside its renewal and an ask given a day dropped as repeats, Jordan's written questions kept. `promise-answers.test.ts`: the panel's answer settles; "…" and "[...]"; out of order, a piece they didn't write, a one-word piece refused; their own ellipsis read whole; a kept promise's elided evidence. `hand-to.test.ts` (web): the times and parts, en and es; `hand-to.test.ts` (backend): `timed` and `partOfDay` through the RPC. E2E (port 5611): `what-matters-mute.spec.ts`'s new test, en and es (the message 8rem or more and its first line whole, the item's words, the offer whole, Undo, axe in light and dark, z undoes; on the old layout the message was 1.7 px wide), and `hand-to.spec.ts`'s lines now with the item's time.

**Ran.** `pnpm typecheck` (every package): clean. `oxlint --deny-warnings` on every changed file: clean. Unit: the backend's `dated-second-look`, `dated`, `dated-evidence`, `promise-answers`, `followups`, `today-links`, `hand-to`; the web's `lib/hand-to`, `lib/today-task`; `packages/ui`'s `source-scan` (all pass). E2E: `what-matters-mute.spec.ts`, `today.spec.ts`, `hand-to.spec.ts` (all pass; load 1.4 to 2). Not run: the perf gates (the look's filter compares a handful of items; `cardMonths` reads a sentence only beside an expiry word), `pnpm test:all` (no store, schema or migration changed), `pnpm ui:rate`, a Codex code review.
